- **The widget** (`findWidget`) is an overlay at the top right of the editor, 419 px wide (narrower in a narrow editor), 4 px below the top and left of the vertical scrollbar: a chevron that shows and hides the replace row (disabled on a read-only document), the find input with the match case, whole word and regular expression toggles inside it (red border when the search string finds nothing), the match count (`2 of 5`, `?` while the position is unknown, `19999+` at the cap, `No results`), previous and next (disabled without a search string or when the search cannot go that way: `State.CanNavigateBack/Forward`), the find in selection toggle (on while the search is limited) and close; the replace row has the replace input with the preserve case toggle inside it and the replace one and replace all buttons (disabled without a search string). It is not inside the editor's `Focus`, so the focus of its inputs is not the editor's; the buttons and toggles do not take the focus (the keys work from the inputs; step 6.5 adds their focus). The input of a find is one line (VS Code's grows to several: a multiline search string needs `findWithSelection` or an argument and is shown on one line). Deviation: `editor.find.addExtraSpaceOnTop` is not implemented (it needs view zones to push the first line down); the widget covers the first lines until the user scrolls, and the setting is not registered.
- **Highlights**: while the widget is open the overlay under the text draws the decorations of the find owner after the selections and the guides: `findScope` and `rangeHighlight` (the lines of the current match) across the whole row with `editor.findRangeHighlightBackground` and its border, `findMatch` with `editor.findMatchHighlightBackground` and its border, `currentFindMatch` with `editor.findMatchBackground` and its border (a match in a folded line is not drawn). The **overview ruler** is a layer over the vertical scrollbar's track (the width of the scrollbar, under its slider): a mark of at least 2 px for every match (middle third, `editorOverviewRuler.findMatchForeground`) and for the lines of the current match (all of the width, `editorOverviewRuler.rangeHighlightForeground`), at the height the line has in the whole content scaled to the height of the editor; marks that overlap are drawn once. Seeded colors: the find, widget, input and toggle colors of VS Code (`editorWidget.*`, `input.*`, `inputOption.*`, `inputValidation.errorBorder`, `toolbar.hoverBackground`, `widget.border`).
- **`kit.InputBox` and `kit.Toggle`** (§10.2) are first versions: the input box is Glutter's `EditableText` in a 25 px box with the input colors, a border that shows the focus or an error, a placeholder and trailing widgets; the toggle is VS Code's 20 px icon toggle (border and tint while on, hover background). Step 6.5 adds history, validation messages, several lines, focus and tooltips. The icon button was finished by step 6.1 (§10.2: 22 px, focus, tooltip, keyboard).

### 9.9 Snippets (`core/snippet`)

Parser for VS Code's snippet grammar (`$1`, `${1:default}`, `${1|a,b|}`, the variables, `${1/regex/format/flags}` transforms, `$0`). Variables are resolved synchronously from values the UI gathers first (the clipboard text is read before inserting). A snippet session tracks tab stops as decorations, mirrors edits to equal tab stops, and ends when the cursor leaves the snippet or reaches `$0`. Snippet files: `~/.config/emetos/snippets/<language>.json` and `*.code-snippets` (global, with `scope`; §9.9.2). Tab priority follows VS Code's when-clauses (`inSnippetMode`, `hasNextTabstop`, `hasSnippetCompletions`, Emmet's).

#### 9.9.1 The parser and the variables (`core/snippet`)

- **Parser** (`Parser`, a port of VS Code's `SnippetParser`): the scanner cuts the text into tokens (`$ : , { } \ / | + - ?`, numbers, names, other text) and the parser builds a tree of markers: `Text`, `Placeholder` (`$1`, `${1}`, `${1:default}`, `${1|a,b|}` for indexes above 0, `${1/regex/format/flags}`), `Variable` (`$name`, `${name}`, `${name:default}`, `${name/regex/format/flags}`), `Choice`, `Transform` and `FormatString` (`$1`, `${1}`, `${1:/upcase}`, `${1:+if}`, `${1:-else}`, `${1:?if:else}`, `${1:else}`), under a `TextmateSnippet`. `\$`, `\}` and `\\` are text; any other backslash is a backslash. Text that is not valid syntax (a missing `}`, an unbalanced regular expression, invalid flags) stays text: a snippet always parses. Placeholders of an index that has no default take the default of the first one that has; nested and cyclic references end. `Parse` can add the final tab stop `$0` (`InsertFinalTabstop`, `EnforceFinalTabstop`). Lengths and offsets of markers are bytes (§6.2). `ToTextmateString` writes a tree back as snippet text (as in VS Code, a placeholder with no default is written `$1`, so a digit after it reads on into the number).
- **Transforms** use `core/regex` in ECMAScript syntax with the flags `d g i m s u v y` (each once); a search is limited to one second and ends the replacing when it runs over. The shorthands are `upcase`, `downcase`, `capitalize`, `pascalcase`, `camelcase`, `kebabcase` and `snakecase` (with Unicode letters); an unknown one leaves the value. When nothing matched and a format string has an else branch, the else branches are the result.
- **Variables** are resolved synchronously by `VariableResolver`s from plain values the caller gathers before inserting: `SelectionResolver` (`SELECTION`, `TM_SELECTED_TEXT` with the lines of a selection of several lines re-indented as the variable is, `TM_CURRENT_LINE`, `TM_CURRENT_WORD`, `TM_LINE_INDEX`, `TM_LINE_NUMBER`, `CURSOR_INDEX`, `CURSOR_NUMBER`; `SelectionValuesOf` gathers them from a document; a cursor with no selection takes the text it typed over last), `FileResolver` (`TM_FILENAME`, `TM_FILENAME_BASE`, `TM_DIRECTORY`, `TM_DIRECTORY_BASE`, `TM_FILEPATH`, `RELATIVE_FILEPATH`), `ClipboardResolver` (`CLIPBOARD`, one line for each cursor when the lines are as many as the cursors), `CommentResolver` (`LINE_COMMENT`, `BLOCK_COMMENT_START`, `BLOCK_COMMENT_END`), `TimeResolver` (the `CURRENT_*` variables, all from one moment; the day and month names are English), `WorkspaceResolver` (`WORKSPACE_NAME`, `WORKSPACE_FOLDER`; empty with no folder; the window gives the name and path of its folder, §10.5) and `RandomResolver` (`RANDOM`, `RANDOM_HEX`, `UUID`), joined by `CompositeResolver`. A variable no resolver knows keeps its default text; one with a transform is always resolved (the transform sees an empty value).

#### 9.9.2 Sessions, snippet files and the Tab key (`core/snippet`, `gui/editor`)

- **Session** (`Session`, `Controller`; ports of VS Code's `SnippetSession` and `SnippetController2`): inserting a snippet resolves its variables (`Env`, gathered by the editor: the word separators, the path of the file and its path relative to the folder that is open, the comment tokens of the language, the folder, `editor.multiCursorPaste`, the clock and the zone, the clipboard text), adjusts its white space (the lines take the indentation of the line it goes into, tabs and spaces follow the document), replaces what the user typed to find it (`OverwriteBefore` and `OverwriteAfter`, bytes) and makes one edit for all the cursors, as one undo step. Every tab stop is a decoration of the editor's snippet owner (`snippet.Editor.Owner`; `DecorationOwner`, 6, when it names none; `snippetPlaceholder`; the final tab stop `finishSnippetPlaceholder`; the active tab stop grows when text is typed at its edges, the others do not). The cursors go to the first tab stop: one cursor for each placeholder of the same index (so the mirrors are typed together), and the text of a placeholder is selected. Tab and Shift+Tab move between the tab stops; leaving a placeholder applies the transforms of its mirrors; `$0` is the final stop, and a snippet without one ends where it was inserted. A snippet inserted while another is being filled in is nested into it (the tab stops are merged in order, the nested ones first). With several cursors each gets its own copy, and `editor.action.insertSnippet` works on all of them. The mode ends when the final tab stop is reached, when the cursors leave the tab stops (a selection that is not inside a placeholder), when the document is set again, when an undo comes back to the text before the snippet, or on Escape (`leaveSnippet`, which also collapses the cursors to the primary one); Enter does not end it.
- **Choices**: a tab stop with options (`${1|a,b|}`) shows them in a `SnippetPicker`, a short list below the caret that the editor widget shows as a `kit.ListOverlay` (above the caret when it does not fit; it takes the focus while it is open and gives it back). Enter on an option replaces the text of the tab stop by it and puts the cursor after it; Escape closes the list and leaves the first option. Step 6.17 moves the lists to the quick input.
- **Context keys** (set in the editor's scope): `inSnippetMode`, `hasNextTabstop`, `hasPrevTabstop`, `inSnippetChoice` and `hasSnippetCompletions` (the Tab key would insert a snippet).
- **Commands and keys** (ids as VS Code's): `jumpToNextSnippetPlaceholder` (Tab; in snippet mode with a next tab stop), `jumpToPrevSnippetPlaceholder` (Shift+Tab; with a previous one), `leaveSnippet` (Escape and Shift+Escape in snippet mode), `acceptSnippet` (goes through the tab stops to the final one; no key), `insertSnippet` (Tab; with `hasSnippetCompletions`, not in snippet mode: the Tab key of `editor.tabCompletion`) and `editor.action.insertSnippet` ("Insert Snippet"; writable documents; the argument is `{"snippet": "<text>"}`, or `{"name": "<name>", "langId": "<language>"}`, or nothing, and the user then picks from a filterable list of the snippets of the language at the cursor, those without a prefix too). The three tab stop commands weigh `WeightEditorContrib + 30`, so they win over `tab`, `outdent` and `insertSnippet`, which win over `tab` in turn; Escape in snippet mode wins over `removeSecondaryCursors` and `cancelSelection`.
- **Snippet files** (`File`, `Service`; ports of `snippetsFile.ts` and `snippetsService.ts`): the user's folder is `<config>/snippets` (§15). `<language>.json` is for that language; `*.code-snippets` is for any language, and each snippet names the languages in `scope` (a comma separated list; a scope `source.js` also matches the language `source`). The text is JSON with comments and trailing commas; a snippet is an object with a non-empty `body` (a string or an array of lines joined by `\n`), `prefix` (a string or an array: one snippet for each; none gives no snippet; missing is no prefix), `description`, `isFileTemplate`, `include` and `exclude` (globs, matched ignoring case against the file name, or the whole path when the glob has a `/`; exclude wins, include limits). In a `.code-snippets` file an object without a `body` is a group of snippets. A name that is not a variable (`${foo}`) becomes a placeholder (`${1:foo}`; the same name gets the same index), as in VS Code. `Service.Reload(ctx)` reads the folder again (the watcher of step 6.13 calls it; a file that cannot be read is left out and the first failure is returned); `Service.Snippets(Query)` gives the snippets of a language and a file, sorted by source and name. Not ported: snippets of extensions and of the workspace (`.vscode/*.code-snippets`; step 6.13), sorting by recent use, the toggle that disables snippets (`SetDisabled` takes identifiers, nothing calls it yet), the file template commands ("Fill File with Snippet") and the editor for snippet files.
- **Tab completion** (`editor.tabCompletion`: `off` (default), `onlySnippets`, `on`): with `onlySnippets` the editor looks, whenever the cursor moves, for the snippets of the language whose prefix the text before the cursor ends with (the word back to the last white space, at most 100 characters), or, for a selection on one line of at most 100 characters, that equal the selection. One match is inserted by Tab (the prefix is replaced); several are offered in the list. `on` needs the suggestions of area 3 and does nothing until then.
- **Colors**: `editor.snippetTabstopHighlightBackground` (`#7C7C7C4D` dark and high contrast dark, `#0A326433` light), `editor.snippetTabstopHighlightBorder` (none), `editor.snippetFinalTabstopHighlightBackground` (none) and `editor.snippetFinalTabstopHighlightBorder` (`#525252`, `#0A326480` light, `#292929` high contrast light). The tab stops are drawn under the text, with the occurrences; one with no text (the final tab stop is usually empty) is not drawn, the cursor is there.
- Deviations: the clipboard is read first when the snippet uses `CLIPBOARD` (the insertion waits for the desktop and is dropped if the document or the cursors changed meanwhile, as VS Code does); the text a cursor typed over (`TM_SELECTED_TEXT` for an empty selection) is not tracked yet (`Overtyped` is nil); the languages at the cursor are not told apart inside an embedded language (the language of the document is used); the Tab key does not wait for tokenization.

### 9.10 Emmet (`core/emmet`)

Abbreviation parser and HTML output; CSS abbreviations from Emmet's snippets (MIT); abbreviation extraction before the cursor; `emmet.includeLanguages`, `emmet.syntaxProfiles`, `emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab` (off by default, as in VS Code).

#### 9.10.1 Abbreviations and markup output (`core/emmet`)

Step 5.10 ports Emmet's markup abbreviations (`emmetio/emmet`, MIT; the code says "Ported from Emmet: <file>"). The package is plain Go with no UI; offsets are byte offsets (every character with a meaning in an abbreviation is ASCII, the other bytes are text; the no-break space is white space and ä, ö, ü and their capitals are letters of element names, as in Emmet). Step 5.11 added the CSS part, the extraction of the abbreviation before the cursor and the expansion at the cursor (`Config.Type`, the `Stylesheet*` options, `css.json`, `ExtractAbbreviation`, `ExpandAt`: §9.10.2).

- **Pipeline** (the layers of Emmet): `Tokenize` (the scanner and tokenizer) -> `ParseTokens` (elements and groups, `Statement`) -> `Convert` (unrolls groups and repeaters into `Node` trees; `ParseAbbreviation` does the three) -> `Parse` (adds the markup steps: snippets, then the transformations) -> `Stringify` (a formatter for the syntax); `Expand(abbr, cfg)` is `Parse` and `Stringify`. `Resolve` does the markup steps for a tree that is parsed already.
- **Grammar**: elements with names of letters, digits, `_ - : !` and umlauts; `>` child, `+` sibling, `^` climb up (any number), `( )` groups, `*N` repeaters and `*` (implicit: one copy for each non-blank line of the wrapped text), `#id`, `.class` (the shorthands may be repeated: `..foo` is a second kind of class, see JSX), `[attr attr=value attr="quoted" attr='quoted' attr={expression} attr. !implied "default value"]`, `{text}` (nested braces are allowed), `$` numbering (`$$$` padded, `$@-` reversed, `$@3` base, `$@^` numbering of the parent repeater), `$#` the text of the implicit repeater, `${1:placeholder}` fields, `${name}` variables, `/` self-closing, `\` escapes, a `/` between two digits in a class name (`w1/2`). Implicit tags: an element with attributes and no name is a `li` in `ul`/`ol`, `td` in `tr`, `span` in an inline element, `div` otherwise (the parent is the closest element, or `Config.Context`). Text given with the abbreviation (`Config.Text`: a string, or a list of lines) goes into the implicitly repeated elements, or into the deepest element; a link that wraps a URL or an e-mail address takes its `href` (`Options.MarkupHref`).
- **Snippets**: `assets/html.json` (155 entries), `xsl.json` (37) and `pug.json` (1), and `variables.json` (`lang`, `locale`, `charset`, `indentation`, `newline`) are copied by `scripts/update-assets.sh` and embedded; a snippet is an abbreviation (`a:blank`, `fset`, `!`, ...), resolved recursively (a snippet that refers to itself, like `img`, is left as it is), with the attributes of the node added to it; a snippet that does not parse is left out (`Config.Warn` is told). `Config.Snippets` and `Config.Variables` start from these and the user adds over them (`SetSnippets`, `SetVariables`; the setting `emmet.variables` is read by `VariablesFromSettings`).
- **Transformations**: merging of attributes (equal names; classes are joined), `lorem[lang][N[-M]]` (Latin, `ru`, `sp`; random words, `Config.Rand` to make them repeatable; at most `MaxLoremWords` = 10000 words), the label preprocessor (`label>input` has no `for`/`id`), XSL (`select` of `xsl:variable` and `xsl:with-param` with content), BEM (`Options.BEMEnabled`: `-element`, `_modifier`, block from the ancestors or `Config.Context`).
- **Output** (`Stringify` by `Config.Syntax`): `html` (and `xml`, `xsl`, `jsx`, `vue`, `svelte` and any other name) with Emmet's formatting rules (block and inline elements, `inlineBreak`, `formatSkip`, `formatForce`, text with line breaks, snippets that wrap their children in a field, comments `[#ID][.CLASS]` templates), `haml`, `pug` and `slim` (the indent based formats). The editor's indentation and line break go in `Config.SetOutput(indent, newline, baseIndent)` (`Options.Indent`, `Options.Newline`, `Options.BaseIndent`; `${indentation}` and `${newline}` take the same values); the fields of the output (tab stops) are written by `Options.Field` (`SnippetField` writes `${1:placeholder}`; the default writes the placeholder) and the text by `Options.Text` (to escape it). Positions given to the hooks are bytes.
- **Syntax defaults** (`NewConfig(syntax)`): `xml` and `xsl` close with `/>`, `xhtml` with ` />`; `xsl` and `pug` add their snippets; `jsx` has `className`, `htmlFor`, `styleName` for `..foo` and `styles.foo` values, and expressions in braces; `vue` writes `..foo` as `:class="foo"`; `svelte` has the JSX mode.
- **Profiles** (`emmet.syntaxProfiles`; `Config.ApplyProfiles(profiles)` picks the profile named by the syntax, `ApplyProfile` applies one): the keys are Emmet 2's options by short name (`tagCase`, `attributeCase`, `attributeQuotes`, `format`, `formatLeafNode`, `formatSkip`, `formatForce`, `inlineBreak`, `compactBooleanAttributes`, `booleanAttributes`, `reverseAttributes`, `selfClosingStyle`, `inlineElements`) and the names of Emmet 1's profiles that VS Code's documentation still shows (`tag_case`, `attr_case`, `attr_quotes`, `tag_nl` (true, false or "decide" = true), `indent` (false is no indentation), `inline_break`, `self_closing_tag` (true `<br/>`, false `<br>`, "xhtml" `<br />`), `compact_bool`, `filters` ("bem", "c")). Unknown keys are ignored; a known key with a wrong value is the error `ProfileValueInvalid` and changes nothing. As in VS Code, JSX is closed like XHTML and XML and XSL like XML unless the profile sets the style. VS Code's helper library that maps the profile to Emmet's options is not in `reference/`; the table above is Emetos's own, built from the names Emmet 1 and 2 use.
- **Limits** (Emmet has none unless `maxRepeat` is given): the elements an abbreviation makes are limited to `Config.MaxRepeat` in all (default `DefaultMaxRepeat` = 1000, at most `HardMaxRepeat` = 10000; counts in the text are read up to 2^30), the nesting of groups and children to 1000 (`NestingTooDeep`), the width of `$$$` numbers to 64.
- **Errors** (`github.com/rm4n0s/errors`; the message ends with the 1-based position as Emmet's, the metadata has `pos` and the abbreviation): `UnexpectedCharacter`, `FieldUnclosed`, `PlaceholderUnclosed` (tokenizer), `UnexpectedToken`, `UnexpectedAttributeToken`, `UnclosedQuote`, `NestingTooDeep` (parser), `ProfileInvalid`, `ProfileValueInvalid`, `VariablesInvalid`, `VariableValueInvalid` (configuration), and `EmmetAssetMissing`, `EmmetAssetInvalid`, `LoremAssetMissing`, `LoremAssetInvalid` (the embedded files; a test checks them).
- **Settings registered**: `emmet.variables` and `emmet.syntaxProfiles` (objects, default `{}`, VS Code's descriptions); step 5.11 added `emmet.includeLanguages`, `emmet.excludeLanguages` and `emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab` (§9.10.2).
- **Deviations from Emmet**: offsets and the positions given to the output hooks are bytes (Emmet's are UTF-16 units); the limits above; `Config` is a struct changed in place instead of a merged partial (`NewConfig` then assign); the snippets are parsed every time they are used (no cache); a list of lines of text asked beyond its end is empty (Emmet's `undefined`); `Convert` of an empty abbreviation with text gives no nodes (Emmet throws); two bugs of Emmet's token writer are fixed: a group bracket in the name of an attribute was written `}` and a field without a placeholder in a name lost its closing brace; `compactBoolean` is also named so (Emmet 2's long name is `compactBooleanAttributes`); no `cache`, no `emmet.preferences` as a registered setting (the core reads its stylesheet keys, §9.10.2), no `emmet.extensionsPath` (custom snippet files) and no `emmet.showExpandedAbbreviation`/`emmet.showAbbreviationSuggestions` (the completion of abbreviations is out of scope with the rest of the suggestions of area 3).
- **Tests**: ports of Emmet's `test/{markup,expand,format,output,snippets,lorem}.ts` and `packages/abbreviation/test/{tokenizer,parser,convert}.ts` (markup cases only), tables for the profiles, routes of every error tag, `FuzzParse` and a seeded random test.

#### 9.10.2 CSS abbreviations, extraction and expansion at the cursor (`core/emmet`)

Step 5.11 adds to the same package (plain Go, byte offsets, nothing of Glutter or `gui`), the stylesheet half of Emmet, the extraction of an abbreviation from a line, and the part of VS Code's Emmet extension (`extensions/emmet/src/abbreviationActions.ts`, `util.ts`) that does not need the document or the editor. The code says "Ported from Emmet: <file>" or "Ported from VS Code: <file>".

- **Stylesheet pipeline** (Emmet's `css-abbreviation` package and `src/stylesheet`): `TokenizeCSS(abbr, isValue)` -> `ParseCSSTokens` (`ParseCSS` does both; `CSSProperty{Name, Value []*CSSValue, Important}`; a function call is a `CSSToken` of type `CSSFunction`) -> `ParseStylesheet(abbr, cfg)` (fuzzy matching of property names, keywords and numbers) -> `StringifyStylesheet(props, cfg)`; `ExpandStylesheet` is the three, and `Expand` calls it when `Config.Type` is `TypeStylesheet`. `css.json` (249 entries, 253 names; copied by `scripts/update-assets.sh`) holds the snippets: a *property* snippet (`"bd": "border:${1:1px} ${2:solid} ${3:#000}"`, values separated by `|`) or a *raw* one (`@m`, `@kf`). The properties are nested (`background-position-x` into `background-position` into `background`) so that the keywords of the specific ones can be used with the shorthand. An abbreviation is matched with `scoreMatch` (the first letters must be equal; characters closer to the start of the name and after a `-` score more); `Options.StylesheetFuzzySearchMinScore` is the lowest score accepted. The rest as Emmet: `p10` is `padding: 10px`, `p.4` `padding: 0.4em`, `z10` `z-index: 10` (`StylesheetUnitless`), `10p` is `10%` and `10r` `10rem` (`StylesheetUnitAliases`), `m0-a` `margin: 0 auto` (`StylesheetKeywords`), `c#f.5` `color: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.5)` and `c#fc0` `color: #fc0` (`StylesheetShortHex`), `lg(...)` a `linear-gradient`, `p!` `padding: ${0} !important`, `bxsh` a custom property; the output uses `StylesheetBetween` and `StylesheetAfter` and, with `StylesheetJSON`, is CSS-in-JS (`marginTop: '10px 20px',`). `Config.Context` is the scope as in Emmet: `ScopeSection` (raw snippets only, the properties that matched no snippet are left out), `ScopeProperty`, `ScopeGlobal`, `ScopeValue` or the name of a property (the abbreviation is its value). `StylesheetStrictMatch` leaves out what matches nothing. Raw snippets keep their tabs (`Options.Indent` is not applied to them): a snippet insertion turns them into the indentation of the document.
- **Configuration**: `Config.Type` (`TypeMarkup`, the default, or `TypeStylesheet`) and the `Options.Stylesheet*` fields (defaults of Emmet: keywords `auto inherit unset none`, unitless `z-index line-height opacity font-weight zoom flex flex-grow flex-shrink`, short hex on, between `": "`, after `";"`, `px`, `em`, aliases `e p x r`, no JSON, fuzzy score 0, not strict). `NewConfig(syntax)` makes a stylesheet configuration for `css scss less sss sass stylus` (`IsStylesheetSyntax`): `sass` has no `;`, `stylus` has no `;` and a space between name and value. `SnippetText` (for `Options.Text`) escapes `\` and `$` of the text for a snippet, as `SnippetField` (for `Options.Field`) writes the tab stops. `Config.ApplyPreferences(prefs)` reads the stylesheet keys of VS Code's `emmet.preferences` object: `css.intUnit`, `css.floatUnit`, `css.unitAliases` (an object, or Emmet 1's `e:em, p:%`), `css.fuzzySearchMinScore` (0 to 1), `css.color.short`, and `<syntax>.propertyEnd` and `<syntax>.valueSeparator` (the syntax of `scss`, `less` and `sss` is `css`); the other keys are ignored, and a wrong value changes nothing and is `PreferenceValueInvalid`. The setting `emmet.preferences` itself is not registered in this step (nothing passes it yet).
- **Extraction** (`ExtractAbbreviation(line, pos, ExtractOptions{LookAhead, Type, Prefix})` -> `Extracted{Abbreviation, Location, Start, End}`; Emmet's `extract`): the abbreviation that ends at `pos` (a byte offset in the line, clamped), found by reading back over letters, digits and `# . * : $ - _ ! @ % ^ + > /`, with `[...]` and `{...}` (attributes and text, markup only) and `(...)` kept balanced and with everything inside brackets accepted, stopping at the end of an HTML tag (`<span>` before the abbreviation) or at a character that does not belong; leading `* + > ^` are dropped. With `LookAhead` it first moves over the `]`, `}`, `)` and one quote that an editor closed after `pos`. With `Prefix` it starts at the nearest prefix before `pos` (`Start` is the place of the prefix). In `TypeStylesheet` only parentheses count. Limit: the search never goes back more than `MaxExtractLength` (4096) bytes, and an abbreviation that is longer is not found (Emmet has none; the scan is linear, but a minified line can be megabytes long). `isAtHTMLTag` and `consumeQuoted` are ports of `is-html.ts` and `quotes.ts`.
- **Languages** (VS Code's `util.ts` and `getSyntaxFromArgs`): `SyntaxFor(languageID, includeLanguages, excluded) (syntax, ok)` gives the Emmet syntax for a language id of the editor: excluded languages give false; `MappingForIncludedLanguages` merges VS Code's built-in mapping (`handlebars` and `php` to `html`) with `emmet.includeLanguages` (entries whose value is not one of `html jade slim haml xml xsl css scss sass less stylus javascriptreact typescriptreact` are dropped); `EmmetMode` maps `javascriptreact`, `typescriptreact` and `jsx-tags` to `jsx`, `jade` to `pug`, `sass-indented` to `sass`, and accepts only `html xml xsl jsx js pug slim haml css sass scss less sss stylus`, so `javascript`, `vue` and `markdown` give false unless `emmet.includeLanguages` maps them. `IncludeLanguagesFromSettings(value)` and `ExcludedLanguagesFromSettings(value)` read the two settings the way VS Code does (entries of the wrong type are ignored). `VS Code's `parentMode` (the language of the grammar a language is embedded in) is not supported.
- **`ExpandAt(req ExpandRequest) (ExpandResult, bool, error)`**: `ExpandRequest{Syntax, TextBefore, Selection, Variables, Profiles, Preferences, Indent, Newline, BaseIndent}`, `ExpandResult{Start, End, Abbreviation, Text, Syntax}`. `Syntax` is the result of `SyntaxFor`. `TextBefore` is the text of the cursor's line from its start to the cursor; `Selection`, when not empty, is the selected text and replaces `TextBefore`. It gives false with no error when there is nothing to expand: no syntax, `TextBefore` empty or ending with a space or a tab (the Tab key then is a Tab), nothing extracted (without a look ahead, `Type` by the syntax), the abbreviation is not valid (`IsAbbreviationValid`), or a stylesheet abbreviation ends with `:` (VS Code issue 1623). For `html`, `<div` before the cursor is `div` and the range takes the `<`. A selection is expanded as a whole: the abbreviation must start at its first byte (the helper's `extractAbbreviationFromText`), and wrapping the selection (`wrapWithAbbreviation`) is not done. Filters `|bem`, `|c` and `|t` (up to three) at the end of the text are removed from the abbreviation, kept in the range, and turn on BEM and comments (the profile's `filters` do the same). `Start` and `End` are byte offsets in `TextBefore` (in `Selection`); `Text` is a snippet: tab stops as `${1:name}`, `${1}` and `${0}`, with `\` and `$` of the text escaped, lines joined by `Newline` (default `"\n"`), indented by `Indent` (default a tab) and, from the second line, by `BaseIndent`. The configuration is made by `ExpandAt`: `NewConfig(Syntax)`, `Variables` (`emmet.variables`; Emmet's `indentation` and `newline` follow the output options), `Profiles` (`emmet.syntaxProfiles`, markup only), then for stylesheets the VS Code default `css.fuzzySearchMinScore` of 0.3 (`DefaultFuzzySearchMinScore`; Emmet's own is 0) and `Preferences`. An error is the tag of the parser or of the configuration (`NestingTooDeep`, `CSSUnexpectedToken`, `ProfileValueInvalid`, `PreferenceValueInvalid`, ...) with the route from `ExpandAt`. `IsAbbreviationValid(syntax, abbr)` is the check of VS Code's helper: a stylesheet abbreviation starts with a letter, `-`, `!`, `@` or `#`, with a `#` first it is a hex color of one to six digits, and before a `#` it is not the name of an HTML tag (`div#main` is a selector); a markup abbreviation starts with a letter, `!`, `(`, `[`, `#`, `.` or `{`, ends with a letter, a digit or one of `) ] } # . * $ ! /`, and `!` alone (any number of them) is valid when it starts with `!`.
- **The contract of the user interface** (`editor.emmet.action.expandAbbreviation`, "Emmet: Expand Abbreviation", writable documents; Tab with `editorTextFocus && tabDoesNotMoveFocus && config.emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab`; weight `EditorContrib`, so the tab stop commands of the snippet session win over it, §9.9.2): for the language id at the cursor (the document's language, or the embedded one when the editor knows it), `syntax, ok := SyntaxFor(id, IncludeLanguagesFromSettings(emmet.includeLanguages), ExcludedLanguagesFromSettings(emmet.excludeLanguages))`; when `!ok` the command does what Tab does (`tab`) if `emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab` is on, and nothing otherwise. With `emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab` on and any non-empty selection, the key is a plain Tab. Each cursor is expanded on its own, in reverse order of position: `TextBefore` is its line up to the cursor, `Selection` its text when it is not empty, `Variables` from `VariablesFromSettings(emmet.variables)`, `Profiles` the object `emmet.syntaxProfiles`, and `Indent`, `Newline` and `BaseIndent` empty (the snippet insertion indents the lines and uses the document's line break). The text `res.Text` replaces the bytes `res.Start..res.End` of the line (the selection when there is one) as a snippet through the session of §9.9.2 (`OverwriteBefore` is `End - Start`); when every cursor gave the same text, one snippet is inserted at all of them, else each gets its own. Nothing was expanded (all false) is a plain Tab when `emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab` is on; an error is shown as the notification "Failed to expand abbreviation" (and is logged) and the key is a plain Tab too.
- **Settings registered** (VS Code's names, defaults, descriptions and scopes): `emmet.includeLanguages` (object, `{}`), `emmet.excludeLanguages` (array of strings, `["markdown"]`) and `emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab` (boolean, `false`; language-overridable). `emmet.showExpandedAbbreviation`, `emmet.showAbbreviationSuggestions` and the other settings of the completion provider are not registered (suggestions are area 3).
- **Deviations**: (1) The helper library of VS Code, `@vscode/emmet-helper`, is not in `reference/`: `IsAbbreviationValid`, the filters and the use of the preferences are written from its documented behavior, not ported from its source, and may differ in the details (the markup rule that a number or numbers with dots only (`.5`, `1.5`) is not an abbreviation is Emetos's own). (2) The cursor is assumed to be where an abbreviation may be: for the languages of markup it is in markup, for stylesheets in a rule. VS Code's checks that need a parse of the document are not done: `isValidLocationForEmmetAbbreviation` (comments of a stylesheet, the selector of a rule and the value of a property, where only a color or `!` expand; the text of a tag, `<script>` types), the CSS inside `<style>` and `style=""` of an HTML document (unless the editor passes the id of the embedded language), `parsePartialStylesheet`, `getHtmlFlatNode`, and the check of symbols of JavaScript. (3) `wrapWithAbbreviation`, `emmet.extensionsPath`, `emmet.optimizeStylesheetParsing`, `emmet.useInlineCompletions` and the other Emmet commands (balance, match tag, ...) are not implemented. (4) A snippet of a stylesheet that does not parse is left out and `Config.Warn` is told (Emmet throws); `ScopeValue` and the property scopes are there for the editor to use when it can tell where the cursor is. (5) Property names, keywords and the abbreviation are ASCII: the order of the keywords of a property, the lowercase of the match and the `toFixed` of numbers follow JavaScript's (`jsToFixed`), but characters over 127 are not letters. (6) Errors, with the tags `CSSUnexpectedCharacter`, `CSSUnexpectedBracket`, `CSSFieldUnclosed`, `CSSPlaceholderUnclosed` (tokenizer), `CSSUnexpectedToken`, `CSSNestingTooDeep` (parser; function calls nested over 1000 deep), `CSSSnippetValueEmpty` (a snippet) and `PreferenceValueInvalid`; the messages end with the 1-based place for the tokenizer and the 0-based place for the parser, as Emmet's.
- **Tests**: ports of Emmet's `test/stylesheet.ts` (all 117 assertions), `test/extract-abbreviation.ts` (all 58) and `packages/css-abbreviation/test/{tokenizer,parser}.ts` (all 88), tables for `SyntaxFor`, `IsAbbreviationValid`, `ExpandAt` (markup and stylesheet, base indentation, a prefix that is not an abbreviation, the colon, the filters, multi-byte text), the preferences, routes of every error tag, `FuzzParseCSS`, `FuzzExtract` and a seeded random test.

- **In the editor** (`gui/editor/emmet.go`): `editor.emmet.action.expandAbbreviation` follows the contract above with the settings in `Options.Emmet` (the editor reads `emmet.*` when the settings change); the context key `config.emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab` is set in the editor's scope. The expansions of all the cursors go in through `snippet.Controller.Apply` (§9.9.2), so the tab stops work and the insertion is one undo step. The key is `Tab` with `editorTextFocus && !editorReadonly && !editorTabMovesFocus && !editorHasSelection && !inSnippetMode && config.emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab`, at `WeightEditorContrib` and registered before the snippet keys, so the order for Tab is: a tab stop of a snippet that is being filled in, then a snippet whose prefix is before the cursor (`insertSnippet`), then Emmet, then `tab`. A selection of several lines is left alone (Emmet's wrap command is not implemented). An error of the expansion (`VariablesInvalid` and the tags of §9.10.1) makes the key a plain Tab and is returned as the error of the command, which the shell shows as a notification and logs.

### 9.11 Diff and merge

- `core/diff`: VS Code's default ("advanced") lines diff: dynamic programming for small inputs and Myers O(ND) for large ones, VS Code's heuristics (shifting to line and blank-line boundaries), refinement to character changes, ignore trim whitespace, and a time limit (`diffEditor.maxComputationTime`, 5 s, 0 for none) that covers the line and the character stage together: when it passes in the line stage the diff is one change over both texts, when it passes in the character stage the blocks not refined yet are each one character change over the whole block (VS Code's behavior, see "Character diff as built"). Moved-code detection is out of scope.
- **Line diff as built (step 9.1; `core/diff`).** `ComputeLines(ctx, original, modified []string, Options) (LinesDiff, error)` is VS Code's `DefaultLinesDiffComputer.computeDiff` up to, and excluding, `refineDiff` (the character stage, `Compute`, step 9.2); texts are lines without their line breaks (a text that ends with a line break has an empty last line). `Options` has `IgnoreTrimWhitespace`, `MaxComputationTime` (zero: no limit; negative: error `DiffTimeoutInvalid`) and `Now` (the clock of the limit, `time.Now` when nil). `LinesDiff` has `Changes []LineRangeMapping` and `HitTimeout`; a `LineRangeMapping` is `Original`/`Modified` `LineRange` (1-based, end exclusive, as VS Code's `LineRange`) and `Inner []RangeMapping` (`Range` of 1-based line and byte column positions), which `Compute` (step 9.2) fills; there is no `Moves` (moved-code detection is not built, §2). `Apply(original, modified, changes)` rebuilds the modified lines (used by the tests and, later, by the accept operations of the merge model) and `Validate(changes, nOriginal, nModified)` checks the invariants VS Code asserts (ranges inside the texts, sorted, not touching, the unchanged lines before, between and after the changes as many in both texts; error `DiffInvalid`).
  - *Algorithm, as in VS Code.* Both texts equal with at most one line, or either text being the single empty line, are answered at once (no changes, or one change over both texts that carries its one character change in `Inner`). Lines are numbered by their text trimmed as JavaScript's `trim()` does (not `unicode.IsSpace`: U+FEFF is white space, U+0085 is not). With fewer than 1700 lines in both texts together the lines are aligned by dynamic programming (O(MN), consecutive diagonals preferred, equal lines scored 0.1 when empty and `1 + ln(1 + length)` otherwise, lines equal after trimming but not equal 0.99), else by Myers' O(ND) algorithm. Then `optimizeSequenceDiffs` (join by shifting, twice, then shift insertions and deletions, at most 100 lines, to the position with the best `getBoundaryScore`: `1000 - (indentation before + indentation after)`, spaces and tabs, leftmost on a tie, moving only over lines that are equal as written) and `removeVeryShortMatchingLinesBetweenDiffs` (two changes are joined when at most 4 characters other than white space lie between them and one of them is more than 5 lines in total, repeated up to 11 times). The line numbers in the result come straight from these alignments.
  - *Ignore trim whitespace.* Lines are always compared trimmed. Without `IgnoreTrimWhitespace`, a line that is equal to its counterpart after trimming but not as written is a change of its own (VS Code's `scanForWhitespaceChanges`; `Compute` refines it to character changes), and changes that touch are joined; with it, such lines are unchanged.
  - *Time limit and cancellation.* `MaxComputationTime` is checked at every cell of the dynamic programming and every round of Myers' algorithm (VS Code's `DateTimeout`: expired for good once over); when it is over the result is the trivial diff, one change over both texts, with `HitTimeout` set. A done context stops the work (looked at every 1024 steps) with a `DiffCancelled` error that wraps the context's error.
  - *Verified against VS Code's fixtures* (step 9.1, line ranges of `ComputeLines`; `TestVSCodeFixtureLineRanges`): the line ranges equal the expected ones in 53 of 58 fixtures; the other five (`class-replacement`, `noisy-move1`, `shifting-twice`, `ts-confusing-2`, `ts-example1`) differ exactly where the character refinement splits a block at lines it finds unchanged or trims a line from its end; step 9.2 matches them all (below).
- **Character diff as built (step 9.2; `core/diff`).** `Compute(ctx, original, modified []string, Options) (LinesDiff, error)` is VS Code's whole `DefaultLinesDiffComputer.computeDiff` without the moved-code detection (§2): the line stage of `ComputeLines` and then `refineDiff` of every changed block, joined into `LineRangeMapping` values whose `Inner` holds the character changes (`RangeMapping` of `Range` of `Position`; at least one per change, sorted, not overlapping). A text has at least one line, so `Compute` takes no lines for one empty line (`ComputeLines` keeps its 9.1 behavior). `Options` gains `ExtendToSubwords` (VS Code's `extendToSubwords`, false for the diff editor: the fixtures are computed without it).
  - *Columns.* `Position.Column` is a 1-based **byte** column of the line (D6, as the editor core's `buffer`), where VS Code counts UTF-16 code units; the diff editor (step 9.5) maps them to editor columns directly. The characters of a diff are code points (a byte that is not valid UTF-8 is a character of its own, different from every other), so a character change never splits a character; VS Code's limits in code units (500 characters for dynamic programming, 20 for the text between two changes, 100 for a large change, the weights of `removeVeryShortMatchingTextBetweenLongDiffs`) count code points here, which differs only for characters above U+FFFF. The fixture test converts the byte columns to UTF-16 columns of the fixture text before it compares them with VS Code's expectations.
  - *Refinement of a block* (`refineDiff`). The block's lines are taken whole (`toRangeMapping2`: from the start of its first line to the start of the line after it, or the end of the last line when it is the last of the text; for an empty block the line break before it) as a `LinesSliceCharSequence`: the characters, `\n` between the lines, and, without `IgnoreTrimWhitespace` (VS Code's `considerWhitespaceChanges`), the white space at the start and end of every line left out (JavaScript's trim). They are aligned by dynamic programming when both sequences together have fewer than 500 characters and by Myers' algorithm otherwise (both with the shared time limit), and then improved in this order: `optimizeSequenceDiffs` with the boundary score of characters (150 right after a line break, 0 between CR and LF, else 10 when the category changes, 1 more from a lower to an upper case letter, plus 0 for letters and digits, 10 at the ends, 2 for other characters, 30 for `,` and `;`, 3 for spaces and tabs, 10 for line breaks), `extendDiffsToEntireWordIfAppropriate` (a change that touches a word of letters and digits grows over it when the equal part is less than two thirds of the word, counting both sequences; with `ExtendToSubwords` a second pass over sub-words (`fooBar` is `foo` and `Bar`) grows it when anything of the sub-word changed), `removeShortMatches` (changes with at most two equal characters between them join), `removeVeryShortMatchingTextBetweenLongDiffs` (two changes with at most 20 characters of text on one line between them join when they are large, by VS Code's weight formula; then a change of more than 100 characters takes over a line prefix or suffix of at most three non-white characters). The ranges are then translated to positions (`translateRange`: the start after the trimmed white space of its line, the end before it, never reversed). The lines between the blocks that are equal after trimming but not equal are refined as blocks of their own unless `IgnoreTrimWhitespace` is set. `lineRangeMappingFromRangeMappings` makes the line blocks of the character changes (`getLineRangeMapping`: a change that starts at the end of a line does not include that line, one that ends at the start of a line does not include it) and joins the ones whose blocks overlap or touch. With `IgnoreTrimWhitespace` a block whose lines differ only in white space has no character change and so no entry, and white space at the start and end of a line is never part of a character change (a joined line break leaves the white space of the next line as it was, so applying the character changes gives the modified text up to white space).
  - *Time limit.* One clock (`Options.MaxComputationTime`, `Options.Now`) covers the line stage and every refinement, as VS Code's one `DateTimeout`. As in VS Code the limit makes an algorithm return the trivial diff, and nothing is skipped: when it passes in the line stage the line alignment is one change over both texts, which is then refined as a block of its own (its refinement, if the time is over, is one character change over both texts); when it passes in the character stage the block being aligned, and every block after it, has one character change over the whole block (the characters of the block, without the white space trimmed under `IgnoreTrimWhitespace`), while the blocks refined before keep their character changes. `HitTimeout` is set when any algorithm hit the limit. The "unrefined line diff" is not what comes back after the limit; a caller that wants only lines uses `ComputeLines`. The context is looked at once per refined block and every 1024 steps of an algorithm; a done context gives `DiffCancelled`. Other errors: `DiffAlignmentBroken` (the unchanged lines before a change differ in number) and `DiffRangeInvalid` (a block that is not part of a valid diff).
  - *Validate.* Also checks the character changes as far as the line counts allow: their lines lie in the texts and in the block of their change (or the line before it or after it where a change starts at the end of a line or ends at the start of one), they are not reversed, and they are sorted and do not overlap.
  - *Fixtures.* `TestVSCodeFixtures` (skipped without `reference/`) compares, for each of the 58 fixture folders, the line blocks and every character change (as `fixtures.test.ts` writes them, with UTF-16 columns and the `EOL` mark) with `advanced.expected.diff.json`; `IgnoreTrimWhitespace` is set for the folders with `trimws` in their name, the `moves` of the expectations are left out (VS Code computes them in addition and they do not change `diffs`), and the character changes must rebuild the modified text. All 58 match; nothing is skipped. The tests also cover the ported cases of `defaultLinesDiffComputer.test.ts` (`LinesSliceCharSequence` and `getLineRangeMapping`, the Myers smoke test), each heuristic, seeded random texts (with non-ASCII and invalid UTF-8 handled by the sequence), the time limit in both stages, cancellation, and a 3000-line file with scattered edits.
  - *Setting.* `diffEditor.maxComputationTime` (number, default 5000, no minimum; "Timeout in milliseconds after which diff computation is cancelled. Use 0 for no timeout."; language overridable, as VS Code's editor settings; the "Diff Editor" group of the settings editor shows it) is registered by step 9.2, but the diff editor (step 9.5) reads it and converts it to `Options.MaxComputationTime` (milliseconds; a negative value is invalid). `diffEditor.ignoreTrimWhitespace` is registered by step 9.5, which builds the editor that reads both; the other `diffEditor.*` settings are registered by the step that builds the editor that reads them (rule 7 of the plan).
- Three-way merge: (a) **conflict markers**: parse `<<<<<<<`, optional `|||||||` (diff3 base), `=======`, `>>>>>>>` into conflicts (VS Code's `merge-conflict` parser); the current and incoming inputs are the file with every conflict taken from one side, the base is the `|||||||` sections or, without them, empty conflict regions; (b) **`--merge` inputs**: base, input 1 (incoming) and input 2 (current) are diffed against the base, and overlapping changes become conflicts (VS Code's `ModifiedBaseRange`). The result starts as the base with every non-conflicting change applied and each conflict region holding the base text, marked unhandled.
- **Three-way merge model as built (step 9.3; `core/diff`, no UI; not safe for concurrent use).** Ported from `mergeConflictParser.ts`, `modifiedBaseRange.ts`, `mapping.ts` (`MappingAlignment`), `mergeEditorModel.ts` and `textModelDiffs.ts`. Texts are lines without line breaks (a text with no line counts as one empty line, as `Compute` does); inputs are named as in the merge editor: **input 1 is the incoming side** (left pane, "theirs", the first path of `--merge <path1> <path2> <base> <result>`, §10.11) and **input 2 is the current side** (right pane, "ours"/HEAD, the second path).
  - *Marker parser* (`ParseConflictMarkers(lines) []MarkerConflict`, `ContainsConflictMarkers(text)`), exactly `scanDocument`: a line that starts with `<<<<<<<` begins a conflict (so `<<<<<<<<` does too; indented markers are not markers); inside a conflict and before its splitter a line that starts with `|||||||` is a common-ancestor header (several are kept) and the line `=======` (exactly, nothing after it) is the splitter (a second one is text); a line that starts with `>>>>>>>` ends the conflict, which is dropped when it has no splitter; a `<<<<<<<` inside an open conflict makes the scan **give up** (the conflicts found so far stay, nothing after is looked at; it is not "restart at the new marker"); a conflict still open at the end of the text is dropped; markers outside a conflict and white-space-only lines are ignored. There is no error. A label is the text after the marker and one more character (`HEAD` in `<<<<<<< HEAD`, `EAD` in `<<<<<<<HEAD`, as `substring(marker.length + 1)`). Each `MarkerConflict` has the whole `Range` and, per section (`MarkerSection`: `Header`, `Content`, `Label`), the 1-based line ranges of current, ancestors, splitter and incoming.
  - *Inputs.* `MergeInputsFromMarkers(lines, conflicts)`/`MergeInputsFromMarkerText(text)` (a text without a complete conflict is `MergeMarkersNotFound`): input 2 is the file with every conflict taken from its current side, input 1 with every conflict taken from its incoming side, the base the file with every conflict taken from its first `|||||||` section, or with the conflict removed (an empty region) when it has none; the labels (`Input1Label`, `Input2Label`, `BaseLabel`) are the first non-empty ones. `MergeInputs.MarkedConflicts` records where the conflicts are in the base: a change that touches one is a conflict to resolve even when only one side differs from the (empty) base there, or both sides are equal, which the diffs alone would resolve silently. `MergeInputsFromFiles(base, input1, input2)` is the `--merge` flow (the result path is the caller's, 9.7). **Line breaks:** `SplitLines` strips `\n` and `\r\n` and returns the line break most lines end with (the first on a tie, `\n` without any); `MergeInputs.EOL` carries it and `ResultText` joins with it, so a CRLF file is written back as CRLF; a mixed file is written with its majority break (the lines that had the other one lose it).
  - *diff3* (`ModifiedBaseRangesFromDiffs`): `Compute` diffs the base against each input (white space counts, no time limit by default: `MergeOptions` can set one); the changes of both lists are sorted by their first base line (input 1 first on a tie) and grouped as long as the next one **intersects or touches** the group (`MappingAlignment.compute`: a change that ends on the line where another begins is joined with it, two insertions at the same place are joined; one unchanged line between keeps them apart). Each group is a `ModifiedBaseRange` with the base range, the range of each input (the base range moved by the lines the earlier changes of that input added, joined with its own changes), the changes of each input with their character changes (`Input1Diffs`, `Input2Diffs`), `IsConflicting()` (both changed it) and `IsEqualChange` (the same changes of the same lines to the same text: not a conflict to resolve). `RangeState` (zero value base; `StateInput1`, `StateInput2`, `StateBoth` with `FirstInput`, `StateUnrecognized` for text edited by hand) has VS Code's `withInputValue`/`toggle`/`swap` (its state of input 1 with input 2 excluded returns the state of input 2, a slip not copied). `TextForState` gives the lines of a state as `getEditForBase` does: an input without changes in the range adds nothing, one input is its whole range, both inputs are the whole range of the first and then of the second (VS Code's "dumb" combination).
  - *Result model* (`NewMergeModel(ctx, MergeInputs, MergeOptions)`; a done context is `DiffCancelled`, untouched). The result starts as the base with the regions applied that are not conflicts (`computeAutoMergedResult`: only input 2 changed: its lines; only input 1: its lines; the same change in both: input 1's) and these are handled; a conflict (both changed differently, or touching `MarkedConflicts`) keeps the base lines, state base, unhandled. `Regions()` lists every region, `Conflicts()` those to resolve (the conflict index of all other methods); a `Region` has the `ModifiedBaseRange`, its `Result` line range now, `State`, `Handled`, `Conflict`. `UnhandledCount()`, `HandledCount()` count conflicts. **Accept** (`Accept(conflict, Choice)`, `AcceptAll(Choice)`, `SetState`, `Toggle`, `Reset`): replaces the result lines of the conflict by `AcceptBase` (base lines), `AcceptInput1` (incoming), `AcceptInput2` (current) or `AcceptBoth` (**current, then incoming**, state `StateBoth` with input 2 first), marks it handled, shifts the regions after it, and returns the `Edit{StartLine, EndLine, NewLines}` (lines of the result as they were before the edit) for the editor to apply to its document as one undoable edit (`AcceptAll` returns the edits of the unhandled conflicts from the last to the first, so they apply in order and against the old result alike). `Reset` puts the base lines back and marks it unhandled; `SetHandled` sets the flag only. **Regions follow edits of the result** (the decorations of VS Code, kept as line ranges instead of recomputing a diff of the result against the base): `ApplyResultEdit(startLine, endLine, newLines)` is a replacement of whole lines; regions before it stay, regions after it move, and a region it overlaps (replaces a line of it, inserts inside it, or inserts into an empty one; an edit at the very start or end of a region that replaces none of its lines is outside it) takes the new lines (the first one overlapped; the others keep what is left of them) and becomes `StateUnrecognized`, or the state its lines equal (`computeState`: base, input 1, input 2, both with input 2 first, both with input 1 first). A manual edit does not change `Handled`. `ResultText()` is the file `Complete Merge` writes (whether to save with unhandled conflicts is the UI's question, `UnhandledCount`); `RegionAtResultLine` finds the region of a line. Undo is the document's. Errors: `MergeConflictIndexInvalid`, `MergeChoiceInvalid`, `MergeStateInvalid`, `MergeEditInvalid`, `MergeEOLInvalid`, `MergeMarkersNotFound`, `MergeRangeWithoutChange` (an internal check).
  - *Not built:* the "smart" combination of both inputs by character changes (`smartCombineInputs`: `RangeState` has no smart flag), re-deriving the states from a result text (`resultTextModelDiffs`, `resetDirtyConflictsToBase`), `getResultValueWithConflictMarkers`, moves, the VS Code option `mergeEditor.diffAlgorithm` (the advanced algorithm is the only one), telemetry. Steps 9.7 and 9.8 build the merge editor on this model: they hand the document edits to `ApplyResultEdit`, apply the returned edits of `Accept` and `AcceptAll` to the result document, show `Regions()` as decorations, and write `ResultText()`. Limits of the marker flow: a conflict with no base section is an insertion of both sides at one place of the base, so two conflicts with no line between them are one region, and an input whose insertion the diff slides away from the other's makes two regions (nothing is lost; both texts are in the result).
- `gui/diffeditor`: two editors side by side (or inline), aligned with view zones, scroll-synced, changed lines and characters colored, an overview ruler, next/previous change (Alt+F5, Shift+Alt+F5); the original side is read-only; save and dirty apply to the modified side; narrow widths switch to inline (`diffEditor.useInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited`). Step 9.5 builds the side-by-side view; step 9.6 the inline view and the switch to it (see "Diff editor as built" and "Inline view and compare commands as built" below).

- **Diff editor as built (step 9.5; `gui/diffeditor`, the pane in `gui/app/diffpane.go`).** The side-by-side view; the inline view is step 9.6 ("Inline view and compare commands as built", below).
  - *Layout.* A `Row`: the original editor, a 1 px divider (`diffEditor.border`, falling back to `editorGroup.border`), the modified editor, and the overview ruler (`RulerWidth` = the width of an editor scrollbar). The two editors always have the same width (50/50, no sash: VS Code's draggable sash and the `diffEditor.experimental` options are not built). Each side is an ordinary `editor.Editor` over its own view model; the diff editor does not copy editor code. Minimap, folding and sticky scroll are off on both sides (`AdjustOptions`: VS Code turns them off in its diff editor; collapsing unchanged regions and its settings `diffEditor.hideUnchangedRegions.*` are not built); the original side is read-only.
  - *Alignment.* `ComputeAlignment` is a Glutter-free port of VS Code's `computeRangeAlignment`: for each changed block the side with fewer rows gets a view zone (§9.1, step 9.4) after its last line of the block so that the next unchanged line is at the same row on both sides; inside a block the character changes of `Inner` are aligned line by line when a hunk starts or ends on a different line on each side (the `innerHunkAlignment` rule), and a wrapped line counts the rows it takes in its editor (zones are re-fitted when the width or the wrapping changes). Zones on the same line are merged into one; zones follow the edits of the document, and unchanged zones are not touched when the diff is recomputed. The zone is the diagonal fill (`diffEditor.diagonalFill`, `/` stripes in an 8 px tile).
  - *Decorations.* `Marks` makes `editor.BackgroundMark`s (a new editor extension, below): whole-line marks for the changed lines (`diffEditor.insertedLineBackground`, `diffEditor.removedLineBackground`, with the gutter colors `diffEditorGutter.insertedLineBackground` / `removedLineBackground` over the line number column) and character marks for the inner changes (`diffEditor.insertedTextBackground`, `removedTextBackground`, with the optional `insertedTextBorder` / `removedTextBorder`). A change that is an empty range on one side marks nothing there (its zone shows the other side's lines). The colors are 12 registered theme colors with VS Code's defaults (`registerDiffEditorColors`), carried by `kit.Colors` as `Diff*` fields; a missing gutter or overview color falls back to the line color, a missing border to the group border.
  - *Scroll sync.* Both editors scroll together vertically (a scroll of one sets the other's top: the row heights match thanks to the zones) and horizontally; a guard stops the echo. The selections and cursors are independent.
  - *Recomputing.* The diff is computed on a goroutine with `diff.Compute` and the setting values `diffEditor.ignoreTrimWhitespace` (boolean, default true, language overridable, "Diff Editor" group; VS Code's `diffEditor.ignoreTrimWhitespace`) and `diffEditor.maxComputationTime` (milliseconds; 0 or less for no limit). The first diff starts at once; a change of either document starts a 200 ms debounce (`DebounceDelay`); a new change, a setting change or `Dispose` cancels the running computation; a result of an older document version or generation is dropped. A result with `HitTimeout` is shown as computed (one change over both texts), without a notice. Computing runs off the UI thread and hands the result over with the binding's `Post`.
  - *Overview ruler.* A strip at the right edge: the left half marks removed ranges (`diffEditorOverview.removedForeground`), the right half inserted ones (`insertedForeground`), positioned by the rows of the whole editor (zones included); a press or drag scrolls the editors so that the place is in the middle (`ScrollToFraction`).
  - *Navigation.* `workbench.action.compareEditor.nextChange` / `previousChange` ("Go to Next Change" / "Go to Previous Change"; Alt+F5 / Shift+Alt+F5, `when: textCompareEditorVisible`) go to the next or previous change of the modified side relative to its cursor line, wrapping around at the ends (`ChangeToGo`, VS Code's `goToDiff`), put the cursor at the start of the change and reveal it in the center of the view (always, as `revealRangeInCenter`, not only when out of view), and focus the modified editor. With no diff editor, or with no changes, they do nothing (the keys are not bound then: nothing is eaten). `workbench.action.compareEditor.focusPrimarySide` (modified), `focusSecondarySide` (original) and `focusOtherSide` move the focus; `diffEditor.switchSide` ("Switch Side", category "Diff Editor", precondition `isInDiffEditor`) moves the focus to the other side with the selection mapped by the line alignment (`TranslatePosition`, a port of VS Code's `translatePosition`). None of the focus commands has a default key.
  - *Context keys.* `textCompareEditorVisible` (a diff editor is the active tab of any group) and `textCompareEditorActive` (the active group's active editor is one), `isInDiffEditor` (the focused editor is a side of one), `diffEditorOriginalWritable` (always false) and `diffEditorModifiedWritable`, set in a scope of keys of the diff widget; `editorReadonly` follows the focused side. `activeEditor` is `emetos.diff`.
  - *The input* (`core/editorgroup.DiffInput`, type `emetos.diff`). It holds the two documents. Name `"<original> ↔ <modified>"` (names as `TextInput` gives them, so a virtual document shows its label), title with both paths, `Resource()` is not set (it is no file: the recent-files list, Reveal and the like skip it), `Dirty()` is the modified document's, capabilities are `CapCanSplit` (it can be split) plus Readonly and Untitled when the modified document is. Two inputs with the same pair are the same tab (`Matches`), and a diff of the same pair opened again activates the tab. Save, Save All, auto save and external-change handling act on the modified document (`editorgroup.EditedDocument`, the interface `DocumentEditor`); a close asks about the modified document, naming it ("Do you want to save the changes you made to new.go?"), and the dirty original is never saved or asked about (a document that a diff shows read-only can still be changed by another editor). `PlanClose` treats inputs that edit the same document as showing the same thing: closing a diff while a text editor of the modified document remains in another group asks nothing. The pane counts one use of each document while it lives and releases them when it closes.
  - *Restore.* The input serializes as `{"original": <uri>, "modified": <uri>}`; the documents are opened by `PrepareRestore` with those of the text editors (hot-exit backups of the modified side come back as for any document). A diff whose file is gone is dropped with the same notice as a text editor ("1 editor could not be restored ..." counts one editor even if both sides are gone); a virtual original whose provider no longer exists is dropped silently (as a virtual text editor is); a saved value that cannot be parsed (`DiffInputInvalid`) is logged and dropped.
  - *`dev.openDiff {"original", "modified"}`* (§15.2) opens a comparison in the active group as a kept tab.
  - *Editor extensions* (`gui/editor`, used by the diff editor): `Options.ReadOnly` (the view model refuses every edit with the error tag `EditorReadOnly` -- typing, undo/redo, clipboard cut/paste, snippets and replace -- while movement, selection and copy work; the `editorReadonly` key follows `ViewModel.ReadOnly()`, which is `Options.ReadOnly || document.ReadOnly()`; this option did not exist before step 9.5); background marks (`SetBackgroundMarks([]BackgroundMark)`: a range, whole-line or not, a fill, an optional border and an optional gutter fill; they are decorations in the view model's owner slot 7, so they follow edits, and are painted under everything else, zone rows skipped); scroll listeners (`AddScrollListener` / `RemoveScrollListener`, `ScrollTo`; `SetScrollTop` notifies only when the value changes).
  - *Not built:* collapsing unchanged regions, moved-code detection and its arrows, the sash, the accessible diff viewer (F7), the revert arrows and "Revert Block" / "Stage" actions, `diffEditor.originalEditable`, `openSide` and swap-sides actions, `diffEditor.renderIndicators`, `diffEditor.wordWrap` (the editors use `editor.wordWrap`), editor title buttons, and the status bar items of the compared editors (the sub-editors are not in the group's editor map: no status bar for the cursor, no language-follow, no context menu of their own).

- **Inline view and compare commands as built (step 9.6; `gui/diffeditor/inline.go`, `gui/editor/linesview.go`, `gui/app/compare.go`, `gui/app/launch.go`).**
  - *The choice of the view.* `diffeditor.Mode` is `SideBySide` or `Inline`. The settings (language overridable, in the "Diff Editor" group, read live; VS Code's keys, defaults and descriptions): `diffEditor.renderSideBySide` (boolean, true: "Controls whether the diff editor shows the diff side by side or inline."), `diffEditor.renderSideBySideInlineBreakpoint` (number, 900: "If the diff editor width is smaller than this value, the inline view is used.") and `diffEditor.useInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited` (boolean, true: "If enabled and the editor width is too small, the inline view is used."). The view is inline when `renderSideBySide` is off, or when `useInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited` is on and the width of the diff editor (the whole widget, the ruler included) is **no more than** the breakpoint (VS Code's `<=`: 900 px is inline). The widget tells the model its width while it is built (`Model.SetWidth`); the model changes its mode on the UI thread a frame later (`SetMode`, never during a build), so a resize across the breakpoint shows the old view for one frame. The context keys of the scope of the diff editor are `diffEditorInlineMode` and `diffEditorRenderSideBySideInlineBreakpointReached` (VS Code's `couldShowInlineViewBecauseOfSize`: `renderSideBySide` on and the width at or under the breakpoint, whatever the other setting says). Not built: VS Code's "smooth resize" (staying inline while a drag ends past the breakpoint), compact mode and its smart choice, the screen reader rule, `diffEditor.experimental.useTrueInlineView`, the keys `diffEditorTemporaryInlineMode` and `diffEditorAutomaticRenderSideBySide`, the "Diff View" submenu and "Inline View" button of the editor title (there is no editor title toolbar yet).
  - *Switching.* The row of the widget has keyed children (`original`, `divider`, `modified`, `ruler`), so the modified editor is the same element in both views: its view model, cursors, selections, scroll position, find state and keyboard focus stay; coming back to side by side scrolls the original editor to where the modified one is. The view model of the modified side is never replaced. Switching takes the zones and marks of the other view off (the filler of the side by side view, the zones of the deleted lines, the marks of the original editor) and puts the ones of the new view on; a diff computed in one view is shown in the other without being computed again.
  - *Inline layout* (`Model.setInlineZones`, VS Code's inline branch of `diffEditorViewZones.ts`). One editor shows the modified document, editable as in the side by side view, with the inserted lines and characters marked as there (`insertedLineBackground`, `insertedTextBackground`, the gutter strips). For each change that has original lines there is one view zone in the modified editor, **above the line that took their place**: `AfterLine` is the change's first modified line minus 1 (0 above the first line; the number of lines for a change at the end), and it has as many rows as the change has original lines (a pure insertion has no zone; a pure deletion has a zone and no marked lines; a replacement has both). Zones follow the edits of the modified document as every zone does (§9.1) until the next diff replaces them; a diff whose changes and colors are those of the zones in place leaves the zones alone (so a recompute after an edit that is no change, such as trailing white space, makes no new widgets). No filler zones are used: the original editor's zones, scroll sync and marks are off while inline, and the original view model only supplies the text, the tokens and the brackets of the deleted lines. A diff that is a little behind the text names lines that are gone: the zone is put after the last line instead.
  - *The deleted lines* (`editor.NewLinesView`, `RenderLinesView`). The widget of a zone draws the lines of the original document, one row each, in the font, the size, the tab size, the syntax colors and the bracket colors of the original editor's view model (so the colors of the original text, tokenized as far as the budget allows, as a text layer does), with the whole-line fill `diffEditor.removedLineBackground` and the removed characters in `diffEditor.removedTextBackground` (and `removedTextBorder`), from the character changes of the diff. The text follows the horizontal scroll of the modified editor. Its margin is the gutter strip of the zone (`ZoneSpec.Gutter`: `diffEditorGutter.removedLineBackground`) with a minus sign (`ZoneSpec.GutterGlyph`: the Codicon `remove`, 11 px, 70% opacity, at the right of the line decorations column on every row: VS Code's `delete-sign`). The zone takes no pointer events. Not built: selecting or copying the deleted text ("Copy deleted lines" and "Revert this change" of its margin menu), the plus sign of the inserted lines (VS Code's `renderIndicators`; the side by side view has no indicators either), wrapping of the deleted lines (with word wrap on, a deleted line stays one row, as long as it is, and scrolls with the editor; the rows of the zone are the number of deleted lines; the horizontal scroll range is the modified editor's, so a deleted line wider than every modified line cannot be scrolled to its end), the rows of the original line numbers (`diffEditor.hideOriginalLineNumbers`).
  - *Ruler and navigation.* The overview ruler measures the one editor: its left half marks the zone of the deleted lines of each change (`diffEditorOverview.removedForeground`), its right half the modified lines (`insertedForeground`). Alt+F5 and Shift+Alt+F5 go to the first line of the modified range of the next or previous change as in the side by side view (`ChangeToGo`); for a pure deletion that is the line after the zone, the zone being above the cursor. `focusSecondarySide` and `focusOtherSide` focus the one editor; `diffEditor.switchSide` does nothing while inline.
  - *Commands.* `toggle.diff.renderSideBySide` ("Toggle Inline View", category "Compare", in the palette while `textCompareEditorActive`; no key) writes `!diffEditor.renderSideBySide`; `diffEditor.toggleUseInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited` ("Toggle Use Inline View When Space Is Limited", precondition `isInDiffEditor`, not in the palette, as in VS Code) writes `!diffEditor.useInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited`; `diffEditor.setViewMode.inline` (`renderSideBySide` false), `.sideBySide` (`renderSideBySide` true and `useInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited` false) and `.automatic` (both true) have no title and no key (VS Code gives them titles in the "Diff View" submenu only). The settings are written where the user has them: the workspace settings when the workspace sets the key, else the user's.
  - *Compare with Saved* (`workbench.files.action.compareWithSaved`, palette "Compare Active File with Saved" in "File", **Ctrl+K D**, no `when`). The left side is the file as it is on disk, the right side the live document, opened as a kept tab named `<file> (in file) ↔ <file>` (VS Code's `{0} (in file) ↔ {1}`). The file is the argument (a path or a `file://` URI of a file that an editor has open; anything else that is not nothing fails with `CompareResourceInvalid`), else the file of the active editor, which for a diff editor is its modified side. A document that is not a file (untitled, virtual) or no editor: nothing happens. The left side is the read-only virtual document `emetos-virtual:/saved/<path>` (§7.1), whose provider reads the file with `fileio.Read` (the encoding detection and the line endings of a file read) each time it is opened, with the language of the file's name; it is read on the UI thread when the diff opens (as an opened file is) and **again, in the background, when the file changes**: when the modified document is saved (`Document.OnSaved`, followed by the pane of the diff) and when the watcher reports a change of the file (`externalEvents`), the document gets the new text (`SetText`) when it differs and the diff is computed again. A file that cannot be read again (deleted) leaves the text as it was. The same comparison again activates the tab.
  - *Compare Active File with Clipboard* (`workbench.files.action.compareWithClipboard`, **Ctrl+K C**, "File"). The left side is a read-only virtual document `emetos-virtual:/clipboard/<n>/<file name>` (a new number for each run: the clipboard may have changed, so each is a tab of its own) with the text the clipboard had when the command ran, tab name `Clipboard ↔ <file name>` (VS Code's label `Clipboard ↔ {0}`: the left side's name is "Clipboard"), the right side is the active file's document (not for virtual documents). The texts are kept in the process while it runs (`Process.clipboard`); after a restart a restored comparison finds its text gone (`CompareClipboardGone`) and is dropped silently like a virtual document whose provider is gone. `CompareClipboardNumberInvalid` is a path that has no number.
  - *Compare Active File With...* (`workbench.files.action.compareFileWith`, "File", precondition `activeEditor`). The quick open of files is shown with the second item active (`ItemSecond`: the first is the file that is open); the file that is chosen is not opened: the diff of the active editor's document (left) and that file (right) opens instead (`quickinput.ShowOptions.OnFile`, which gives the chosen `FileOpen` to the function of the session; Escape ends the pick and the next quick open opens files as usual). The file is read in the background; one that cannot be opened is a notification. Without an editor the command does nothing.
  - *The Explorer's comparisons.* `selectForCompare` ("Select for Compare", group `3_compare`, order 30), `compareFiles` ("Compare with Selected", order 20) and `compareSelected` ("Compare Selected", order 30) are rows of the Explorer's context menu (VS Code's `when` clauses: the pressed row is not a folder and has a resource; Compare with Selected also needs the context key `resourceSelectedForCompare` and not two selected rows; Select for Compare not two selected rows; Compare Selected two selected rows, the key `listDoubleSelection`, which the menu context of the Explorer sets from the selection). They have no title as commands, no key, and are not in the palette. `selectForCompare` remembers the file of the focused row (or the argument's, or the active editor's) and sets the window's key `resourceSelectedForCompare` (cleared when the shell goes away); a folder is not selected. `compareFiles` opens the remembered file (left) and the file it is run on (right); `compareSelected` the two selected files, in the order of the selection (the tree's order), left first; with a folder among them, or with nothing remembered, nothing happens. Files are read in the background. Not built: the same rows in the Open Editors view and in the tab menu (`OpenEditorsContext`, `TwoEditorsSelectedInGroup`: Emetos has no Open Editors context menu, and tabs have no multi-selection), "Compare New Untitled Text Files" (the original side of Emetos's diff editor is read-only).
  - *`emetos --diff a b`* (§10.11). The two files are read in the background and compared in the active group of the window the launch opens in: the first on the left, kept tab `a ↔ b`; relative paths were made absolute against the working directory of the launch by the parser. The first instance does the same on top of its restored editors. With `--wait` the client waits until the **diff editor's tab is closed** (not until the files are closed: another editor may still show a file; closing the window ends the wait too); the exit code is 0, or 1 when a file cannot be opened (a notification names it; also when a different number of files than two arrives, which the parser does not allow). `--merge` still gets its notice (step 9.7).
  - *The notice of a conflict* (§6.3 "External changes"). The notification about a dirty document whose file changed on disk, and the one of a save that finds the file newer (`FileModifiedSince`), have a first button **Compare** (VS Code's "Compare", `workbench.files.action.resolveConflict`): the same diff as Compare with Saved, with the file on disk (`<file> (in file)`, read-only) on the left and the document with its unsaved changes on the right. The notification stays: **Overwrite** and **Revert** resolve it as before, and the left side then shows what was written. VS Code's title of this diff, `{0} (in file) ↔ {1} (in Emetos) - Resolve save conflict`, and the "Learn More" help are not built.
  - *Registered by this step.* Commands: `toggle.diff.renderSideBySide`, `diffEditor.toggleUseInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited`, `diffEditor.setViewMode.inline|sideBySide|automatic`, `workbench.files.action.compareWithSaved`, `compareFileWith`, `compareWithClipboard`, `selectForCompare`, `compareFiles`, `compareSelected`. Keys: Ctrl+K D and Ctrl+K C. Menu rows: the three of the Explorer's context menu, and the palette rules for Toggle Inline View and the toggle of the limited space. Settings: the three above. Context keys: `diffEditorInlineMode`, `diffEditorRenderSideBySideInlineBreakpointReached` (the scope of the diff editor), `resourceSelectedForCompare` (the window), `listDoubleSelection` (the menu context of the Explorer). Errors: `VirtualPrefixInvalid`, `VirtualPrefixDuplicate`, `CompareResourceInvalid`, `CompareClipboardNumberInvalid`, `CompareClipboardGone`. Editor extensions (`gui/editor`): `ZoneSpec.Gutter` and `ZoneSpec.GutterGlyph` (the margin of a zone), `NewLinesView`.
- `gui/mergeeditor`: incoming (left) and current (right) on top, the result below, with accept incoming/current/both (current then incoming) per conflict and for all, and "Complete Merge": for a marker file it saves the file; for `--merge` it writes the `result` path and exits 0 when `--wait` is on (1 if the window closes without completing).

## 10. The workbench (`gui/workbench` and others)

### 10.1 Layout

Menu bar (File, Edit, Selection, View, Go, Help) at the top, activity bar on the left, primary side bar, editor area, panel, status bar. Part sizes, visibility and the panel position are saved per window (§10.10). The window's title bar is the compositor's, or Glutter's where the compositor draws none (`DecorationsAuto`); Emetos adds nothing to it except the title (`<file> - <folder> - Emetos`). From milestone M9b on (D17, §10.14) Emetos draws the title bar itself (`DecorationsApp`): one 35 px row with the menu (`window.menuBarVisibility`, default `compact`: a `≡` button), the workspace tabs and the window buttons, which takes the place of the menu bar row; the window title still goes to the task bar.

**Built in `gui/workbench` (step 6.6).** `Workbench` is the widget that lays the parts out as VS Code's grid does, with `kit.SplitView`: the menu bar area over a row of the activity bar, the side bar and the center, over the status bar; the center is the editor area and the panel, in a split of its own.
