The parts are empty containers that the views of later steps fill (the options of `Workbench` take the content of each: the editor area, the title and body of the side bar, the tabs and body of the panel, the items of the status bar, the menus of the menu bar, the icons of the activity bar).

| Part | Size (VS Code's) | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Menu bar area | 30 px, as high as `kit.MenuBar`; none when hidden | `window.menuBarVisibility`: classic and visible show it, toggle shows it after the Alt key (or `workbench.actions.menubar.focus`), hidden never. The bar is always mounted, for the Alt key. Background and border: `titleBar.activeBackground`, `titleBar.border` |
| Activity bar | 48 px wide, fixed | Side of the side bar (left, or right with the side bar). Icons at the top, a Manage area at the bottom. `activityBar.background`, `activityBar.border` |
| Side bar | 300 px, at least 170 | Title (uppercase, 11 px, 35 px high) over the body. Priority low. `sideBar.*`, `sideBarTitle.*` |
| Editor area | at least 220 x 70 | Highest priority: takes what the window gains or loses, until it is at its least; then the panel and the side bar give way, in that order of priority. Hosts the editor with its breadcrumbs, the notices and the home page |
| Panel | 300 px, at least 77 high or 300 wide | Bottom, top, left or right of the editor area. Title strip (35 px) with the tabs of its views, a maximize and a hide button. Priority low. `panel.*`, `panelTitle.*` |
| Status bar | 22 px | The items of the status bar registry, left and right (see "Status bar as built" below). `statusBar.*`, and `statusBar.noFolder*` while no folder is open |

The split views never lay out for no room (a window that has no size yet, or is minimized): the panes keep the sizes they were given. A part that moves (the panel to another side, the side bar to the other side) keeps its widgets and their state (global keys), and a hidden part comes back with the size it had. Maximizing the panel hides the editor area and gives the panel its place; restoring gives the panel the size it had. Hiding a part that has the focus focuses the editor area (the panel, when the editor area is hidden).

**State.** `Layout` is the model (no widget): `State()` gives and `Restore(LayoutState)` takes the visibility of the side bar, activity bar, status bar and panel, the width of the side bar, the height and the width of the panel (also its last non-maximized size, since a maximized panel does not record its size), the side of the side bar, the position of the panel and whether it is maximized. Step 7.5 writes it. The settings below decide where the side bar is and whether the activity bar and the status bar are shown, now and whenever they change, as in VS Code; the position and the size of the panel are state, and `workbench.panel.defaultLocation` is where a new window starts. The panel is hidden in a new window, as in VS Code.

**Commands** (VS Code's ids, titles and Linux keys; they are registered with the other commands, §11.2): `workbench.action.toggleSidebarVisibility` (Ctrl+B), `workbench.action.togglePanel` (Ctrl+J), `workbench.action.toggleMaximizedPanel`, `workbench.action.positionPanelLeft`, `...Right`, `...Bottom`, `...Top`, `workbench.action.focusSideBar` (Ctrl+0), `workbench.action.focusActiveEditorGroup`, `workbench.action.focusPanel`, `workbench.action.focusStatusBar`, `workbench.action.focusActivityBar`, and the ones VS Code keeps in a setting, which write it: `workbench.action.toggleStatusbarVisibility` (`workbench.statusBar.visible`), `workbench.action.toggleActivityBarVisibility` (`workbench.activityBar.location`, hidden or default) and `workbench.action.toggleMenuBar` (`window.menuBarVisibility`: classic or visible to toggle, else classic), and `workbench.actions.menubar.focus`. Showing a part moves the panel or side bar into view before it gets the focus.

**Status bar as built (step 6.20, `gui/workbench/statusmodel.go`, `statusbar.go`, `statuslabels.go`, `statusnotify.go`; the items and pickers in `gui/app/statusbar.go` and `statuspickers.go`).** Ported from `statusbarModel.ts`, `statusbarPart.ts`, `statusbarItem.ts`, `editorStatus.ts`, `notificationsStatus.ts` and the markers contribution.
- **Registry.** `workbench.StatusBar` (no Glutter type; the SPEC lists no package of its own for it, so it sits beside `Layout`) holds items: `Add(id, alignment, priority, StatusEntry)` gives a `StatusAccessor` with `Update`, `SetVisible` and `Dispose`, `Items(alignment)` lists the visible ones in the order of the bar, and `OnChange` tells the widget. As in VS Code the higher priority is further left on both sides, equal priorities keep the order they were added in, and negative infinity is last, so the notifications bell ends the right side and the status message ends the left side. An update to an equal entry tells nobody. `StatusEntry` has the name, text, tooltip, command and arguments, the kind (`StatusStandard`, `StatusWarning`, `StatusError`, `StatusProminent`) and `ShowProgress`; the text takes codicons as `$(name)` (`ParseStatusText`; `$(name~spin)` is drawn still, `\$(name)` is the text, a name that is not a codicon is shown as written; `fonts.CodiconByName` looks the names up in a table the generator makes from `codicon.csv`). The window owns the registry (`Window.StatusBar`), so that a later step (profiles, remote) adds an item with `Add`.
- **Widget.** `Workbench` shows it when `Options.StatusBar` is set (`StatusBarOptions`: the registry, the label of the key of a command, a hook for the failure of a command). 22 px high on `statusBar.background` (`statusBar.noFolderBackground` without a folder), 12 px text and 16 px icons. An item is a box with 3 px margins and 5 px padding, and 8 px padding without margin for the first item on the left, the last on the right and an item that has a kind; the left side starts 2 px from the edge and the right ends 2 px from it. A clickable item (one with a command) has the pointer cursor, `statusBarItem.hoverBackground` and `statusBarItem.hoverForeground` under the pointer and `statusBarItem.activeBackground` while pressed; the kinds have their own colors and hover colors. A click runs the command through the registry (`command.SourceStatusBar`) with the arguments of the item; a failing command is reported as an error notification. The tooltip is a compact hover above the item after the hover delay (the items share a delegate, so the next one shows at once), and ends with the key of the command in brackets when it has one ("Go to Line/Column (Ctrl+G)"). The right side takes its natural width and the left side gets what is left and cuts off what does not fit (no ellipsis; VS Code wraps the right side). `workbench.statusBar.visible` and Toggle Status Bar Visibility (step 6.6) hide it.
- **Items** (the ids are those of VS Code's `status.*`; the priority decides the place):
  | Item | Side, priority | Text | Click |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Problems (`status.problems`) | left, 50 | `$(error) E $(warning) W`, and `$(info) I` when there are infos (a count past 999 is written "1K", past 9999 "10K+"); the tooltip lists the counts ("Errors: 2, Warnings: 1") or says "No Problems" | `workbench.actions.view.toggleProblems` |
  | Status message (`status.message`) | left, last | the message of a pending chord ("(Ctrl+K) was pressed. Waiting for second key of chord..."), which stays until the chord is completed, aborted or forgotten after 5 s; "The key combination (Ctrl+K, X) is not a command." stays 10 s | none |
  | Selection (`status.editor.selection`) | right, 100.5 | `Ln 12, Col 5`, `Ln 12, Col 11 (6 selected)`, `2 selections`, `2 selections (11 characters selected)`; the column is the status bar column of §6.2 and a line break counts as the line ending of the file (two for CRLF) | `workbench.action.gotoLine` |
  | Indentation (`status.editor.indentation`) | right, 100.4 | `Spaces: 4` or `Tab Size: 4` (Emetos has one size for the tab and the indent) | the indentation picker |
  | Encoding (`status.editor.encoding`) | right, 100.3 | the short label ("UTF-8", "UTF-8 with BOM", "Windows 1252") | `workbench.action.editor.changeEncoding` |
  | End of line (`status.editor.eol`) | right, 100.2 | `LF` or `CRLF`; not for a read-only file | `workbench.action.editor.changeEOL` |
  | Language (`status.editor.mode`) | right, 100.1 | the name of the language ("Go", "Plain Text") | `workbench.action.editor.changeLanguageMode` |
  | Profile (`status.profile`) | right, 100 | `$(account) <name>` of the profile of the window, tooltip "Profile: <name>"; hidden for the default profile (step 8.6b, §10.12) | `workbench.profiles.actions.switchProfile` |
  | Notifications (`status.notifications`) | right, last | `$(bell)`; `$(bell-dot)` with an unread or a running notification; `$(bell-slash)` and `$(bell-slash-dot)` in do not disturb mode | `notifications.showList`, or `notifications.hideList` while the center is open |
  The items of the editor (all but the profile) show for the active text editor and are hidden when there is none. They follow the active editor, its cursors and selections (the selection listener of the view model), its edits, its indentation, and the pickers below. Unread means a notification that was added while the center was closed and that was not closed since; opening the center reads them all. The tooltips of the bell are "No Notifications", "No New Notifications", "1 New Notification", "N New Notifications" (with " (N in progress)" while a progress runs and neither the toasts nor the center show), "Hide Notifications" and, in do not disturb mode, "Do Not Disturb Mode is Enabled".
- **Pickers** (the quick input of step 6.17, `Controller.Pick`; `PickOptions.ActiveID` makes an item active when the picker opens). *Indentation* (`changeEditorIndentation`, no title, as VS Code's per-window command): "Select Action" with the headings "change view" and "convert file" over Indent Using Spaces, Indent Using Tabs, Change Tab Display Size, Detect Indentation from Content, Convert Indentation to Spaces, Convert Indentation to Tabs and Trim Trailing Whitespace; the first three ask for the tab size in "Select Tab Size for Current File" (1 to 8, marked "Configured Tab Size", "Default Tab Size" or "Current Tab Size") unless the command is given the size. *End of line* ("Select End of Line Sequence"): LF and CRLF, the current one active; the change is an edit of its own that can be undone. *Encoding* ("Select Action": "Reopen with Encoding" for a file and "Save with Encoding" for a document that can be saved, an untitled one having only the latter; "Save with Encoding" lists the encodings in "Select File Encoding to Save with", the current one active, makes the document save in the one chosen (the byte order mark follows: UTF-16 and "UTF-8 with BOM" have one) and saves it at once, an untitled one asking for its file first): "Reopen with Encoding" lists the encodings of §6.3 (the configured one first, then VS Code's order, without the ones that can only be saved in, with the encoding guessed from the file, a byte order mark or valid UTF-8, first as "Guessed from content"), the current one active; the file is read again with the encoding (`Document.Reload`: the text, the encoding, the byte order mark and the line ending are replaced, the history is cleared and the document counts as saved). A document with unsaved changes asks first, as VS Code does ("Do you want to revert the active text editor and reopen with a different encoding?", button "Discard Changes and Reopen"). *Language mode* ("Select Language Mode"): "Auto Detect" (when the document has text; it takes the language of the file name and first line, not VS Code's content-based detection), "Configure File Association for '.ext'..." and the languages by name with their ids ("Go (go) - Configured Language" for the current one) under the heading "languages (identifier)"; the command takes the name or id of a language as its argument and then sets it at once. The association picker lists the languages ("Current Association" on the one the name has now) and writes `files.associations`: `*.ext` for a name of the form name.ext, the whole name otherwise, to the workspace settings when the association is made there already and to the user settings otherwise; the open documents whose language was the one their name gave take the new association. Not built: "Configure 'x' language based settings...", the marketplace entry, the icons of languages in the picker, the items "Column Selection", "Tab Moves Focus", "OVR" and the file information of binary editors, "Current Problem" (`problems.showCurrentInStatus`), "Problems are turned off" (`problems.visibility`), the animation of `~spin`, keyboard navigation of the items (`workbench.action.focusStatusBar` is only a focus part), hiding single items, and the profile and remote items.

**Settings:** `workbench.sideBar.location` (left, right), `workbench.activityBar.location` (default, top, bottom, hidden; top and bottom are registered but the activity bar stays at the side of the side bar), `workbench.statusBar.visible`, `workbench.panel.defaultLocation` (left, bottom, top, right), `workbench.panel.opensMaximized` (always, never, preserve), `window.title`, `window.titleSeparator`.

**Context keys** (set in the scope of the window, named as VS Code's `contextkeys.ts` names them): `sideBarVisible`, `sideBarFocus`, `panelVisible`, `panelFocus`, `panelPosition`, `panelAlignment` (always `center`), `panelMaximized`, `mainEditorAreaVisible`, `editorAreaFocus`, `statusBarFocused`. Each part has a focus scope and a context key scope of its own below the window's (registered with `kit.FocusScopes`), for the views in it; the activity bar has no key of its own, as in VS Code.

**Dialogs.** The workbench holds the one `kit.DialogHost` of the window, over everything; while a dialog shows, the key dispatcher of the window runs no command and uses no key.

**Menus and the menu bar (step 6.7).** The menu bar is made from the **menu registry** (`command.MenuRegistry`, VS Code's `MenuRegistry` of `platform/actions/common/actions.ts`; it is in `core/command`, which §5.2 gives to the registries of commands, menus and views). A menu has an id (a string: VS Code's `MenuId`s `MenubarMainMenu`, `MenubarFileMenu`, `MenubarEditMenu`, `MenubarSelectionMenu`, `MenubarViewMenu`, `MenubarGoMenu`, `MenubarTerminalMenu`, `MenubarHelpMenu`, `MenubarPreferencesMenu`, `MenubarAppearanceMenu`, `MenubarRecentMenu`, `PanelPositionMenu`, `EditorContext`, `ExplorerContext` and `GlobalActivity`, the Manage menu) and holds items, each either a command or a submenu (another menu id) with an optional title (the command's when empty), a mnemonic title (`"&&Undo"`: `&&` marks the mnemonic as VS Code writes it), a toggled title, a `group` (`1_do`, `2_ccp`... as VS Code names them), an `order`, a `when` (the item is in the menu), a `precondition` (overrides the command's: the item stays, dimmed) and a `toggled` expression (the check mark). `MenuRegistry.Items(menu, ctx)` gives the entries as VS Code's `Menu.getActions` does: the items whose `when` holds, groups sorted by name (`navigation` first, no group last), items by order and then by title, one separator between two groups, no empty group, no empty submenu, no item whose command is not registered or that has no title. A menu can also have **providers** (`SetProvider`), functions asked each time the menu is built, whose items are sorted with the others: the recently opened files, for instance. `MenuRegistry.All()` lists the rows; a test checks that every row of `DefaultMenus` refers to a registered command and shows a title. The rows of the built-in menus are data in one file (`command/menus_default.go`), in VS Code's groups and orders: a step that adds a command VS Code puts in a menu adds its row there (rule 7 of the plan). `MnemonicMenuLabel` turns a title into a label of the menu widgets (`&&File` into `&File`, a single ampersand into `&&`).

`workbench.MenuSource` turns entries into `kit.MenuItem`s: labels with the mnemonics of the menu bar (the context menus show none), the key of the command (`keybinding.Resolver.LookupPrimaryKeybinding`, written in the label style of Linux: `Ctrl+Z`), the check mark, the dimmed look of a disabled item, submenus and separators. The menu bar is `BarMenus()`: the submenus of `MenubarMainMenu` that have items, so a menu with no command yet (File, Terminal and Help until their steps) is not in the bar, as in VS Code. Each menu is built when the user opens it (`kit.MenuBarMenu.Source`; `kit.MenuItem.Data` carries the arguments of the command back to `OnRun`, and `kit.MenuBarController.Open` opens a menu from outside), in the context of that moment (§11.2): the context keys of the window, the settings as the keys `config.<setting>` (`config.workbench.statusBar.visible`...) and the keys the window keeps for the editor it shows (`editorWordWrap`, `canToggleWordWrap`, `editorIsOpen`, `editorReadonly`), which the editor's own scope cannot give because the menu has the focus. Running an item runs its command through the registry with `command.SourceMenu` and the arguments of the item, in the second frame after the menu closed: the editor binds its commands while it has the focus, and a menu gives the focus back when it closes. A command that fails is logged. The **context menu of the editor** (`EditorContext`: Cut, Copy, Paste, a separator, Select All; Cut and Paste only while the editor is not read-only) opens on a right click, in a `kit.ContextMenuRegion` around the editor; the press of the secondary button gives the editor the focus and puts the cursor under the pointer unless a selection holds the place (VS Code's `contextmenu.ts`). Select All is not in VS Code's editor context menu (its simple editors have it); Emetos has it. Items for the commands VS Code puts in a context menu that Emetos does not have yet (Go to Definition, Format Document) arrive with those commands; the Command Palette row of the editor context menu is not added.

**Window title.** `window.title` (default `${dirty}${activeEditorShort}${separator}${rootName}${separator}${profileName}${separator}${appName}`) is expanded with VS Code's `template`: `activeEditorShort`, `activeEditorMedium`, `activeEditorLong`, `activeEditorLanguageId`, `activeFolderShort`, `activeFolderMedium`, `activeFolderLong`, `rootName`, `rootNameShort`, `rootPath`, `folderName`, `folderPath`, `dirty` (`● `), `appName` (Emetos), `profileName` (empty for the default profile), `focusedView`, `remoteName`, `activeEditorState` and `separator` (`window.titleSeparator`, ` - `, kept only between two values). The variables of a remote, a workspace file or a repository are empty. Paths in the home folder are written `~/...`. The title is set when the active editor, its dirty mark, the folder or the settings change; a title that comes out empty is `Emetos`.

### 10.2 Widget kit (`gui/kit`)

Glutter has no IDE-styled controls, so Emetos builds them on Glutter's `widgets` layer (D9):

- `Theme`: an inherited widget carrying the resolved workbench colors and fonts.
- `SplitView` (resizable sashes, min/max sizes, VS Code's layout priorities so the editor area absorbs window resizes, double-click to reset), `ScrollableElement` (VS Code-style overlay scrollbars, finished by step 6.2), `Tree` (on Glutter's `TreeSliver`, step 6.4), `List` (virtualized, step 6.4b; with the Tree it shares multi-select, type to find and drag and drop), `Tabs` (step 6.8, §10.3), `Button`, `IconButton`, `ActionBar`, `InputBox` (on Glutter's `EditableText`), `Toggle`, `Checkbox`, `Dropdown`, `Hover` (an overlay portal like `RawTooltip`'s, see `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`), `Menu` and `MenuBar` and `ContextMenu` (on `RawMenuAnchor`/`MenuController`), `Dialog` (in-window, modal), `ProgressBar`, `Badge`, `ListOverlay` (a simple list that a widget places where it wants it, used before quick input exists: `kit.ListOverlay` and its model `kit.ListModel`; see below).
- Icons: VS Code's Codicons font, bundled and loaded through `RunAppOpts.Fonts` (D14), with code points generated from the font package's `codicon.csv`.
- Fonts: the workbench uses the desktop's UI font (`UIFont()`), then `sans-serif` (D14).
- **`ListOverlay`** (`kit/listoverlay.go`, `listmodel.go`): rows of 22 px with an optional Codicon, a label and a dimmer description, in `editorWidget.background` (or the caller's), with a shadow. It takes the focus when it is shown (autofocus). Up and Down move the focus (no wrap) over the rows that are not disabled, PageUp and PageDown move it by the rows in view less one, Home and End go to the ends, Enter calls `OnSelect` with the index of the item and Escape calls `OnDismiss`; a click on a row chooses it, the wheel scrolls, the row under the pointer is in `list.hoverBackground` and the focused one in `list.activeSelectionBackground` with a `list.focusOutline`. With `Filterable`, typing shows the typed text in a row on top and keeps the rows whose label contains it (ignoring case), Backspace takes a character off. With `TypeAhead` (and no `Filterable`) typing a letter moves the focus to the next row that starts with it (going round, ignoring case); `Foreground` sets the color of the text of the rows (the dropdown's). A click outside, or the focus going elsewhere, calls `OnDismiss` (once); a `TapGroup` lets the widget that opened the list count its own regions as inside. At most `Rows` rows (10) are in view. The list does not position itself: the caller puts it in an `OverlayPortal`. Step 6.x replaces its uses with the quick input where VS Code has one.

- **`Button`** (`kit/button.go`; port of `button.ts` and `button.css`): a text button, 12 px text on a 16 px line, padding 4 px by 8 px, a 1 px border, radius 4, as wide as its content (`Expand` fills the width; `Small` is 11 px with 3 px by 6 px). Primary: `button.foreground`/`background` (`button.hoverBackground` under the pointer and while it has the focus), border `button.border`; secondary: `button.secondaryForeground`/`secondaryBackground`/`secondaryHoverBackground`/`secondaryBorder`. An optional leading Codicon (16 px, 4.8 px before the label). The focus outline is 1 px `focusBorder`, 2 px outside the button; a click gives it the focus, Enter, the numpad's Enter and Space press it, Escape gives the focus up and calls `OnEscape`. Disabled (or without `OnPressed`) it is at 40 %, ignores the pointer and the keyboard and does not take the focus. Not ported: the dropdown, the description button, the short label, markdown labels and the button bar.
- **`IconButton`** (`kit/iconbutton.go`, on the `actionItem` look it shares with the action bar): 22 px square, a 16 px Codicon, radius 5; `toolbar.hoverBackground` under the pointer, `toolbar.activeBackground` while pressed, `actionBar.toggledBackground` when `Checked`; disabled icons are at 40 % (the find widget's look); the focus outline is 1 px `focusBorder` inside the box; Enter and Space press it while it has the focus (a click does not take the focus, so the editor keeps it); `Tooltip` shows a `Hover`.
- **`InputBox`** (`kit/inputbox.go`, `kit/history.go`; port of `inputBox.ts` (`InputBox`, `HistoryInputBox`), `inputBox.css` and the keys of `contextScopedHistoryWidget.ts`): a box on `EditableText` in `input.background`, 25 px high (the find widget's), 13 px text, 4 px of padding at the sides, a 1 px border of radius 2 and a placeholder in `input.placeholderForeground` while it is empty; `Trailing` widgets (the find input's toggles) sit at its right edge. The border is `input.border`, `focusBorder` while the text has the focus, `inputValidation.errorBorder` with `Invalid`, and the border of the severity of the message while it has one. **Validation**: `Validate(text)` runs after each change (and in `InputBoxController.Validate`) and gives an `InputMessage{Content, Type}` (`ValidationInfo`, `ValidationWarning`, `ValidationError`) or nil; a box without `Validate` shows the `Message` its owner sets. The message shows in an overlay below the box while the box has the focus (above it when there is no room below), as wide as the box and 1 px over it, 12 px text on 17 px lines with 4.8 px (0.4em) of padding and a 1 px border, in `inputValidation.<severity>Background`/`Foreground`/`Border` (a severity whose foreground the theme leaves unset keeps `foreground`); a message without text only colors the border. **History**: with `History` (a `kit.History`: values oldest first, each once, at most 100, a position that starts past the last value and goes back there after every `Add`; port of `HistoryNavigator`) Up and Down (and Alt+Up and Alt+Down) show the previous and next value as `showPreviousValue`/`showNextValue` do (the typed text is first added to the history unless it is there; Up at the first value stays; Down past the last gives an empty box; the new text goes through `OnChanged`), Enter adds the text before `OnSubmit`. **Keys**: the box sees Enter (`OnSubmit`), Up and Down before the text does, through the key handler of its focus node (which it chains with the node's own and gives back on dispose); every other key goes to the text. **Multi-line** (`MultiLine`): Enter makes a line (Ctrl+Enter submits), long lines wrap and the box grows by 17 px a line from its 25 px up to `MaxHeight` (then the text scrolls, without a scrollbar: VS Code hides the textarea's own and the kit's overlay scrollbar is not attached to it); Up and Down go through the history only when the caret is on the first or last line (counted by the line breaks of the text, not the wrapped lines), or with Alt. `Disabled` shows the text in `disabledForeground`, takes the box out of the focus order and ignores the keys. An `InputBoxController` (`NewInputBoxController(text)`, owned by the caller, `Dispose`) holds the text controller and focus node of the box and is given through `InputBoxOpts.Controller` (the box takes them where `NewInputBox` is given none): `Focus`, `Blur`, `HasFocus`, `SelectAll`, `Select(start, end)` and `Selection` (byte offsets), `Value`, `SetValue` (reports `OnChanged` and validates, as VS Code's setter), `Validate`, `AddToHistory`, `ShowPreviousValue`, `ShowNextValue`. Not ported: the actions inside the box (use `Trailing`), the password type, the tooltip and the screen reader announcements.
- **`Toggle`** (`kit/toggle.go`; port of `toggle.ts` and `toggle.css`): a Codicon (16 px) in a 20 px box with a radius of 3 and a 1 px border: on it is `inputOption.activeForeground` over `inputOption.activeBackground` with an `inputOption.activeBorder` border, off it is `icon.foreground` and the pointer gives it `inputOption.hoverBackground`; while it has the focus the border is `focusBorder` (VS Code draws it dashed). Space and Enter flip it (`OnChanged(!Checked)`), a click flips it without taking the focus, so the input next to it keeps it. `Disabled` is at 40 % and ignores the pointer, the keyboard and the focus; a toggle without `OnChanged` ignores them too (not dimmed); `NotFocusable` keeps it out of the focus order (the toggles of the find widget); `Tooltip` shows a compact `Hover`.
- **`Checkbox`** (`kit/checkbox.go`; port of `Checkbox` in `toggle.ts` and `toggle.css`): an 18 px box (radius 3, 1 px border) in `checkbox.background`/`border` with a 16 px check mark in `checkbox.foreground` while checked, a label of 13 px text 9 px after it (the label toggles too; `LabelColor` overrides its color). `Selected` paints the box of a selected row (`checkbox.selectBackground`, `checkbox.selectBorder`), `Disabled` uses `checkbox.disabled.background` (also as the border) and `checkbox.disabled.foreground` and takes it out of the focus order. A click focuses it (the border becomes `focusBorder`) and flips it; Space and Enter flip it. Not ported: the tri-state checkbox.
- **`Dropdown`** (`kit/dropdown.go`; port of the field and keys of `selectBox.ts`/`selectBoxCustom.ts`/`selectBoxNative.ts` and `selectBox.css`): a field 22 px high, at least 100 px wide (`Width`, else the width it is given), radius 4, in `dropdown.background`/`foreground`/`border` (`focusBorder` while it has the focus or the list is open), 13 px text with 8 px before it, a chevron-down Codicon at the right and, with no selected option, the `Placeholder` in `descriptionForeground`. The list of `DropdownOption{Label, Description, Disabled}` is a `ListOverlay` (`Rows` rows, 10 by default, as wide as the field, in `dropdown.listBackground` or `dropdown.background` and `dropdown.foreground`, the selected option focused) in an overlay below the field, above it where it does not fit below. Closed: a click, Enter, Space, Up and Down open it; typing a letter selects the next option that starts with it (going round, ignoring case, skipping disabled ones) and calls `OnChanged`. Open: Up, Down, PageUp, PageDown, Home and End move the focus (disabled options are skipped), typing a letter moves it to the next option that starts with it, Enter or a click chooses (`OnChanged(index)` unless it is the selected one; the dropdown never changes its own `Selected`), Escape, a click on the field or outside, and the focus going elsewhere close the list, and Escape and a choice give the focus back to the field. `Disabled` is at 40 %. Not ported: separators, the details pane and the native select.
- **`ProgressBar`** (`kit/progressbar.go`; port of `progressbar.ts` and `progressbar.css`): a line 2 px high across the width it is given in `progressBar.background`. **Infinite** (`Infinite`): a bit of 2 % of the width runs along the bar, a trip of 4 s, linear, repeating (`InfiniteProgressBit(phase)` gives its place: `translateX` 0 to 25 to 49 bit widths and `scaleX` 1 to 3 to 1 at 0, 50 % and 100 % of the trip), and after 10 s it moves in steps of 1 % of the trip (VS Code's `infinite-long-running`); the animation runs while the bar is infinite only and is driven by the frame clock, so it is tested with the fake clock. **Discrete** (`Total`, `Worked`; `DiscreteProgress`): the bar fills from the left to `Worked / Total` (`Total` 100 when it is not above 0, at most the whole bar, nothing while `Worked` is not above 0), growing to a new value in 100 ms, linear. Not ported: the fade out when done (the caller stops showing the bar) and the screen reader signal.
- **`Badge`** (`kit/badge.go`; port of `countBadge.ts` and `countBadge.css`): a count (`Format` with `{0}`, "{0}" by default) in 11 px text on an 11 px line, padding 3 px by 5 px, radius 11, at least 18 px by 18 px, text centered, in `badge.background` and `badge.foreground`; `Tooltip` (with `{0}`) shows a compact `Hover`. Not ported: the `long` variant (nothing in `countBadge.ts` sets it) and the border (`contrastBorder`, which is unset in the themes that are not high contrast).
- **`Dialog`**, **`DialogHost`**, **`DialogController`** (`kit/dialog.go`; port of `dialog.ts` and `dialog.css`): see §10.7.
- **`ActionBar`** (`kit/actionbar.go`; port of `actionbar.ts`, `actionViewItems.ts` and `actionbar.css`): a row (`ActionsHorizontal`) or column (`ActionsVertical`) of `Action`s: icon actions (the look of the icon button), text actions (11 px, 3 px padding, `foreground`, `disabledForeground` when disabled) and separators (1 px by 16 px, margin 5 px by 4 px, in `disabledForeground`; a line across a column). A checked action is shown toggled (VS Code needs `highlightToggledItems`). `OnRun(action)` is called on a click, or on Enter and Space on release, and never for a disabled action or a separator. One item is in the tab order (the one that had the focus last, else the first); Left and Right (Up and Down when vertical) move the focus, wrapping unless `PreventLoopNavigation`, skipping separators and, with `FocusOnlyEnabledItems`, disabled items; Home and End go to the ends; Escape gives the focus up and calls `OnEscape`, and without `OnEscape` goes on to the widgets above. Moving the focus with the keys shows the tooltip of the new item at once. An `ActionBarController` moves the focus from outside. `PrepareActions` cleans up separators as VS Code's `prepareActions`. Not ported: view items for dropdowns and selects, the action runner, action contexts and `setFocusable`.
- **`Hover`** (`kit/hover.go`; port of `hover.ts`, `hoverWidget.ts` and the `WorkbenchHoverDelegate`): wraps a target widget and shows a popup in the nearest overlay: text (13 px, line height 19 px, padding 4 px by 8 px; `Compact`, the tooltip look, is 12 px with 2 px by 8 px) or a widget from `Content`, in `editorHoverWidget.background`/`foreground`/`border` (1 px, radius 5) with a `widget.shadow` shadow, at most 700 px wide. It shows when the pointer has rested on the target for the delay (500 ms, `workbench.hover.delay` on Linux), or at once when the hover is asked for by a `HoverController`, or, in a `HoverDelegate` made with `Instant`, when another hover of the group hid less than 200 ms ago (an action bar's items share one such delegate); a group shows one hover at a time. It hides when the pointer leaves or is pressed, and on any key that is not a modifier (Escape among them; the key goes on to the focused widget), and a hover still waiting for its delay is cancelled the same way. `PlaceHover` positions it: `HoverBelow` (the default) or `HoverAbove` with its left edge on the target's, `HoverRight` or `HoverLeft` with its top edge on the target's; a side that does not fit flips (below and above exchange; right and left exchange, else below) and the popup is kept inside the window. Not ported: markdown content, the pointer arrow, links and actions in a hover, hovering into the popup, the fade-in and the sticky (locked) hover.
- **`SplitModel`** (`kit/splitmodel.go`; port of the layout of `splitview.ts`, tested without widgets): views in a line, each a `SplitItem` with a size, `MinSize` (0), `MaxSize` (0 is none), a `LayoutPriority` (`LayoutNormal`, `LayoutLow`, `LayoutHigh`), `Snap`, `PreferredSize` (0 is none) and `Hidden`. `Layout(size)` fits the views to a size: with `ProportionalLayout` each keeps its share, else the change goes to the views by priority (`resize`: High first, Low last, each side of the last view in turn) and `distributeEmptySpace` fills what is left. `SashStart`/`SashChange`/`SashEnd` drag the sash after a view: the views before it, nearest first, take the change and the views after it the opposite, within their limits (`resize`'s `minDelta`/`maxDelta`), a view that snaps hides when the drag passes half its minimum size and shows again when dragged back, and with Alt held the view after the sash (before it, for the last sash) is resized on both sides alike (`InverseAltBehavior` swaps that). `SashStates()` gives each sash `SashEnabled`, `SashDisabled`, `SashAtMinimum` or `SashAtMaximum` (`updateSashEnablement`). `ResizeView`, `SetViewVisible` (a hidden view keeps its size to come back with, its space goes to the others by priority), `DistributeViewSizes`, `UpdateView` (new limits: the neighbours absorb the change), `AddView`/`RemoveView` with a `Sizing` (`SizeOf`, `SizingDistribute`, `SizingSplit`, `SizingAuto`, `SizingInvisible`) and `ResetSash` (a double click: the view before the sash and the view after it get their `PreferredSize`; with none, the sizes are distributed; nothing happens at the edge of a hidden view that snaps). The cases of VS Code's `splitview.test.ts` that need no DOM are ported (§15.1).
- **`Sash`** (`kit/sash.go`; port of `sash.ts` and `sash.css`): the line between two views, 4 px across (`DefaultSashSize`), vertical (`SashVertical`, dragged sideways) or horizontal. It shows nothing until the pointer has rested on it for 300 ms (`sash.hoverBorder` over its 4 px, `DefaultSashHoverDelay`), or at once while it is pressed; the cursor is `ew-resize`/`ns-resize` (`ResizeLeftRight`/`ResizeUpDown`), `e-resize`/`s-resize` at the minimum (`ResizeRight`/`ResizeDown`) and `w-resize`/`n-resize` at the maximum (`ResizeLeft`/`ResizeUp`); a disabled sash ignores the pointer. `OnDidStart`, `OnDidChange` (window coordinates and whether Alt is held) and `OnDidEnd` report a drag with the primary button; a second press within 400 ms and 5 px of the first reports `OnDidReset` after its release. Not ported: corner (orthogonal) sashes, linked sashes, touch gestures, the 100 ms fade of the highlight. VS Code's sash has no keyboard resize.
- **`SplitView`** (`kit/splitview.go`): `NewSplitView(panes, opts)` puts `SplitPane`s (a child with `Size`, `MinSize`, `MaxSize`, `Priority`, `Snap`, `PreferredSize`, `Hidden`) side by side (`SplitHorizontal`) or stacked (`SplitVertical`, VS Code's default) with a `Sash` of 4 px centered on each boundary (the sash is over the panes, so the panes keep their whole size). It lays the model out for the size it gets, so a window resize gives the change to the panes by priority: the pane with `LayoutHigh` takes it (the editor area, §10.1), `LayoutLow` panes keep their size longest; unlike VS Code, where the split view is proportional unless told not to, `ProportionalLayout` is off by default. A drag moves the sash with the pointer and stops where the limits stop it (the cursor stays the resize cursor while the pointer outruns the sash); a double click resets the neighbouring panes to their `PreferredSize` (`OnDidSashReset` replaces that). A pane is a `Positioned` in a stack, in an `Offstage` so that a hidden pane keeps its state; a new `SplitView` with another `Hidden` shows or hides it, with other limits updates them, and with another number of panes starts over from the sizes of the panes. `OnDidChange(sizes)` reports the sizes (0 for a hidden pane) after the frame in which they changed: once at the end of a drag, never during one. `SplitViewController` reads and sets them from outside: `Sizes`, `SetSize`, `SetVisible`, `IsVisible`, `CachedVisibleSize`, `Distribute`, `IsExpanded`, `ContentSize`. `SeparatorBorder` draws a 1 px line at the start of every pane but the first (`ISplitViewStyles.separatorBorder`). A size that is not positive (no size yet, or a minimized window) lays nothing out: the panes keep the sizes they were given, but not below their least, until a real size comes. When the panes' minimum sizes add up to more than the split view, it scrolls along its axis with an overlay scrollbar (`Scrollbar`). Not ported: orthogonal sashes, `moveView`/`swapViews`, adding and removing panes while it lives, scroll events.
- **`ScrollableElement`** (`kit/scrollable_element.go`, `kit/scrollmodel.go`; port of the `scrollbar/` folder of VS Code): overlay scrollbars over a child, never taking room from it. `NewScrollableElement(child, opts)` scrolls in one of three ways: with `Model` (a `ScrollModel`: the editor), with `Controller`/`HorizontalController` (Glutter scroll views of the child that use those controllers, such as a list or a tree: `ScrollControllerModel` reads their sizes and moves them; the element switches off Glutter's own scrollbars and overscroll glow below it, and learns of a change of size through `ScrollMetricsNotification`), or with neither, when the element scrolls any child itself, in the `Axes` (`ScrollAxisVertical`, `ScrollAxisHorizontal`, `ScrollAxisBoth`). Scrollbars: vertical and horizontal with `ScrollbarAuto` (shown when the content is longer than the viewport and the pointer is over the element or the content scrolled; hidden 500 ms after, 100 ms in and 800 ms out), `ScrollbarVisible` or `ScrollbarHidden` (it still scrolls); 10 px by default (`DefaultScrollbarSize`, the editor passes 14 and 12), slider of at least 20 px in `scrollbarSlider.background` (`hoverBackground` under the pointer, `activeBackground` while dragged), a track in `scrollbar.background` (transparent in the bundled themes), a click on the track jumps the slider there (`ScrollByPage` pages), a drag goes on outside the bar, and `UseShadows` draws a 4 px `scrollbar.shadow` gradient at the top and the left of content that is scrolled (off by default, as in VS Code's lists). Wheel (on by default when the element scrolls a child itself, off with a `Model` or controllers, whose child scrolls itself; `HandleMouseWheel`): one notch (53 px in Glutter) is 50 px times `MouseWheelScrollSensitivity` (1), Alt multiplies by `FastScrollSensitivity` (5), only the axis that moves more scrolls (`NoScrollPredominantAxis`), Shift scrolls sideways (`ScrollYToX` always, `FlipAxes` swaps), a step is rounded away from zero, and a mouse wheel scrolls smoothly over 125 ms (ease-out cubic; `NoSmoothScroll`); a touchpad scrolls with the fingers, at once. The element takes the wheel from what is below it only when it moves (`AlwaysConsumeMouseWheel` always, `ConsumeMouseWheelIfScrollbarIsNeeded` while a bar is needed), so a list at its end passes the wheel on. Not ported: arrows, inertial scrolling, the corner between the bars, the reset of a drag that moves far from the bar (Windows only), keyboard focus.
- **`Tree`** (`kit/tree.go`, `kit/treemodel.go`; port of the row, mouse and key rules of `abstractTree.ts`, `tree.css`, `listWidget.ts` and the `list.*` commands of `listCommands.ts`): `NewTree(model, opts)` shows a `TreeModel`. The model is plain Go, tested without a window: `SetItems` gives the nodes (`TreeItem`: a unique non-empty `Key` such as a path, the caller's `Data`, `Children`, `HasChildren` for a folder that is read and empty, `Lazy` for a folder that is not read; an empty or repeated key is refused with `TreeItemKeyEmpty` or `TreeItemKeyDuplicated`), `Rows()` are the nodes below the expanded ones, and the expansion state is kept **by key**, so giving the items again after a folder was read again, or restoring `ExpandedKeys` of an earlier session (`SetExpandedKeys`), finds every node back as it was; the focused row and the selected node are kept by key too (a focus whose node is gone moves to the nearest node above it that is shown; a selection of a node that is gone is dropped). A lazy node shows a chevron; the first expansion calls `TreeModelOpts.LoadChildren(key, data)` once (also for a lazy node that a restored state expands while it is shown), and until `SetChildren(key, items)` answers (or `FailLoad` collapses the node, to try again) the node shows the loading icon and one placeholder row below it ("Loading...", `LoadingLabel`; it cannot be focused, selected or clicked; a loader that answers at once never shows it). `ExpandAll`, `CollapseAll`, `Reveal(key)` (expands the nodes above it), `Toggle(key, recursive)`; the model is used on the UI thread. **Rows** are 22 px high (`TreeModelOpts.RowHeight`) and indented by 8 px plus 8 px per level; the twistie is a 16 px Codicon (chevron right, chevron down, the loading icon, nothing for a leaf) in a 22 px box (16 px and 6 px of padding) with the chevron moved 3 px to the right, then the contents, which the caller's `Render(bc, node, state)` builds (an icon and a label; the default is the `Data` as one line of 13 px text) in the foreground the `TreeRowState` names. **Colors**: the selected row is in `list.activeSelectionBackground`/`Foreground` while the tree has the keyboard focus and in `list.inactiveSelectionBackground`/`Foreground` otherwise; the focused row of an active tree is in `list.focusBackground`/`Foreground` (unset in the bundled themes) with a 1 px inside outline of `list.focusOutline` (`list.focusAndSelectionOutline` when it is selected too and the theme sets it), and in `list.inactiveFocusBackground` with `list.inactiveFocusOutline` (unset) in a tree without the focus; the row under the pointer, if neither selected nor focused, is in `list.hoverBackground`; a color a theme leaves unset leaves the row as it is. **Indent guides** (`TreeOpts.IndentGuides`): a 1 px line as high as the row at x = 16 px plus 8 px per ancestor, in `tree.inactiveIndentGuidesStroke` (shown while the pointer is over the tree with `TreeGuidesOnHover`, VS Code's default, or always with `TreeGuidesAlways`) and, for the ancestor of the focused row and of the selected node (the node itself when it is expanded and has children), in `tree.indentGuidesStroke` whenever guides are on; `TreeGuidesNone` shows none. **Mouse**: pressing a row focuses it and gives the tree the keyboard focus; a click on the twistie of an expandable node toggles it and nothing else (Alt toggles the whole subtree); a click elsewhere on the row selects it (`OnSelect`) and, with `ToggleOnClick` (default on; VS Code's `expandOnlyOnTwistieClick` inverted), toggles an expandable node; a second press within 400 ms and 5 px on the same row is a double click, which calls `OnOpen` and, with `ToggleOnClick` off, toggles the node (with it on, the first click has toggled it already and the second does not toggle again, unlike VS Code); a press of the secondary button only focuses the row. **Keys** (when the tree has the focus; a tree that takes the focus with no focused row focuses the selected row or the first): Down and Up move the focus (from no focus both go to the first row; they stop at the ends and skip the placeholder), Home and End go to the ends, PageDown focuses the last row completely in view, or scrolls a page and focuses the last row in view when it is focused already (`focusNextPage`), PageUp the same upwards, Right expands a collapsed node or moves to the first child of an expanded one (not used on a leaf, so it goes on to the widgets above), Left collapses an expanded node or moves to the parent (not used on a root that is collapsed or a leaf), Enter toggles an expandable node (with `ToggleOnClick`) and selects the node (`OnSelect` and `OnOpen`), Space toggles an expandable node and selects a leaf (`OnSelect`, not `OnOpen`), Ctrl+Left collapses everything, Escape reduces several selected nodes to the focused one, then clears the selection, then the focus (not used when there is none); the keys of a multiple selection (Shift+Up, Shift+Down, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+Space) and the find are in the next bullet; other keys with Shift, Ctrl, Alt or Meta, and the others, are not used. Moving the focus scrolls the least that shows the row. **Scrolling and the controller**: the rows are a `TreeSliver` in a `CustomScrollView` inside a `ScrollableElement` (overlay scrollbars, wheel by Glutter's own physics); `TreeController` gives `Reveal(key)` (expands above and scrolls after the next frame), `ScrollTo`, `ScrollOffset`, `ViewportHeight`, `Focus` and `HasFocus`. **Glutter's `TreeSliver` limits**, and what the tree does about them (`docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`): nodes cannot be removed and expansion lives in the nodes, so the model owns the state and every change makes the nodes again, with children only for the expanded part; a node is toggled only if it has children, so a node that is not read has the placeholder as its only child; contents are looked up with `reflect.DeepEqual`, so a node holds only its key; the toggle animation is off (`AnimationStyleNoAnimation`) and the indentation is none (the rows indent themselves). Not ported: sticky scroll, compressed folders, horizontal scrolling of wide rows, the spinning of the loading icon, `list.hoverForeground`, accessibility. Ported tests: none of `abstractTree.test.ts`/`objectTreeModel.test.ts`/`asyncDataTree.test.ts` apply as they are (they need VS Code's model classes and DOM); the cases of the keys, paging and lazy loading are written from the code of the commands.
- **Multi-select, find and drag and drop of `List` and `Tree`** (step 6.4b; `kit/listselection.go`, `kit/listnav.go`, `kit/listfind.go`, `kit/listwidget.go`, `kit/listdnd.go`; port of `MouseController`, `DefaultMultipleSelectionController` and `KeyboardController` of `listWidget.ts`, of the `list.*` commands of `listCommands.ts`, of `FindFilter`/`FindController`/`FindWidget` of `abstractTree.ts` and of `ListView`'s drag and drop). The model part is shared: `ListSelectionModel` (the selection by key, in the order it was made, the primary key = the one selected last, the anchor; `Press`, `SelectRange`, `ExpandTo`, `SelectAll`, `Toggle`, `Prune`, tested over a `RowSource`) and a navigation core (`rowNav`: row positions for fixed or different heights, the view, focus, paging, the keys, the find) that `TreeModel` and `ListViewModel` embed. **Multi-select** is opt-in (`TreeOpts`/`ListOpts.MultiSelect`, `TreeModelOpts`/`ListViewModelOpts.MultiSelect`; without it modifiers are ignored and only one row can be selected): Ctrl+click toggles the row and makes it the anchor; Shift+click selects the rows from the anchor (the focused row when there is no anchor) to the clicked row, replacing the run of selected rows that holds the anchor and keeping other blocks, as VS Code's; Ctrl+Shift+click adds that range to the selection (VS Code's default controller treats it as Shift+click); a click with Ctrl or Shift only changes selection and focus, it never toggles a tree node (also not on the twistie); a plain click, Enter and the arrow keys make the clicked or focused row the anchor; Shift+Down and Shift+Up move the focus and extend the selection (`list.expandSelectionDown/Up`: onto a selected row the row left is deselected, else the new row is added; the anchor stays); Ctrl+A selects every row that is shown (a list's `list.selectAll`; a tree selects all shown nodes too, not VS Code's scope of the focused node's siblings) and clears the anchor; Ctrl+Space and Ctrl+Shift+Enter toggle the focused row (`list.toggleSelection`); Escape reduces several selected rows to the focused row (`SelectionNavigation` off clears them, VS Code's default of the explorer), then clears the selection, then the focus. The selection is kept **by key**: a tree keeps nodes that a collapsed folder hides (they are selected, hidden; ranges and Ctrl+A count only the rows shown), `SetItems` and `SetChildren` drop the keys that are gone and a lost anchor. `SelectedKeys`, `SelectedIndexes` (list), `IsSelected`, `SetSelection`, `Selected` (the primary key); `OnSelectionChange` (`func(keys []string)` of a tree, `func(keys []string, indexes []int)` of a list) is called for the changes the user makes, never for the program's. **Find** (type to highlight or filter): a printable letter typed into the list or tree (no Ctrl, Alt or Meta; `TypeToFind` off turns it off) opens the **find box** above the rows and starts the pattern; the list keeps the keyboard focus and the box shows the pattern (an `InputBox` that does not take the focus, as `ListOverlay`'s filter), so Backspace edits it, Space is a letter while typing, Enter ends the typing (and selects/opens the focused row; Space and the other keys are the list's again, a letter types again), a click on a row ends it, Escape closes the box and forgets the pattern. The box is 200 px at most, 6 px from the left, 3 px padding, `listFilterWidget.background`, a 1 px outline (`listFilterWidget.outline`, `listFilterWidget.noMatchesOutline` when nothing matches) with a `listFilterWidget.shadow`, square above and round (6 px) below; it holds the input ("Type to search" or "Type to filter"), a toggle for the filter mode (Codicon list-filter), a toggle for the fuzzy match (search-fuzzy) and a close button, and the message "No results found." under them when nothing matches. It takes room above the rows (it does not cover them). Modes (`FindModeHighlight`, the default, and `FindModeFilter`; `TreeModelOpts`/`ListViewModelOpts.FindMode`) and match types (`FindMatchFuzzy`, a case-insensitive subsequence, the default, and `FindMatchContiguous`, a case-insensitive substring; a fuzzy pattern that is part of the label matches that part) apply to the label of a row (`TreeItem.Label`, `ListViewItem.Label`; the item's `Data` when that is a string). Highlight keeps every row, marks the matching parts of labels (`NewHighlightedLabel`: bold in `list.highlightForeground`, `list.focusHighlightForeground` in a focused or selected row of an active list) and, while more than one row matches, the keyboard moves the focus between the matches only (`FindController.shouldAllowFocus`; a click can focus any row); typing or changing the pattern focuses the first match from the focused row on, wrapping. Filter shows only the rows that match, and in a tree the nodes above a match, shown expanded without changing the expansion state (collapsing such a node does nothing until the find closes; nodes not read are not read for a find); in the filter mode the matching parts also have `list.filterMatchBackground` behind them. `FindState`, `OpenFind`, `CloseFind`, `SetFindPattern`, `SetFindMode`, `SetFindMatchType`, `FindMatch(key)` are on the models. Not ported: history of patterns, a find box that takes the keyboard focus (paste, caret), `list.find` and the toggle commands without a key, `list.filterMatchBorder` (registered, not painted). **Drag and drop** (`TreeOpts`/`ListOpts.DragAndDrop`, a `DragAndDrop` with `CanDrag(keys)`, `DragLabel(keys)`, `CanDrop(target, position, keys) DropEffect` (`DropEffectNone`, `Move`, `Copy`) and `OnDrop(target, position, keys)`, `AutoExpandDelay`): the items are the keys of the selected rows when the pressed row is selected, else the pressed row; a drag starts after the pointer moved 4 px with the primary button down (a smaller move is a click; after a drag no click follows); while it goes on a drag image (the label: `DragLabel`, the row's label, "N items" for several; 12 px text, 1 px by 7 px padding, radius 10, `list.activeSelectionBackground` and `list.focusOutline`) follows the pointer; the row under the pointer is the target and its sector says the position, as `DropPositionAt`: the top quarter is `DropBefore`, the bottom quarter `DropAfter` and the middle half `DropInside`; the empty part below the rows is the root (target `""`, `DropInside`); `CanDrop` is asked every time the target changes and a refusing target shows nothing and drops nothing; an accepted `DropInside` has the row in `list.dropBackground` (the whole view for the root), `DropBefore` and `DropAfter` a 1 px line across the row's top or bottom in `list.dropBetweenBackground`; a tree row that is a collapsed folder with an accepted `DropInside` expands after the drag rests on it for 500 ms (`DefaultAutoExpandDelay`), and moving away stops the timer; within 35 px of the top or bottom of the view it scrolls by `floor(0.3 * distance into the strip)`, at most 14 px, every 16 ms (`animateDragAndDropScrollTop`), and the target follows the content; Escape cancels the drag (nothing is dropped when the pointer is released); releasing over an accepted target calls `OnDrop`. The rows of a drag in progress keep their selection. Glutter's `Draggable` is not used: it starts a mouse drag after 1 px (a shaky click would become a drag), cannot be cancelled from the keyboard and `DragTarget` reports no position while the pointer rests; the drag is built on the pointer events of a `Listener` (`docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`). Not ported: drags to other windows or applications and drops from the file manager (the explorer adds those with Glutter's `DragTarget` of `ExternalDragData`, step 6.15), drop effect cursors.
- **`List`** (`kit/list.go`, `kit/listviewmodel.go`; port of `listWidget.ts`, `listView.ts` and `list.css`): `NewList(model, opts)` shows a `ListViewModel` in a `SliverFixedExtentList` (or, with `HeightOf`, a `SliverVariedExtentList`) inside a `CustomScrollView` with the overlay scrollbars of `ScrollableElement`, so only the rows in view and 250 px around them are built. `SetItems` gives the `ListViewItem`s (a unique non-empty `Key`, the caller's `Data`, a `Label` for the find; an empty or repeated key is refused with `ListItemKeyEmpty` or `ListItemKeyDuplicated`), `Rows()` are the items that are shown (all, or the matches while the find filters), the focus and the selection are kept by key. Rows are 22 px high (`ListViewModelOpts.RowHeight`) or as high as `HeightOf(index)` says (index among the rows shown), padded 8 px before the contents, which the caller's `Render(bc, row, state)` builds in the foreground the `RowState` names (the default is the label in one line of 13 px text with the matches of a find highlighted; `RowState` is the type `TreeRowState` also is). The colors, the focus outline, the hover and the double click (400 ms, 5 px; `OnOpen`) are those of the tree's rows. A click focuses and selects the row (`OnSelect`; Ctrl, Shift as above with `MultiSelect`). Keys, when the list has the focus (a list that takes the focus with no focused row focuses the selected row or the first): Up, Down, Home, End, PageUp and PageDown as the tree's (no wrap; the page keys count the heights of the rows), Enter selects the focused row and opens it (`OnSelect`, `OnOpen`), Space selects it (`OnSelect`), Escape as above, and the keys of the previous bullet. `ListController` gives `Reveal(key)` (scrolls after the next frame), `ScrollTo`, `ScrollOffset`, `ViewportHeight`, `Focus` and `HasFocus`. `ListView`'s type navigation by the start of a label is replaced by the find. Ported tests: none of `listWidget.test.ts` applies as it is (it needs the DOM); the cases of the clicks, the keys, the ranges and the find are written from the code of `MouseController` and the commands.
- **`Menu`** (`kit/menu.go`, `kit/menumodel.go`; port of `menu.ts`): the panel of a menu, 13 px text, items 24 px high with 4 px of margin at each side and a radius of 6, 4 px of padding above and below, at least 160 px wide (as wide as the widest row), a radius of 8, a 1 px `menu.border` (none where the theme sets no border) and a 12 px `widget.shadow` shadow, on `menu.background`. Items (`MenuItem`) are actions (a label with its mnemonic marked as VS Code writes it, `&File`, `&&` for a literal ampersand and `文件(&F)`; an optional keybinding label, a string the caller computes from the keybinding service, at the right edge in 70 % of the text color; a check mark in a 26 px column at the left; disabled items in `disabledForeground`, their keybinding at 40 %), separators (a 1 px line in `menu.separatorBackground` with 5 px above and below; separators at the ends and repeated ones are dropped, `PrepareMenuItems`) and submenus (a chevron at the right; a submenu without items shows a disabled "(empty)"; a submenu is never disabled, as in VS Code). The focused item is in `menu.selectionForeground` over `menu.selectionBackground` with a `menu.selectionBorder` outline when the theme has one. Label and keybinding have 26 px (2 em) of padding at each side. The keyboard (the menu takes it with `Autofocus`): Up and Down move the focus over the items that can take it, wrapping, from no focus Down gives the first and Up the last; Home and PageUp go to the first, End and PageDown to the last (the Linux behavior of VS Code); Enter, the numpad's Enter and Space run the focused item or open its submenu with the first item focused; Right opens a submenu; Left and Escape close one and give its item the focus back; Escape in the first level calls `OnCancel`; with mnemonics on (`EnableMnemonics`; the mnemonic letter is underlined) the letter runs the item that has it (a submenu opens) or, if several share it, focuses the next of them each time; Tab does nothing. The pointer: moving over an item focuses it (only a pointer that moves does, not one that rests under a menu that has just appeared), a disabled item or a separator takes the highlight off, the pointer leaving the menu does too unless a submenu is open, a click runs the item (a click on a disabled item or a separator does nothing), the pointer resting on a submenu item opens its submenu after 250 ms and the submenu closes 750 ms after the pointer went to another item of its parent (a submenu opened by the pointer does not select an item). The submenus are `RawMenuAnchor`s inside the row of their item, one group for the whole tree (the menu makes a `RawMenuAnchorGroup` of its own when it is not inside a context menu or a menu bar), placed by `PlaceSubmenu` (right of the item, left without room, overlapping it by 10 px without room on either side; vertically level with the item, or ending at its bottom edge without room below). A menu that is higher than `MaxHeight` (the window less 35 px) scrolls (7 px scrollbar, the keyboard scrolls the focused item into view). `OnRun` gets the item (after its own `OnRun`), also from submenus; the code that shows the menu hides it there. A `MenuHandle` focuses the menu from outside and reports the focused path. Not ported: the alternative actions of Alt and Shift, touch gestures, the frosted glass and the opening animation of the modern UI. Ported tests: the mnemonic cases of `menubar.test.ts`, the stationary pointer cases of `menu.test.ts`, `layout.test.ts`; the others need VS Code's DOM or `ModifierKeyEmitter`.
- **`ContextMenu`** (`kit/contextmenu.go`): the host of a context menu, `NewContextMenu(child, opts)` with a `ContextMenuController` (`Show(position, items)`, `Hide`, `IsShowing`; the position is in window coordinates, calling `Show` with a menu open moves it), and `NewContextMenuRegion(child, items func() []MenuItem, opts)`, which opens it at the pointer on a click of the secondary button (the items are asked for at that moment; no items, no menu). The menu is in the root overlay, placed by `PlaceContextMenu`: the pointer is a 2 px block, the menu opens to its right and below it, flips above it without room below, flips left of it without room to the right, and is pushed against the window's edge when it fits on neither side (VS Code's `layout2d`; `Layout` is its `layout`). It takes the keyboard focus and gives it back when it closes; Escape, a click outside (which goes on to what is under it), the window changing size and an item running close it, `OnHide` is called, `OnRun` after the menu is hidden. By default no item is focused (`SelectFirst` focuses the first) and mnemonics are off (`EnableMnemonics`). A region also opens its menu for the menu key and Shift+F10 while a widget in its child has the focus, at that widget's bottom left corner (an editor that knows its cursor calls `Show` itself).
- **Drag and drop of tabs** (step 6.10; `kit/tabdrag.go`, `kit/bounds.go`; port of the drag and drop of `multiEditorTabsControl.ts`, see §10.3): `Tabs` takes `TabsOpts.OnDragStart`, `OnDragMove` and `OnDragEnd` (positions in window coordinates; a tab is dragged by the primary button after 4 px, Escape and a cancelled pointer end the drag with `dropped` false; the press activates the tab as before), `OnContextMenu` (a press of the secondary button), `Feedback` (a `TabDropFeedback`: the place of a 2 px line in `tab.dragAndDropBorder` before a tab, or after the last one, which the strip draws and updates without building the others again) and `Controller` (a `TabsController`: `Bounds`, `TabRect(i)` in window coordinates wherever the strip is scrolled to, `ScrollBy`, `Scrolled`, `Reveal(i)`; `RevealDelta` and `TabEdgeStep` are the pure rules). `Bounds` and `NewBoundsProbe(child, &bounds)` tell code outside a widget where it is in the window; `ScrollableElementOpts.Handle` (a `ScrollHandle`: `Position`, `ScrollTo`, `ScrollBy`) scrolls an element from outside. The window decides what a drop does (§10.3).
- **`MenuBar`** (`kit/menubar.go`; port of `menubar.ts`, `menubar.css` and `menubarControl.css`): titles of 13 px text, 22 px high with 8 px of padding and a radius of 5, in a bar 30 px high (the title bar's; `Height`), in `titleBar.activeForeground`; the title that is open, focused or under the pointer (while the bar is not focused) is in `menubar.selectionForeground` over `menubar.selectionBackground`, with a `menubar.selectionBorder` outline when the theme has one (solid, VS Code draws a dashed line for the pointer). A menu opens under its title (left edges aligned, pushed in where the window has no room). The state machine (`menuBarModel`) has VS Code's four states: hidden, visible, focused and open. The pointer: the press of the primary button on a title opens its menu and the release is ignored; a press on the title of the open menu closes it (on the release); with a menu open, entering another title opens that menu (no item selected); with the bar focused and no menu open, entering a title focuses it; a click outside the bar and its menus gives the focus up. The keyboard: a tap of Alt (pressed and released with no other key or pointer press and no other modifier between) focuses the first title and shows the mnemonics; a second tap, or Alt while the bar is focused, gives the focus up; Left and Right move between the titles (wrapping), Down and Enter open the focused menu with its first item focused, a mnemonic letter opens its menu, Escape gives the focus up; with a menu open, Left and Right (not in a submenu) switch to the previous or next menu, Escape closes the menu and leaves its title focused; Alt with a mnemonic letter opens that menu from anywhere (it needs no focus; the key still reaches the focused widget, Glutter does not let a key handler consume it). Alt held down underlines the mnemonics of the titles; they stay underlined while the bar is in use, and the menus have mnemonics only when they were opened by the keyboard or a mnemonic. When the bar gives the focus up, it goes back to the widget that had it. `MenuBarVisibility` (`window.menuBarVisibility`, registered in `core/settings`): `MenuBarClassic` shows the bar except in full screen (`FullScreen`), `MenuBarVisible` always, `MenuBarToggle` hides it until Alt focuses it and hides it again when it gives the focus up, `MenuBarHidden` never shows it for Alt (`compact` is not offered); a mnemonic with Alt opens its menu in the last two as in VS Code, showing the bar until the menu is left. A hidden bar is an `Offstage` (it takes no room, keeps its state). `DisableMnemonics` (`window.enableMenuBarMnemonics` off), `AlwaysOnMnemonics`, `DisableAltFocus` (`window.customMenuBarAltFocus` off; Alt still toggles the visibility toggle), `OnRun`, `OnVisibilityChange`, `OnFocusChange`, and a `MenuBarController` (`ToggleFocus`, `Blur`, `IsVisible`, `IsFocused`, `IsOpen`, `FocusedMenu`, `MnemonicsShown`) for the command that focuses the application menu. The settings that choose these options are wired by step 6.7. Not ported: the compact mode, the overflow menu of a bar that is too narrow, the macOS tab navigation, and the deferral of an update of the menus while the bar is in use.

### 10.3 Editor groups and tabs (`core/editorgroup`, `gui/workbench`)

A grid of groups (nested split views). Each group has its editors in order, a preview editor, pinned editors, an active editor and a most-recently-used order. Editors are text editors, diff editors, merge editors, the settings UI, the keybindings UI and, later, others; all implement one `EditorInput` interface (type id, resource, title, dirty, save, revert, serialize/deserialize for restore). `core/editorgroup` holds this as data; `gui/workbench` shows it.

**The model (step 6.8; `core/editorgroup`).** `Model` is a port of VS Code's `EditorGroupModel` (`editorGroupModel.ts`): the editors in tab order, at most one preview editor (a preview is replaced by the next preview; `Pin` keeps it, `Unpin` makes it a preview again), the **sticky** editors (VS Code's pinned tabs, with the pin icon, kept at the left; `Stick`/`Unstick`, the commands `pinEditor`/`unpinEditor`), the active editor, the most-recently-used order, and the events (open, close, move, active, pin, sticky, dirty, label, capabilities, will-dispose). `OpenEditor` follows `workbench.editor.openPositioning` (`left`, `right`, `first`, `last`; a sticky tab always keeps the first places) and `CloseEditor` follows `workbench.editor.focusRecentEditorAfterClose` (the most recently used editor, else the neighbour on the right, else the left). The terms: in the model and the code, "pin" means "keep" (not a preview) and "stick" means the pin icon; the commands and the settings use VS Code's words. `Serialize` and `RestoreModel` give and read the editors, the preview, the sticky count and the active editor as JSON, for step 10.x. `TabLabels` gives the labels of the tabs, and an editor whose name another one shares also shows its shortest differing folders (`Shorten`).

`EditorInput` is the interface of an editor's content: `TypeID`, `Resource`, `Name`, `Description`, `Title` (the tooltip), `Dirty`, `Matches`, `Capabilities` (`CapReadonly`, `CapUntitled`, `CapCanSplit`, `CapCannotClose`), `Serialize`, `Subscribe` (events `InputDirty`, `InputLabel`, `InputCapabilities`, `InputWillDispose`) and `Dispose`; `BaseInput` implements the events. Saving and reverting are the window's (§6.3), not methods of the interface. `TextInput` (type id `emetos.textEditor`) shows a `*document.Document`: its name is the file's, its description the folder relative to the window's folder (or `~`), its title the full path, and it serializes as the file URI; it follows the dirty state of the document.

**Documents are counted.** An editor uses a document and several editors can show one (two groups, a diff). `Documents.Acquire` opens a file (or finds the open one) and counts one user, `Retain` counts another and `Release` takes one away; the release by the last user closes the document. `OpenFile` counts nobody (the users of step 3.x and the tests keep working, and a document nobody retained is never closed by a release).

**The tab strip (`kit.Tabs`).** Port of the `multiEditorTabsControl.ts` and `editorTabsControl.css` look: tabs 35 px high, 120 px at least, a 1 px `tab.border` at the right, the active tab in `tab.activeBackground` with `tab.activeBorderTop` (2 px, if the theme sets it) and `tab.activeBorder`, hover in `tab.hoverBackground`, the unfocused group in the `tab.unfocused*` colors; a preview tab has an italic label; a sticky tab has the pin icon in place of the file icon and sits left of `tab.lastPinnedBorder`; a modified tab shows the dot where the close button is (the pinned-and-modified icon on a sticky one), `tab.activeModifiedBorder` and the others when `workbench.editor.highlightModifiedTabs` is on. The close button shows on the active tab and under the pointer. A click activates, a double click on a preview tab keeps it (`OnPin`), a click on the pin icon unpins it, a middle click closes. The strip scrolls sideways (3 px scrollbar) and reveals the active tab. `TabsSingle` shows the active editor's title only (a simplified single-tab header: icon and label). The colors are registered in `core/theme` (`tab.*`, `editorGroupHeader.tabsBackground`, `editorGroupHeader.tabsBorder`, `editorGroupHeader.border`, `editorGroupHeader.noTabsBackground`) and carried by `kit.Colors`.

**Settings** (`core/settings/builtin.go`, VS Code's keys and defaults): `workbench.editor.showTabs` (`multiple`, `single`, `none`), `enablePreview` (true), `focusRecentEditorAfterClose` (true), `openPositioning` (`right`), `tabCloseButton` (`right`; `left`, `right`, `off`), `tabActionLocation` (`right`), `tabActionCloseVisibility` (true), `pinnedTabSizing` (`normal`; `compact`, `shrink`), `highlightModifiedTabs` (false) and `decorations.colors` (true; step 7.4: whether the label of a deleted editor is drawn in `list.errorForeground`; `decorations.badges` is not registered). A **deleted** editor (`editorgroup.DeletedState`/`IsDeleted`, implemented by `TextInput` while its document is orphaned, §6.3 "External changes") has its label and description struck through (`TabItem.Deleted`, `TabsOpts.DecorationColors`, the row of Open Editors too) and the tooltip "title • Deleted" (`DecoratedTitle`); `list.errorForeground` (#F88070 dark, #B01011 light, none in the high contrast themes) is registered with the colors of `listColors.ts`. VS Code's `tabActionUnpinVisibility` is not registered (the pin icon always unpins).

**The group in the app (`gui/app/group.go`, `editors.go`).** Step 6.8 shows one group: the tab strip, the breadcrumbs and the editor of the active tab. Each editor has a `textEditor`: a view model of its own over the shared document (so the cursors, the scroll position, the folds and the find state come back when its tab is activated again) and its own tokenizer, bracket pairs, indent levels and folding regions, stopped when the editor is closed. Files that the command line opens are kept (pinned) tabs; a file opened by the breadcrumbs picker or by navigation (`dev.openFile` included, with its arguments unchanged) is shown as a preview when `enablePreview` is on, an editor that is open already is activated, and an edit keeps a preview. The window title follows the active editor (`● name` while it is modified). With no editor the empty state (the logo and the name) is shown and `activeEditor` is cleared. `confirmClose` is a stub that returns true: step 7.2 puts the save prompt in it.

**Commands and keys** (VS Code's ids, titles and Linux keys): `workbench.action.closeActiveEditor` (Close Editor, Ctrl+W; also in the File menu), `closeEditorsInGroup` (Ctrl+K W), `closeUnmodifiedEditors` (Close Saved Editors in Group, Ctrl+K U), `closeOtherEditors`, `closeEditorsToTheRight`, `closeAllEditors` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+W), `keepEditor` (Ctrl+K Enter), `pinEditor` and `unpinEditor` (Ctrl+K Shift+Enter, by `activeEditorIsPinned`), `nextEditor` (Ctrl+PageDown), `previousEditor` (Ctrl+PageUp), `openNextRecentlyUsedEditorInGroup` and `openPreviousRecentlyUsedEditorInGroup` (no key; Ctrl+Tab and Ctrl+Shift+Tab open the editor picker since step 6.19, see "History and switching as built" below and §10.6). The commands that close many editors leave the sticky ones. The window's context keys are `activeEditor` (the type id of the active input), `activeEditorIsPinned` (sticky), `activeEditorIsNotPreview` and, since step 7.1, `activeEditorIsDirty`, `activeEditorCanRevert` (an editor is open and is not an untitled one) and `dirtyWorkingCopies` (a document of the window has unsaved changes). A `TextInput` follows the saves of its document (`Document.OnSaved`) as well as its changes, and sends `InputCapabilities` when a Save As makes an untitled document a file.

**The grid (step 6.9; `core/editorgroup`).** `Grid` is a port of the behavior of VS Code's `Grid`, `GridView` and `EditorPart`: a tree of splits (`Horizontal` puts the children side by side, `Vertical` stacks them; the children of a split have the other axis, and the root is always a split) whose leaves are `Group`s (an id that is never used again and a `Model`). The sizes are shares of the extent of the split (they add up to 1), not pixels; the view keeps the least sizes (a group is at least 220 px wide and 70 px high). A split never has one child: removing a group folds a split that is left with one child into its parent (a split of the other axis hoists its children), and a root left with one split becomes that split.
- **Adding** (`AddGroup(at, dir)`; `DirUp/Down/Left/Right`): next to a group along the axis of its split, or, across it, the group and the new one become the two halves of a new split. A single group makes the root run along the side. `workbench.editor.splitSizing` (`auto`, `distribute`, `split`; default `auto`) sizes it: `split` halves the group, `distribute` makes the whole split equal, `auto` does the second when the children of the split are equal already (within half a percent) and the first otherwise. **Removing** gives the room to the neighbor on the left or above (the one on the right for the first child), or distributes it by the same rule. `RemoveGroup` of a group with editors moves them first into the most recently active other group; the last group is never removed.
- **Closing empty groups** (`workbench.editor.closeEmptyGroups`, default true): a group whose last editor closes is removed (unless it is the only one), after the grid has told its listeners about the close; the active group then becomes the most recently active other group.
- **Active group and order**: `Activate`, `Active`, the most-recently-used order (`Groups(MostRecentlyActiveGroups)`), the order of the grid (`GridAppearance`: depth first, left to right, top to bottom, which "group 1..8" and `dev.openFile`'s `group` count in) and the creation order. Activating a group that is not the maximized one ends the maximized state.
- **Neighbors** (`FindByDirection(group, dir, wrap)`): the groups whose edge touches the edge of the group on that side (found by the geometry of the sizes), the most recently active first (a group that was never active sorts first, as VS Code's sort does); with `wrap` the edge of the grid leads to the opposite edge (a single group finds itself). `FindByLocation` gives the first, last, next or previous group of the grid order, with or without wrap. `FocusIndex(n, side)` is Ctrl+n: the n-th group, a new group on the preferred side of the last one for n = count + 1, nothing further away.
- **Editors between groups**: `MoveEditor` opens the very same input in the target (pinned, sticky when it was, active unless `Inactive`; at `Index` if given) and then closes it in the source with the context `CloseMove`, so an editor keeps its view model; a target that holds a matching editor keeps it. `CopyEditor` opens a copy (`Copier.CopyInput`, which `TextInput` has; an input without it stays in one group, as a singleton editor) and `SplitGroup` is `AddGroup` + copy of the active editor + `Activate`. `MergeGroup(group, target)` moves all the editors to the end of the target (those that are sticky in the target keep their place; only the active editor of the active group stays active) and removes the group; `MergeAllGroups` does it for all the others (`joinAllGroups`). `MoveOrCopyTarget(source, to, value, side)` is VS Code's `moveCopyEditorsToGroup`: `left`/`right`/`up`/`down` find the neighbor or add a group there, `first`/`last`/`previous`/`next` go by the grid order (`next` of the last group adds a group on the preferred side, `previous` of the first on the opposite one), `center` and `position`. `CopyGroup` clones a group (`Model.Clone`: the same order, preview, sticky, active and most-recently-used order, with copies of the inputs). `MoveGroup` moves a group next to another one (within its own split it reorders and keeps the sizes).
- **Sizes**: `DistributeSizes` makes every split equal (`evenEditorWidths`); `SetSizes(path, sizes)` records a sash drag without counting as a change; `Version` counts the changes the grid made to the tree and the sizes, which tell the view to start over from them. `Maximize`/`ExitMaximized`/`ToggleMaximize`: the other groups are hidden (VS Code hides them too; the sizes are kept and they come back as they were); adding, removing, moving, evening out, applying a layout and activating another group end it.
- **Layout and state** (used by step 7.5, §10.10): `Layout` and `ApplyLayout` are VS Code's `EditorGroupLayout` (`{orientation, groups: [{size, groups}]}`; the nodes without a size share what the sized ones leave; a split of one node is that node; with fewer places than groups the editors of the extra groups are merged into the last place, with more empty groups are added; the groups keep their places in the grid order). `Serialize` and `RestoreGrid` give and read the whole grid as JSON (a text input serializes as the URI of its document: a file, or an untitled one, `untitled:3`, which step 7.6 restores from its backup; a virtual document is left out) (`SerializeWith` and `RestoreGridWith` also carry a `State` per input, which the window fills with the view state of its editor and the model does not look into): the tree with sizes and the serialized model of each group, the active group, the most-recently-used order, the maximized group and the next id; a state that is not a tree of groups fails with the tag `GridStateInvalid`. The other failures have the tags `GroupUnknown` (a group that is not in the grid), `GroupMoveOntoItself`, `GroupMergeOntoItself`, `GroupTargetInvalid` and `LayoutInvalid`.
