**The grid in the app (`gui/app/group.go`, `gridview.go`, `groupcommands.go`).** The editor area is the grid: a `kit.SplitView` for each split (proportional, `editorGroup.border` between the panes, the sizes of the grid as the first sizes, the shell asks for a new split view with a new key whenever `Version` changes) and for each group a tab strip, the breadcrumbs and the editor of its active tab, or, for an empty group, the logo and the name (a click gives it the focus; `editorGroup.emptyBackground` and, while it is active, `editorGroup.focusedEmptyBorder`). A group keeps its widgets (and so their state) when it moves in the tree, through a global key; an editor keeps its own when it moves to another group. The strip of a group that is not the active group has the `tab.unfocused*` colors. Every editor in every group has a view model of its own over the document, which several groups share through `Documents.Acquire/Retain/Release` (an editor counts one use; the document stays open while any group shows it, and a closed group releases its editors' uses). **The view models of one document have owners of their own**: a view model reserves 8 consecutive owners (`Document.AllocOwners`) and uses them as slots (auto-closed pairs 1, folding 2, find 3, word highlight 4, selection highlight 5, snippets 6, which `ViewModel.owner(slot)` turns into the document's owner; `snippet.Editor.Owner` names the one of the snippets), so each editor sees and replaces only its own marks, and the first view model of a document has the owners 1 to 6 as before. A pointer pressed in a group, a tab chosen, or the focus moving into an editor or its find widget makes its group the active group; every change of the active group, and an editor that had the focus closing, gives the keyboard focus to the active group (its editor, or its empty area), tried again over a few frames until the widgets are there, and the focus is not followed while that waits. Opening a file (`dev.openFile`, the breadcrumbs) goes to the active group unless a group is given, and makes that group the active one. `workbench.editor.openSideBySideDirection` (`right`, `down`; default `right`) is the side `workbench.action.splitEditor` splits to (`splitEditorOrthogonal` the other one).

**Commands and keys of the grid** (VS Code's ids, titles and Linux keys; registered in `core/command/builtin.go` and `core/keybinding/defaults.go`): `workbench.action.splitEditor` (Ctrl+\), `splitEditorOrthogonal` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+\; VS Code's source gives this chord to the four directional splits too, which cannot all have it), `splitEditorLeft/Right/Up/Down`, `focusFirstEditorGroup` (Ctrl+1) to `focusEighthEditorGroup` (Ctrl+8; the second to eighth have no title, as in VS Code), `focusLastEditorGroup`, `focusNextGroup`, `focusPreviousGroup`, `navigateEditorGroups`, `focusLeftGroup/RightGroup/AboveGroup/BelowGroup` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+arrows; they wrap) and the four `...WithoutWrap` ones, `moveEditorToPreviousGroup` (Ctrl+Alt+Left), `moveEditorToNextGroup` (Ctrl+Alt+Right), `moveEditorToAboveGroup/BelowGroup/LeftGroup/RightGroup`, `moveEditorToFirstGroup` (Shift+Alt+1), `moveEditorToLastGroup` (Shift+Alt+9), the same eight as `splitEditorTo...Group` (copies), `moveActiveEditor` and `copyActiveEditor` (the argument `{"to", "by": "tab"|"group", "value"}`; by tab only moves), `newGroupLeft/Right/Above/Below`, `moveActiveEditorGroupLeft/Right/Up/Down` (Ctrl+K arrows; the target may also be across the direction, as in VS Code), `duplicateActiveEditorGroupLeft/Right/Up/Down`, `joinTwoGroups` (the neighbor on the right, else below, left, above), `joinAllGroups`, `closeGroup` (Ctrl+W while the active group is empty and there are several, a rule of its own that wins over Close Editor), `closeEditorsAndGroup`, `closeAllGroups` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+Shift+W; closes the sticky editors too and removes the empty groups whatever `closeEmptyGroups` says), `closeEditorsInOtherGroups`, `closeEditorInAllGroups`, `evenEditorWidths` (Reset Editor Group Sizes), `toggleMaximizeEditorGroup` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+M, enabled with several groups or while one is maximized) and the layouts `editorLayoutSingle`, `TwoColumns`, `ThreeColumns`, `TwoRows`, `ThreeRows`, `TwoByTwoGrid`, `TwoColumnsBottom`, `TwoRowsRight` and `layoutEditorGroups` (the argument is a `Layout`). `closeEditorsInGroup` now closes the group when it empties, and `closeAllEditors` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+W) closes the editors of every group. The context keys are `multipleEditorGroups`, `editorPartMultipleEditorGroups`, `editorPartMaximizedEditorGroup` and `activeEditorGroupEmpty`, besides those of step 6.8. The menus are View > Editor Layout (Split Up, Down, Left, Right and the layouts, VS Code's groups and order) and Go > Switch Group (Group 1 to 5, Next and Previous Group, Group Left, Right, Above, Below; the rows from Group 3 on and the rest are enabled when there are several groups). Not done: the commands that expand a group (`minimizeOtherEditors`, `minimizeOtherEditorsHideSidebar`, `toggleEditorWidths`; they need the least size of a group in pixels in the model), `maximizeEditorHideSidebar`, locking groups, side-by-side editors and moving to a new window. Settings: `workbench.editor.openSideBySideDirection`, `workbench.editor.closeEmptyGroups` and `workbench.editor.splitSizing`. Colors: `editorGroup.border` (`#444444`, `#E7E7E7`), `editorGroup.emptyBackground` and `editorGroup.focusedEmptyBorder`.

**Closing with unsaved changes as built (step 7.2; `core/editorgroup/closeplan.go`, `gui/app/closing.go`).** Every way to close editors goes through one function, `shellState.closeConfirmed(targets, done)` (`closeEditors(group, editors)` is the form for one group): the close button and middle click of a tab, the close commands (`closeActiveEditor`, `closeEditorsInGroup`, `closeOtherEditors`, `closeEditorsToTheRight`, `closeAllEditors`, `closeEditorsInOtherGroups`, `closeEditorInAllGroups`, `closeEditorsAndGroup`, `closeAllGroups`), the close button of the Open Editors view and of the editor picker, and Close Folder and Open Folder (§10.11). `closeUnmodifiedEditors` closes only editors that are clean, so it never asks; `closeGroup` moves the editors to another group and closes none; deleting a file in the Explorer closes clean editors only (§10.5); moving an editor between groups closes nothing.
- **The plan** (`editorgroup.PlanClose(grid, targets)`, pure Go): the targets are sets of editors of groups (`CloseTarget`); the plan lists the editors among them that have unsaved changes (`EditorInput.Dirty`) and whose document no editor that stays open shows, in the order of the targets, each document once (`Unsaved`). VS Code's rule (`doHandleCloseConfirmation`) is ported: a dirty document that another editor in any group still shows is not asked about, and it is asked about when its last editor closes. An empty untitled document is not dirty (§7.1), so it closes silently. `editorgroup.AllTargets(grid)` is every editor of every group.
- **The dialog** (`askUnsaved`): one dialog for the whole batch (VS Code asks editor by editor for a group, and once for the dirty working copies when a window closes; Emetos asks once for all of them). Type warning; message "Do you want to save the changes you made to {name}?" for one document (the file name, or `Untitled-n`), "Do you want to save the changes to the following {n} files?" for several, with the names in the detail, ten at the most and then "...1 additional file not shown" or "...N additional files not shown" (VS Code's `getFileNamesMessage`; the same wording as the delete confirmation of §10.5), a blank line and "Your changes will be lost if you don't save them."; buttons "Save" ("Save All" for several; the default, so Enter presses it), "Don't Save" and "Cancel" (Escape; a click beside the dialog does nothing). The window is modal while it shows.
- **The answers.** *Save* saves the documents one after the other with the pipeline of §6.3 (`saveDocument`: an untitled document through Save As, one at a time); the first that fails (its notification shows) or whose Save As is cancelled, and a document that was edited again meanwhile, cancels the close, as VS Code vetoes an editor that is still dirty; when all are saved the editors close. *Don't Save* closes the editors: the documents are dropped with their last editor (`Documents.Release`), which is what VS Code's revert before the close achieves; the files are not touched and nothing is read. *Cancel* closes nothing. A file closed with Don't Save is still recorded for Reopen Closed Editor (§10.3 "Closed editors"), which reads it from disk (VS Code does the same); an untitled document is not recorded.
- **Re-entry.** From the moment the dialog shows until its answer is carried out (the saves and Save As included) `closeState.busy` is set, and any other request that would ask (another close, Open Folder, the close of the window) is ignored; a close that has nothing to ask about still closes. The editor picker hides when its close button would ask, since the dialog comes over it.
- **Auto save before the question (step 7.3, §6.3 "Auto save").** `closeConfirmed` first saves the documents that `files.autoSave` `onFocusChange` and `onWindowChange` save instead of asking about, then plans the close again and asks about what is still dirty; a close with nothing left to ask closes at once. `closeNow` holds back the auto save of focus changes, since the editors that come to the front while others close are not the user leaving them.
- **Not built:** VS Code's activation of the editor that is asked about before the dialog shows, the pending-confirmation memo per editor, and a document that is being saved is asked about like any other (VS Code skips it).

**Tab interactions (step 6.10; `gui/app/tabdrag.go`, `gui/app/tabmenu.go`, `core/editorgroup/droptarget.go`).** A tab is dragged (primary button, 4 px, a label with the name of the editor follows the pointer, 12 px below and to the right of it, kept inside the window) over the tab strips and the editor areas of all the groups. The places are found from where the widgets are on the screen (`kit.TabsController` for a strip and its tabs, a `kit.Bounds` for the group). The rules are pure and tested without a window (`core/editorgroup`): `TabDropIndex(spans, x)` is the place in a strip (before the tab the pointer is over when it is in the left half or on the middle, after it in the right half, at the end past the last tab; the line shows it, and the tab dragged over a strip scrolls into view when the pointer comes to it); `ZoneAt(x, y, w, h, opts)` is the zone of an editor area (the area below the tab strip, or all of an empty group): the middle (all but a ring of a tenth of the width and the height) is `DropCenter`, and in the ring the area is in thirds along the preferred axis (`PreferVertical`: `openSideBySideDirection` is `right`: left third, right third, then up and down in halves of the middle third; otherwise up, down, and left and right in halves) giving `DropLeft/Right/Up/Down`, which split; without `workbench.editor.splitOnDragAndDrop` (default true, which Alt turns around for one drag) the whole area is the center; `ZoneRect` is the half of the area that a split zone highlights (the whole area for the center) in `editorGroup.dropBackground`; `DropAllowed` refuses moving the only editor of a group onto its own group. The drop is `Grid.DropOnTabs(from, editor, onto, index, copied)` (within a group the place counts the tab itself; a place in the pinned region pins the tab; the group becomes active) and `Grid.DropOnZone(from, editor, onto, zone, copied)` (the center moves the editor into the group; a side adds a group next to it and moves the editor into that; when the editor is the last of its group, `closeEmptyGroups` is on and it moves, the group itself moves next to the target, with its editor). Ctrl held at the drop copies instead of moves (VS Code's Ctrl on Linux). Escape cancels; a drag that ends over nothing drops nothing. While the pointer rests within 35 px of an end of a strip the strip scrolls (`TabEdgeStep`: up to 14 px each frame). Not here (VS Code has them): dragging groups, dragging several selected tabs, dropping files from the file manager, the drop-into-editor prompt, dragging a tab out of the window, opening the tab that a drag rests on for 1.5 s, and `workbench.editor.dragToOpenWindow`.
**The context menu of a tab.** A right click on a tab opens the `EditorTitleContext` menu (rows as data in `core/command/menus_default.go`, VS Code's groups and order): Close, Close Others (dimmed when the group has one editor), Close to the Right (dimmed for the last tab; only while the tabs show), Close Saved and Close All (`1_close`); Copy Path and Copy Relative Path (`1_cutcopypaste`, for files); Keep Open (dimmed unless the tab is a preview; only with `workbench.editor.enablePreview`) and Pin or Unpin (`3_preview`); Split Right (Split Down while `openSideBySideDirection` is `down`) and the Split & Move submenu (Split Up/Down/Left/Right, Move Above/Below/Left/Right; `5_split`). Reveal in Explorer View (`revealInExplorer`, group `2_files`, when `isFileSystemResource`) is there since step 6.14 (§10.8). Save and Save As (`1_close_save`, when `activeEditorIsDirty`) are there since step 7.1. Not yet: Reveal in File Manager (step 7.x), Reopen Editor With, Share, the moves into a new window. The when-clauses and preconditions read the keys of the tab that was clicked, set for the menu as VS Code sets them (`activeEditorIsNotPreview`, `activeEditorIsPinned`, `activeEditorIsDirty`, `activeEditorIsFirstInGroup`, `activeEditorIsLastInGroup`, `groupEditorsCount`, `editorTabsVisible`, `isFileSystemResource`, and `splitEditorsVertically`, which is also a key of the window: the direction is `down`). **Every row runs its command with the context `{"groupId": n, "editorIndex": n}`** (VS Code's `resolveCommandsContext`): the commands that act on an editor (`closeActiveEditor`, `closeEditorsInGroup`, `closeUnmodifiedEditors`, `closeOtherEditors`, `closeEditorsToTheRight`, `keepEditor`, `pinEditor`, `unpinEditor`, `splitEditor` and `splitEditorLeft/Right/Up/Down` through `Grid.SplitEditor`, `moveEditorTo...Group` and `splitEditorTo...Group`, `copyFilePath`, `copyRelativeFilePath`) act on that editor and its group, and on the active editor of the active group without it; a context whose group or tab is gone does nothing. `closeEditorsToTheRight` keeps the active editor (it is pinned, as before) and closes what is on the right of the tab that was clicked. **Copy path:** `copyFilePath` (Ctrl+Alt+C outside the editor, Ctrl+K Ctrl+Alt+C in it), `copyRelativeFilePath` (Ctrl+Shift+Alt+C, Ctrl+K Ctrl+Shift+Alt+C) and `workbench.action.files.copyPathOfActiveFile` (Ctrl+K P) put the path of the file on the system clipboard (through the clipboard of the editor widget); the relative path is relative to the folder of the window and is the full path for a file outside it or without a folder; the settings `explorer.copyPathSeparator` and `explorer.copyRelativePathSeparator` are not registered. Menu rows run through `workbench.MenuSource`, like the other menus. The menu closes, and the keyboard focus goes back to the active group. **Colors:** `tab.dragAndDropBorder` (the active tab's foreground; `contrastActiveBorder` in the high contrast themes) and `editorGroup.dropBackground` (`#53595D` at 50 %, `#2677CB` at 18 %, none in high contrast dark, `#0F4A85` at 50 %). **Setting:** `workbench.editor.splitOnDragAndDrop`. **Focus:** an editor that was replaced by another in its group (the active tab changed, a file opened over it) gives the keyboard focus to the new active editor of the group, as a closed editor does, instead of letting the focus fall to an editor of another group, which made that group active. For that, the request for the focus of a group counts as done only when its editor has the focus (it is tried again after each frame, up to the 6 frames of §10.3), and an editor widget that goes while it has the focus tells `OnFocus(false)` (the window no longer takes the stale "focused" of an editor that came back for the truth).
**Left to later steps.** The keyboard navigation inside the strip and the full single-tab header; saving the grid is step 7.5 (`Serialize` and `Layout` exist).

**History and switching as built (step 6.19; `core/editorgroup/{navstack,history,closed}.go`, `gui/app/{navigation,switcher}.go`).** Ports of `historyService.ts` (`EditorNavigationStack`, `EditorNavigationStacks`, `EditorSelectionState`, the recently used and recently closed editors), `TextEditorPaneSelection` of `textEditor.ts` and the actions of `editorActions.ts`. `editorgroup.History` has no Glutter type; the window gives it an `Opener` (show a location, read the active editor) and tells it what happens.
- **Locations.** A `NavLocation` is a group, the file of the editor (an editor with no file is kept as it is and leaves the stacks when it is disposed) and a `NavSelection` (the main cursor). Three stacks make one set: *selections* (Back and Forward), *edits* (Go to Last Edit Location, the `Edits` commands) and *navigations* (the `Navigation` commands). The active editor changing goes to the selections stack only. A selection change comes with a reason (`SelectionReason`: programmatic, user, edit, navigation, jump): it always goes to the selections stack, an edit also to the edits stack, a navigation or a jump also to the navigations stack, and a jump adds the place it left as well (go to line is a jump; moving the cursor by the mouse or the keys is the user's; showing a location and restoring a view are programmatic). A selection counts as the same place when its first line is the same, as similar when it is less than 10 lines away (`SelectionThreshold`) and as different at 10 or more. A new entry is pushed when the group or the editor changes, when a selection is unknown, when it is different, or when it comes from a jump or a navigation even if it is similar; otherwise the entry at the place is replaced. A new entry drops the entries after the place, the stack keeps 50 (`MaxNavigationStack`), adjacent entries that are the same place are joined when entries are removed, and a file that is deleted (by the Explorer or by another program, as the watcher says) takes its entries away while a moved file keeps them at the new path. A group that is removed takes its entries away. Going back, forward, previous (the place the stack was at before it moved) or to the last entry moves the place and shows the entry; the stack ignores the changes the showing causes until the opener says it is done (the file of a closed editor is read on a goroutine first). A filtered stack (edits, navigations) first shows the entry at the place when the editor is not at it, before it moves.
- **Scope** (`workbench.editor.navigationScope`, default `default`; `editorGroup`, `editor`): one set of stacks for the window, for each group, or for each editor of a group (its stacks go when it closes); changing the setting starts the stacks empty.
- **Showing a location** (`openLocation`): the editor of the file in the group of the location (the active group when that is gone) becomes active, or the file is opened there as a preview editor when it was closed; the cursor goes where the location says, programmatically, and the editor scrolls to center it. A file that cannot be read is not shown and the history goes on.
- **Closed editors** (`ClosedEditors`, at most 20 = `MaxRecentlyClosedEditors`): every editor that closes, except a preview that another replaces and an editor that moves to another group, is recorded when it has a file (an untitled document is not), with its group, its place, whether it was pinned, its view state and a batch number; the editors that close in one turn of the UI thread (Close All, Close Others, a closed group) are one batch. Recording a file again moves its entry to the end; files that are deleted or moved follow the explorer. `workbench.action.reopenClosedEditor` takes the last batch, reads the files on a goroutine and opens them in the **active group** (as VS Code's `reopenLastClosedEditor` does, not necessarily in the group they were closed in), kept, pinned if they were, at the place they had unless that place is among the other kind of tabs, with the view they had when `workbench.editor.restoreViewState` is on (default true: it applies to reopening only; the view of an editor that is opened again from the Explorer is not remembered). A file of the batch that is gone is skipped, one that the group shows already is not opened again, and when nothing of a batch opened the one before it is tried. The context key `canReopenClosedEditor` follows the list; Clear Editor History also forgets the recently opened files (it asks first).
- **Recently used editors** (`RecentStack`): `openNextRecentlyUsedEditor`, `openPreviousRecentlyUsedEditor` and the `InGroup` variants walk the order the editors were used in, a list taken when the walk starts and kept (so that two commands do not swap two editors) until anything else changes the order. `nextEditor` and `previousEditor` (Ctrl+PageDown, Ctrl+PageUp) open the next and the previous tab, go on to the first or the last editor of the next or the previous group that has one (round the groups), and `nextEditorInGroup` and `previousEditorInGroup` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+PageDown, Ctrl+K Ctrl+PageUp) go round the active group.
- **Commands and keys** (VS Code's ids, titles and Linux keys): `workbench.action.navigateBack` (Go Back, Ctrl+Alt+-, when `canNavigateBack`), `navigateForward` (Go Forward, Ctrl+Shift+-, when `canNavigateForward`), `navigateLast` (Go Previous), `navigateBackInEditLocations`, `navigateForwardInEditLocations`, `navigatePreviousInEditLocations`, `navigateToLastEditLocation` (Go to Last Edit Location, Ctrl+K Ctrl+Q), `navigateBackInNavigationLocations`, `navigateForwardInNavigationLocations`, `navigatePreviousInNavigationLocations`, `navigateToLastNavigationLocation`, `reopenClosedEditor` (Reopen Closed Editor, Ctrl+Shift+T), `clearEditorHistory` (Clear Editor History), `openNextRecentlyUsedEditor`, `openPreviousRecentlyUsedEditor`, the two `InGroup` ones (no key), and the editor picker commands of §10.6. The Browser Back and Forward keys that VS Code also binds are not bound. Menu rows: Back, Forward and Last Edit Location in the Go menu (`1_history_nav`, dimmed while their context key is off), the Switch Editor submenu of the Go menu (Next Editor, Previous Editor, Next Used Editor, Previous Used Editor, Next Editor in Group, Previous Editor in Group, Next Used Editor in Group, Previous Used Editor in Group; not the first and second side of a side by side editor, which Emetos does not have) and Reopen Closed Editor in the Open Recent submenu of the File menu as VS Code has it (the submenu has no row in the File menu yet: `MenubarRecentMenu`). Context keys set by the window from the history: `canNavigateBack`, `canNavigateForward`, `canNavigateBackInNavigationLocations`, `canNavigateForwardInNavigationLocations`, `canNavigateToLastNavigationLocation`, `canNavigateBackInEditLocations`, `canNavigateForwardInEditLocations`, `canNavigateToLastEditLocation` and `canReopenClosedEditor`. New settings: `workbench.editor.navigationScope` and `workbench.editor.restoreViewState`.
- **Not built.** The history of editors that VS Code saves between sessions (the stacks of locations, the recently closed editors: step 7.5 saves the editors of the groups with their order of use, and the quick open list, §10.10, and nothing else), `workbench.editor.sharedViewState`, the view state of an editor that is opened again by other means than Reopen Closed Editor, the mouse buttons and the Browser keys for Back and Forward, transient editors, and the editors of a side by side input.

**Editor kinds (step 8.1; `gui/app/panes.go`).** The editor of a `TextInput` is a `textEditor`, which the shell makes for every one; the editor of any other type of input is a **pane**, and the window finds out how to handle a type of input in a list, `editorKinds()`, whose entries are `editorKind{typeID, restore, newPane, icon}`. `restore(value)` makes the input again from what its `Serialize` gave (the state of a window comes back through it: `restoreGrid` asks the kind of every saved editor that is not text; a kind that cannot make the input, or an unknown type id, drops the editor silently); `newPane(shell, input)` makes the `pane` that shows an input; `icon` is the Codicon of its tab. A `pane` has `build(bc, group, active)` (the widget that fills the group below the tab strip), `focus()` and `hasFocus()` (the keyboard focus goes to the pane when its group takes it, and the focus that moves into the pane makes its group the active one: `paneFocus` embeds the bookkeeping), and `dispose()`. The shell keeps `panes` next to `editors` (an input has one of the two): `ensureEditor` makes a pane for an input of a kind, a closed input disposes its pane, the group widget shows `paneColumn` (the tab strip over the pane, in a keyed subtree with a key of the pane so that its widget state follows it to another group) when the active input has a pane, and the tabs of any input show the icon of its kind. Everything else in the shell that looks at editors (saving, the history, the status bar, the editor commands) finds no `textEditor` for such an input and does nothing for it, as it does for an empty group. The keyboard shortcuts editor (step 8.3, §11.3: the type id `emetos.keybindings`, the icon `record-keys` since the Codicons have no keyboard glyph, `core/editorgroup.KeybindingsInput`, `gui/app/keybindingspane.go`) is the second kind; the diff editor (9.5) and the merge editor (9.7) are added to `editorKinds()` the same way.

**The settings input (step 8.1; `core/editorgroup/settingsinput.go`).** `SettingsInput` (type id `emetos.settings`, tab name "Settings", the settings gear icon) shows no resource, is never dirty (the editor writes a setting as it changes it), has no capabilities (it cannot be split or copied: it is shown in one group), and `Matches` any other settings input, so opening it again finds the one that is open. Its `Serialize` is the JSON of its view state (`SettingsViewState`: the tab `target` (`user`, `workspace`), the `category` selected in the tree, the `scroll` position of the list and the `query` of the search), which the editor writes into the input whenever the user changes the view and which the state of the window saves (§10.10) like any other change of the groups; `ParseSettingsInput` reads it (an empty or unknown `target` is the User tab; text that is not JSON fails with the tag `SettingsInputInvalid`). It is not part of the backups of hot exit (§10.10): it has no content. Closing it never asks.

**Serialization of virtual inputs (step 8.2).** A `TextInput` of an `emetos-virtual:` document serializes as its URI (`emetos.textEditor` with the value `emetos-virtual:/defaultSettings.json`), and the restore (`Window.openSavedDocument`) opens it through `Documents.OpenVirtual`, so the content is made again and reflects the settings of the new session; a URI no provider makes (tag `VirtualURIUnknown`, logged at info level) drops the editor and is not counted in the notice of the editors that could not be restored. An untitled input still needs its backup, and a file its file.

### 10.4 Activity bar and side bar views

Activity bar entries for Explorer (and, later, Search, Source Control, Run and Debug, Extensions), plus the Manage menu (settings, keybindings, themes, profiles). Views are registered with an id, title, icon and container, so later areas add their own.

**Built in `core/command` and `gui/workbench` (step 6.7).**

- **View registry** (`command.ViewRegistry`, VS Code's `ViewContainersRegistry` and `ViewsRegistry` of `workbench/common/views.ts`). A **view container** has an id, a title, the code point of its Codicon, an order and a location (`sidebar`, the default, or `panel`). A **view** has an id, a name, a container, a `when`, an order and whether it starts `Collapsed`. `Containers(location)` lists the containers in the order of the activity bar (equal orders keep the registration order), `Views(container, ctx)` the views whose `when` holds, in order. Registering fails with `ViewContainerInvalid`, `ViewContainerDuplicate`, `ViewInvalid`, `ViewContainerUnknown` or `ViewDuplicate`. `DefaultViews` has the panel containers of step 6.12 (§10.13) and the Explorer (`workbench.view.explorer`, "Explorer", the `files` Codicon, order 0) with the views `workbench.explorer.openEditorsView` ("Open Editors", order 0, `HideByDefault`), `workbench.explorer.emptyView` ("No Folder Opened", when `workbenchState == 'empty'`) and `workbench.explorer.fileView` ("Folders", when `workbenchState != 'empty'`) (§10.8); the steps that build the views of a container register their containers and views there. A view is a header over an empty body until its step. A `View` has `HideByDefault`, and `workbench.Views` keeps which views are hidden (`IsHidden`, `SetHidden`, which also unfolds a view that is shown); the side bar skips a hidden view. The side bar takes slots from the window (`workbench.Options.ViewName`, `ViewActions`, `ViewBodyHeight`, `OnViewFocus`): a view can be titled other than its registry name (the folder view has the name of the folder), have buttons at the right end of its header that show while the pointer is over the view or the focus is in it, have a body of a fixed height instead of a share of what is left, and tell where the focus is.
- **State** (`workbench.Views`, one per window): the container the side bar shows (the first of the activity bar to begin with) and which views the user collapsed or expanded (a view starts as its descriptor says). `Open(id)` shows a container and the side bar; `Toggle(id)` is a click on its icon: the container that is open hides the side bar, any other opens.
- **Activity bar** (`workbench.ActivityIcons`, `workbench.ManageButton`, filled by the workbench when `Options.Views` is set): a column of icons, 48 px squares with 24 px Codicons, in `activityBar.inactiveForeground`, in `activityBar.foreground` under the pointer or when active, the active one with a line of 2 px beside it in `activityBar.activeBorder` (none is active while the side bar is hidden); a `kit.Hover` with the title of the container shows to the right of an icon; Enter and Space on a focused icon run it. At the bottom is the **Manage** gear: it opens the Manage menu (`GlobalActivity`) to the right of the activity bar, its bottom on the bottom of the gear; a second click closes it. Until a step adds items to `GlobalActivity` (Command Palette, Profiles, Settings, Keyboard Shortcuts, Themes), the menu has one dimmed item, `(empty)`.
- **Side bar** (`workbench.SideBarViews`): the title of the active container (uppercase, 11 px, 35 px high) over its views. A view header is 22 px high with a chevron and the name in bold capitals (11 px) on `sideBarSectionHeader.background`, a line above it in `sideBarSectionHeader.border` unless it is the first; a click, Enter or Space toggles the view, Right opens and Left folds it. The bodies of the views that are open share the height that is left; a view that is folded takes its header only. A body is built by `Options.ViewBody`; a view it gives nothing for has an empty body.
- Colors registered with this step: `activityBar.foreground`, `activityBar.inactiveForeground`, `activityBar.activeBorder`, `activityBar.activeFocusBorder`, `activityBar.activeBackground`, `sideBarSectionHeader.background`, `sideBarSectionHeader.foreground`, `sideBarSectionHeader.border`.

Not built: badges on the icons (`activityBarBadge.*`), the position of the activity bar (top, bottom), drag and drop of icons and views, the context menus of the activity bar and of the view headers, and the Account entry of VS Code's global activity.

### 10.5 Explorer (`core/workspace`, `gui/views/explorer`)

- `core/glob` has VS Code's glob patterns (`Parse`, `ParseRelative`, `ParseExpression` for `files.exclude`-style objects with `when` sibling clauses, `BasenameTerms` and `PathTerms` for searches) and a gitignore matcher (`Ignore`: nested `.gitignore` and `.ignore` files, negation, folder-only and anchored patterns, `**`, character classes, and the rule that a path below an excluded folder stays excluded; checked against `git check-ignore` by a test that needs git; the file list of the quick open reads the ignore files of a folder as the walk enters it, §10.6). Patterns run on Go's `regexp`. Sibling checks are synchronous (VS Code also accepts promises).
- The tree model reads folders lazily, sorts like VS Code (`explorer.sortOrder`), hides `files.exclude` matches (`core/glob`, VS Code's glob syntax), and watches with inotify (`golang.org/x/sys/unix`): the opened folder (recursively, as folders are read) and the parent folder of every open file outside it; events are coalesced every 50 ms; watching a directory (not inodes) sees files replaced by rename. Events for a file whose `FileStat` matches Emetos's own last write are ignored. Watching falls back to polling a folder when the inotify limit is reached, with a notice.

**As built (step 6.13, `core/workspace`).** Ports: `explorerModel.ts` (the tree), the `FileSorter`, `FilesFilter` and `ExplorerCompressionDelegate` of `explorerViewer.ts`, `comparers.ts`, and `platform/files/common/watcher.ts` (`EventCoalescer`). The package imports no Glutter. The files are `compare.go`, `config.go`, `workspace.go`, `sort.go`, `apply.go`, `events.go` (the coalescer), `watcher.go`, `inotify_linux.go` (and `inotify_other.go`, a stub that makes other systems poll).

*Model.* `workspace.Open(ctx, path)` opens one folder (Emetos has no multi-root workspaces): the path is made absolute and clean and must be a folder (`WorkspacePathInvalid`, `WorkspaceFolderNotFound`, `WorkspaceStatFailed`, `WorkspaceNotDirectory`, `WorkspaceOpenCancelled`). A `Workspace` is a tree of `Item`s (name, path, `IsDir`, `IsSymlink` with `IsDir` telling what a link points at, `IsUnknown` for sockets and devices, size, modification time, `Resolved`, `Err`); it is safe for concurrent use. Nothing is read at open: `Item.Children(ctx)` reads a folder the first time (`os.ReadDir` through the injectable `FS`; a link is a folder when its target is), drops what `files.exclude` hides, and returns the children sorted. A folder that cannot be read keeps the error on the item (`Err`) and stays unread, so the next `Children` tries again, and fails with `WorkspaceReadDirFailed` or `WorkspaceReadCancelled`; the rest of the tree is not affected. Links are shown but never followed by the model itself (a link to a parent loop is a folder that can be expanded again and again, only by the user). `Refresh(ctx, item)` reads again a folder and every folder below it that was read, keeping the `Item`s that are still there (and so what was read below them); a folder that is gone leaves the tree; `Excluded`, `Find` and `Loaded` complete it. `explorer.sortOrder` has VS Code's six values: `default` (folders first, names), `mixed`, `filesFirst`, `type` (folders first, files by extension then name), `modified` (folders first, newest first, names breaking ties) and `foldersNestsFiles` (file nesting is not built, so it sorts as `default`); `explorer.sortOrderLexicographicOptions` (`default`, `upper`, `lower`, `unicode`) and `explorer.sortOrderReverse` are read as `FileSorter` reads them. Names compare as `comparers.ts` does, with the root collation of `golang.org/x/text/collate` where VS Code uses `Intl.Collator` of the user's locale: numbers compare as numbers (`file2` before `file10`), punctuation first, `a` before `A`, and names equal as numbers (`foo1`, `foo01`) are told apart by length. `files.exclude` is parsed with `glob.ParseExpression` and matched against the path relative to the folder, with the siblings of the entry for the clauses with a `when`. **Compact folders** (`explorer.compactFolders`, default true): `Workspace.CompactChain(ctx, item)` gives the folders the Explorer shows as one element, `a/b/c`: the item and then its only child while that is a folder, reading the folders it needs. As in VS Code's `ExplorerCompressionDelegate`, the root and the folders directly below it never take part in a chain; Emetos also never goes through a link. `SetConfig` changes the settings of the tree (the order follows at once, `Refresh` applies `files.exclude`), `ConfigFromSettings` reads them.

*Events and changes.* The watcher reports `Event`s (`EventCreated`, `EventChanged`, `EventDeleted`, `EventRenamed` with `OldPath`, and `EventRescan` for a folder whose events were lost). `Workspace.Apply(ctx, events)` applies them to what the tree has read and tells the listeners of `OnChange` (called on the goroutine that applied them, with no lock held) a `Change` each: `ChangeAdded`, `ChangeRemoved`, `ChangeUpdated`, `ChangeRenamed` or `ChangeRefreshed`. An event for a folder whose children were not read is ignored (reading it later finds the new state), so is one for a path outside the folder or one `files.exclude` hides. A created or changed path the tree does not have is added, one it has is updated (a file replaced by rename arrives as a change). A renamed or moved item keeps what was read below it and its paths follow; a move out of the read part of the tree is a removal; a rename over an item replaces it. The deletion of the folder itself is reported (`ChangeRemoved` of the root) and the tree is left as it was. The explorer view (§10.8) listens to these changes and shows them on the UI thread (`Post`).

*Saving and the watcher (step 7.1).* The window calls `Watcher.Suppress(path, stat)` with the stat that `fileio.Write` returned, on the goroutine of the write and before the result goes to the UI thread, so the event of the editor's own write (same size and modification time) is dropped; reads (open, revert, reopen) make no event and are not suppressed.

*Open documents after `Apply` (step 7.4).* The window's listener of the batches (`externalFileEvents`, on the UI thread) first hands the batch to the open documents (`externalEvents`: renames re-point documents, every document a path of an event touches is looked at, §6.3 "External changes") and then lets the history forget the files that are gone. The documents are looked at whatever the event kind (an own write that `Suppress` did not catch, a spurious event, a created file that was open and deleted before: the stat decides).

*Watcher.* `workspace.NewWatcher(ctx, WatcherOptions)` starts a watcher that stops with `ctx` (`Done()` closes when its goroutines are gone). `Watch(ctx, Target)` adds a target and returns the function that removes it: a folder (`Recursive`: every folder below it is watched, also those that appear later, `IN_DONT_FOLLOW` so links are not followed) or a file (`File`: its folder is watched, not recursively, and only events of that path are reported; the app uses it for the open files outside the folder). `Exclude` takes the keys of `files.watcherExclude` (matched with `TrimForExclusions`, so `.git/objects/**` also covers the folder `.git/objects`; relative to the target, or absolute): excluded folders get no watch and their events are dropped. `Watch` reads the tree to place the watches, so it runs on a goroutine (`WatcherPathInvalid`, `WatcherPathNotFound`, `WatcherStatFailed`, `WatcherStopped`, `WatcherAddFailed` for the target itself; a subfolder that cannot be watched is left out and logged). inotify is used through `os.NewFile` over a non-blocking descriptor, so closing it ends the read; the mask is create, delete, modify, close-write, attrib, moved from/to, delete self and move self. A queue overflow sends `EventRescan` for every target. *Coalescing* (`coalescer`, port of `EventCoalescer` plus renames): events are gathered for `DefaultDebounce` (50 ms, from the first event of a batch; the clock is injectable) and then reported in one batch, in the order deletes (shortest path first), renames, creates and changes, rescans. Rules: created then deleted cancels both; deleted then created is changed; created then changed stays created; otherwise the last kind wins; a delete below a deleted folder is dropped. A move from and a move to with the same cookie are one `EventRenamed`; an unpaired move from is a delete and an unpaired move to a create; a rename of a path created in the same batch (the temporary file of an atomic save), or onto a watched path from one that is not watched, is a `EventChanged` of the new path (inotify does not say whether the target existed, so a first save of a new file also arrives as a change: consumers treat a change of an unknown path as a creation, as `Apply` does). Folders moved inside the tree keep their watches under the new path; folders moved out lose them. **Own writes:** `Suppress(path, FileStat)` records the `FileStat` that a write or read of the editor returned (`fileio.Write` does); a created, changed or renamed event for that path whose file still has the same size and modification time is dropped, another stat or a delete forgets the entry (256 entries are kept). Step 7.1 calls it when it saves. **Polling:** a target is polled (a snapshot of the paths, sizes and times every `DefaultPollInterval`, 2 s, compared with the last one; it produces created, changed and deleted, no renames, and skips excluded folders; at most 500,000 entries) when `ForcePolling` is set, when inotify cannot be initialised (`WatcherInitFailed`, a `NoticePollingFallback` notice), or when its watches run out (`ENOSPC`: that target alone is polled, its inotify watches are dropped, an `EventRescan` is sent and a `NoticeWatchLimit` notice). Each kind of notice is sent once per watcher. The app shows a notice as one warning notification.

*Settings* (VS Code's keys, defaults and scopes): `files.exclude` (`**/.git`, `**/.svn`, `**/.hg`, `**/.jj`, `**/.DS_Store`, `**/Thumbs.db`), `files.watcherExclude` (the six `.git/objects`, `.git/subtree-cache` and `.hg/store` patterns), `files.watcherInclude` (`[]`), `explorer.sortOrder`, `explorer.sortOrderLexicographicOptions`, `explorer.sortOrderReverse` and `explorer.compactFolders`. Not registered: `explorer.excludeGitIgnore`, `explorer.fileNesting.*`, and `files.legacyWatcher`.

*In the app* (`gui/app/workspace.go`). `NewWindow` opens the folder as `Window.Workspace` (`Window.Folder` is its path; a folder that is not there fails the window, route `NewWindow->Window.openWorkspace->Open.WorkspaceFolderNotFound`). `cmd/emetos [folder] [files...]` already passed the first directory argument as the folder. The window uses the workspace for the breadcrumbs (`BreadcrumbsOpts.Root`: the folder first, then the path inside it), the title (`${rootName}`, `${folderName}`, `${folderPath}`), `Window.SnippetWorkspace()` (`WORKSPACE_NAME`, `WORKSPACE_FOLDER`, and `RELATIVE_FILEPATH` relative to the folder), and the Problems panel. `Window.StartWatching(ctx)` starts the watcher (the shell calls it when it is created, with its lifetime; it does nothing unless the process was made with `ProcessOptions.WatchFiles`, which the editor sets and tests leave off, since the widget tester never disposes a window): targets are the folder (recursive, with `files.watcherExclude`, plus the entries of `files.watcherInclude`: relative paths joined to the folder, absolute ones only inside it), the user's configuration folder (recursive; it is made if it is missing), and every open file outside the folder (`Documents` tells the window when a file is opened or closed). On a batch of events the window reads `settings.json` (user and `<folder>/.emetos/settings.json`) again with `Store.Reload`, the snippets with `snippet.Service.Reload` when something in the snippets folder changed, and applies the events to the tree. A change of `explorer.*` or `files.exclude` makes the workspace take the new configuration and refresh; a change of `files.watcherExclude` or `files.watcherInclude` restarts the watch of the folder. Keybindings are not reloaded (there is no user keymap yet).

Not built: multi-root workspaces, file nesting, `explorer.excludeGitIgnore`, renames reported by the polling fallback, and the use of `files.exclude` in the Problems panel and the breadcrumbs picker. The file operations are built (step 6.15, below).

**As built (step 6.15, file operations).** Ports: `fileActions.ts` (`incrementFileName`, `validateFileName`, the new, rename, delete and paste actions), `fileCommands.ts`, `fileImportExport.ts`, `explorerViewer.ts` (`FileDragAndDrop`, the inline input), `explorerService.ts` (the undo history) and `editorService.ts` (`handleMovedFile`, `handleDeletedFile`). The core is `core/workspace/names.go` and `fileops.go` (no Glutter), the view is `gui/views/explorer/edit.go` and `dnd.go`, and the window's logic is `gui/app/fileops.go`.

*Names* (`names.go`). `IncrementFileName(name, isFolder, naming)` makes the name of a copy as VS Code does: `NamingSimple` (`file copy.txt`, `file copy 2.txt`), `NamingSmart` (a trailing number, `file1.txt` to `file2.txt`, with the width of the digits kept; a name with no number or one too large falls back to the simple form) and `NamingDisabled` (the name is kept). `ValidateFileName(name, current, exists)` returns a `*NameProblem` (message and `Severity`) or nil: an error for an empty name, a name that starts with a slash, a name that exists in the folder (unless it is the item's own), and a part that is `.`, `..`, empty, longer than `MaxNameBytes` (255 bytes) or has a NUL; a warning for a part with leading or trailing whitespace; `CleanNewName` gives the relative path typed into the new-item input (`a/b/c.txt` makes the folders above, a trailing `/` makes a folder).

*Operations* (`fileops.go`). `NewFileOps(FileOpsOptions{Trash, Rename})` is the service; its methods take a context, run one at a time and return an `OpResult` (`Label`, `Moves`, `Created`, `Removed`, `Targets`; `Events()` turns it into watcher events, which the window applies to the tree at once, `Follow(path)` tells where an item went): `CreateFile`, `CreateFolder` (the folders above are made; an existing name fails), `Rename`, `Move(dst, srcs, MoveOptions{Overwrite})` (the same file system by rename, another by copying and deleting; an item moved over another replaces it by sending it to the trash, so undo restores it), `Copy(dst, srcs, CopyOptions{Naming, Overwrite})` (files, folders and links, a copy of a folder is bounded in depth, `Conflicts` tells the names that are taken), `Trash` (the freedesktop home trash with `.trashinfo`; an item on another file system fails with `FileOpTrashUnavailable` and the window offers the permanent delete), `Delete` (permanent), `Undo` and `Redo`. The history keeps 100 operations and is cleared with `ClearHistory`; `CanUndo`, `CanRedo`, `UndoLabel`, `RedoLabel` and `NextUndo` (the label and the `UndoPrompt` of the operation about to be taken back: `explorer.confirmUndo` is `verbose` (asks before every undo of a rename, move, paste or creation), `default` (before a paste or creation, which remove files) or `light` (only before undoing a creation, as VS Code asks for the removal of a new item); an undo of a trash is never asked). Undo takes back a creation (the item goes to the trash, so redo restores it), a move, a copy and a trash (restore); a permanent delete cannot be undone and clears the history. Errors: `FileOpPathInvalid`, `FileOpNameInvalid`, `FileOpParentMissing`, `FileOpSourceMissing`, `FileOpDestinationExists`, `FileOpCreateFailed`, `FileOpMoveFailed`, `FileOpMoveSame`, `FileOpMoveIntoItself`, `FileOpCopyFailed`, `FileOpCopySame`, `FileOpCopyIntoItself`, `FileOpCopyTooDeep`, `FileOpCopyUnsupported`, `FileOpTrashUnavailable`, `FileOpNothingToUndo`, `FileOpNothingToRedo` and `FileOpCancelled`.

*Settings* (VS Code's keys, defaults and scopes): `files.enableTrash` (true), `explorer.enableDragAndDrop` (true), `explorer.confirmDragAndDrop` (true), `explorer.confirmPasteNative` (true), `explorer.confirmDelete` (true), `explorer.enableUndo` (true), `explorer.confirmUndo` (`default`; `verbose`, `default`, `light`), `explorer.incrementalNaming` (`simple`; `simple`, `smart`, `disabled`), `explorer.autoOpenDroppedFile` (true), `explorer.copyRelativePathSeparator` and `explorer.copyPathSeparator` (`auto`; `/`, `\`, `auto`, which is `/` on Linux) and `workbench.editor.closeOnFileDelete` (false). A "Do not ask me again" box of a confirmation writes the setting to the user settings.

*Commands and keys* (Linux): `explorer.newFile` and `explorer.newFolder` (with `workbench.files.action.createFileFromExplorer` and `createFolderFromExplorer`, the buttons of the header), `renameFile` (F2), `moveFileToTrash` (Delete), `deleteFile` (Shift+Delete, and Delete where the trash cannot be used), `filesExplorer.cut` (Ctrl+X), `filesExplorer.copy` (Ctrl+C), `filesExplorer.paste` (Ctrl+V), `filesExplorer.cancelCut` (Escape while something is cut), `explorer.openToSide` (Ctrl+Enter), `revealFileInOS` (Ctrl+Alt+R), `copyFilePath` (Ctrl+Alt+C) and `copyRelativeFilePath` (Ctrl+Shift+Alt+C) (the last two are also in the tab menu and take the paths of the Explorer's selection when the Explorer has the focus). `undo` and `redo` run the file history while the tree has the focus (the window pushes a handler on the command stack, and removes it when the focus leaves). The keys are enabled by `foldersViewVisible && filesExplorerFocus && !inputFocus` and by the context keys `explorerResourceIsFolder`, `explorerResourceIsRoot`, `explorerResourceReadonly`, `explorerResourceMoveableToTrash`, `explorerResourceCut`, `explorerViewletFocus`, `filesExplorerFocus`, `fileCopied`, `resourceScheme` and `isFileSystemResource`. The key rules have the weight of the workbench's plus 10, as VS Code's `explorerCommandsWeightBonus`, so that they win over the lists' keys.

*Context menu* (`ExplorerContext`; a press of the secondary button on a row selects it, unless it is in the selection, and opens the menu; on the empty part of the tree it clears the selection and shows the menu of the folder): New File..., New Folder... (folders), Open to the Side (files), Open Containing Folder, then (files only, step 9.6) Compare with Selected (while a file is selected for compare, and one row is selected), Select for Compare and, with two rows selected, Compare Selected (group `3_compare`; §9.11), then Cut, Copy, Paste (folders; disabled while nothing was copied), Copy Path, Copy Relative Path, Rename..., Delete (or Delete Permanently where the trash cannot be used; VS Code shows that as an alternative while Shift is held). The root folder has no Cut, Copy, Rename and Delete.

*The inline input.* New File and New Folder open (and expand) the folder of the focused item (the folder of a file, the root with nothing focused) and show a row with an input in the tree: the icon, then a `kit.InputBox`; a new item is typed with its relative path. Rename shows the input over the row with the name selected, without the extension for a file. The validation of `ValidateFileName` shows in a balloon below the input (the input box's validation error or warning colors; the balloon stays while the input has the focus). Enter commits (an error blocks it, a warning does not), Escape or losing the focus cancels. The new item is selected, and a new file opens in the active group.

*Delete* asks (`explorer.confirmDelete`) with the names (at most ten, then "...N additional files not shown"), the button "Move to Trash" and "Do not ask me again" (which sets `explorer.confirmDelete` to false); a permanent delete asks with a warning, "This action is irreversible!", and no box; items with unsaved changes ask first ("You are deleting ... with unsaved changes"). A move to the trash that fails offers "Delete Permanently". *Cut, copy and paste:* cut dims the rows (opacity 0.5) until pasted or cancelled; copy and cut also put the `file://` URIs (and the paths as text) on the system clipboard (`text/uri-list`), so a file manager can paste them; paste reads the internal clipboard first and then the system's (files of a file manager are copied in, asking by `explorer.confirmPasteNative`); pasting a cut moves and clears the clipboard, pasting a copy copies with the incremental name; where the name is taken and `explorer.incrementalNaming` is `disabled`, the user is asked to overwrite. *Drag and drop:* the rows are dragged onto a folder (the highlight is `list.dropBackground`) to move them, with Ctrl held to copy; a drag onto itself, its own child or the folder it is in already is refused; a move asks first when `explorer.confirmDragAndDrop` is true (with "Do not ask me again"), a copy does not; a move over an item of the same name asks to overwrite (the replaced item goes to the trash). Files dragged from a file manager copy into the folder under the pointer (or the root folder), asking before a replacement, and `explorer.autoOpenDroppedFile` opens a single dropped file.

*Open editors follow the operations.* A rename or move renames the document of an open file (and of every file below a folder moved or renamed) with `Document.SetURI` (listeners: `OnURIChange`; the tab, the title, the breadcrumbs, the language and the text input label follow, and the document keeps its text, its undo history and its unsaved changes); an item deleted by the Explorer closes the editors of its files that have no unsaved changes (an editor with unsaved changes stays). A file deleted by another program (the watcher's events) keeps its editors, struck through (the document is orphaned), and closes its clean editors only when `workbench.editor.closeOnFileDelete` is true; a file that is back on disk is read again or marked conflicted (§6.3 "External changes"); a file renamed or moved by another program is followed like one of the Explorer's operations when the watcher pairs the rename (inotify does; the polling fallback reports a delete and a create), and an editor of a file with unsaved changes is kept in every case. An undo of a delete restores the file; the editors that were closed are not reopened.

*Not built:* the rows of the context menu for a workspace root, Open With, Find in Folder, Open in Integrated Terminal, the web's Upload and Download, and the drag of files out of Emetos to another application; a progress notification for a long copy; and one overwrite question for each item of a drop (one question lists them all).
- File operations (new, rename, move, copy, delete to trash, paste) are undoable from the explorer (Ctrl+Z when the explorer has focus; undoing a delete restores from the trash), and open editors follow renames and moves. Cut/copy/paste uses an internal clipboard and also puts the files' URIs on the system clipboard (`text/uri-list`), so a file manager can paste them.

### 10.6 Quick input (`core/fuzzy`, `gui/quickinput`)

One quick input widget with modes by prefix, as in VS Code: no prefix (files), `>` (commands), `:` (go to line, revealing the line while typing), `?` (help), `@` (symbols, area 3), `#` (workspace symbols, area 3), `view ` (views). It reports the active item while the user moves through the list (used for theme preview), and restores focus to the previous widget when it closes. File search lists the folder's files in the background, respecting `files.exclude`, `search.exclude` and, with `search.useIgnoreFiles` (default true), `.gitignore` and `.ignore` files; it scores with VS Code's fuzzy scorer and shows recently opened files first. The command palette lists commands with their keybindings and recently used commands first. The recently opened files of the window are kept with its UI state (§10.10); the commands history is kept in memory until it is saved too.

**The fuzzy scorer as built (step 6.16, `core/fuzzy`).** A port of VS Code's `fuzzyScorer.ts` and of the parts of `filters.ts` it uses (`fuzzyScore`, `createMatches`, `matchesPrefix`, `isUpper`, the separator tests), with the same numbers, so that results are ordered as VS Code orders them; the tests of `fuzzyScorer.test.ts` (68, all ported) and of the `fuzzyScore` tests of `filters.test.ts` (26) are the oracle. **Positions and matches are offsets in runes**, not UTF-16 units and not bytes (VS Code counts UTF-16 units): a `Match{Start, End}` indexes the runes of the label, so a widget that draws highlights can use it directly; for text in the Basic Multilingual Plane the numbers are VS Code's, for an emoji or other astral character they are one less than VS Code's per such character before the match. Strings are lower-cased rune by rune (`unicode.ToLower`), so a string and its lower case form have the same length. API: `PrepareQuery(string) *PreparedQuery` (`Original`, `OriginalLowercase`, `PathNormalized` with `\` turned into `/`, `Normalized` without white space, `*`, `"`, `…` and a trailing `#`, `NormalizedLowercase`, `ExpectContiguousMatch` for a quoted query, `Values` the pieces of a query with spaces, `ContainsPathSeparator`; `PieceToQuery`, `PiecesToQuery`); `Scorer` (scratch memory for the tables and runes, `NewScorer()` or the zero value, **not safe for concurrent use**: one per goroutine) with `ScoreFuzzy(target, query, queryLower, allowNonContiguousMatches) Score{Score, Positions}` (scoreFuzzy), `ScoreFuzzy2(target, query, patternStart, wordStart) Score2{Matched, Score, Matches}` (scoreFuzzy2, for symbols) and `FuzzyScore(pattern, patternLow, patternStart, word, wordLow, wordStart, FuzzyScoreOptions) (WordScore, bool)` (filters' `fuzzyScore`; only the first 128 runes count), and the same as package functions that allocate; generic over the item type, `ScoreItemFuzzy[T](s *Scorer, item, query, allowNonContiguousMatches, ItemAccessor[T], *Cache) ItemScore{Score, LabelMatch, DescriptionMatch}` and `CompareItemsByFuzzyScore[T](...) int`, with `ItemAccessor[T]{ItemLabel, ItemDescription, ItemPath}` (empty string is a missing one; a nil `*Scorer` or `*Cache` is allowed); `Cache` (`NewCache`, `NewCacheSize(n)`, `Len`) is a map of 64-bit hashes of label, description, match mode and query (VS Code hashes the same inputs to 32 bits), not safe for concurrent use. The constants `PathIdentityScore`, `LabelPrefixScoreThreshold` and `LabelScoreThreshold` are VS Code's. Differences from the reference, none of which changes a score for the inputs of the tests: positions in runes (above); the path separator is `/` only (Linux; the Windows branches are not ported, a `\` in a query is a `/`); `compareAnything` collates with `golang.org/x/text/collate` (CLDR root, numeric for file names) in place of `Intl.Collator`; a query that is not a subsequence of the target is rejected before the matrix is filled (the same result as filling it, much faster); a matrix over 2,097,152 cells (query length times target length) scores nothing, where VS Code allocates without bound; the cache keys are hashes of different numbers; `graceful` and `anyScore` variants of `filters.ts` are not ported (the scorer does not use them). **Performance** (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`): scoring and sorting a keystroke over 100,000 generated paths takes 9 to 19 ms on the development machine, scoring alone 9 to 17 ms (target of §14: 30 ms); the quick open (step 6.18) scores without a cache, on several goroutines, and gives the sort a cache for the paths that make the list only.

**The quick input as built (step 6.17, `gui/quickinput`, plus the filters in `core/fuzzy`).** The model has no Glutter type in it; `Host` is the one widget.
- **Model.** `Item` (label, description, detail, icon, separator, keybinding, buttons, `AlwaysShow`, highlights, `Accept`, `Trigger`) and `Row` (an item with its match highlights and its row height: 22 px, 44 px with a detail line). A separator is not a row of its own: it is the right-aligned label and the top line of the next row, as in VS Code. `Picker` holds the value (with the length of the prefix), the placeholder, the items and the filtered rows, the active row (`Move` wraps, `Page`, `First`, `Last`), the selection of the input, and reports changes (`OnChange`, `OnDidChangeValue`, `OnDidChangeActive`, used for the live preview of go to line). `Registry` holds the providers by prefix: the longest prefix that the value starts with wins, and the provider with the empty prefix is the default (the file mode of step 6.18; a window without one shows the placeholder "Type '?' to get help on the actions you can take from here."). A `Provider` gives a `Handler` per session (`ValueChanged`, `Accept`, `Done`) through a `Session` (`Picker`, `Context`, `Prefix`, `Hide`, `Show`, `Post`, `OnEnd`); `ListProvider` is the base of the providers that list items, with an optional asynchronous `PicksAsync(ctx, ...)` whose results come back with `Post`, and a `DefaultFilter` that tells whether the text of the previous provider is kept or the last word is. `Controller` shows one picker at a time (`Show`, `Pick` for a list that the caller owns, `Accept`, `Trigger` for the buttons, `Hide`) and is used on the UI thread only. Duplicate prefixes and providers without a type are errors (`QuickAccessPrefixDuplicate`, `QuickAccessProviderInvalid`).
- **Modes.** `>` commands, `:` go to line, `::` go to offset, `?` help, `view ` views. *Commands*: every command with a title whose precondition holds in the context of the widget that had the focus, as "Category: Title" with its key; the editor commands only while an editor is open; a command with a row in the `CommandPalette` menu whose `when` does not hold is left out (`workbench.action.quickOpenView`); recently used commands come first, under the separators "recently used" and "other commands", with a button that takes a command out of the history; two commands with the same label show the command id as the description; the category Developer is last; no match shows "No matching commands". The filter is VS Code's (`or(matchesBaseContiguousSubString, matchesWords)`), ported in `core/fuzzy/filters.go` with `MatchesFuzzy`, `MatchesCamelCase`, `FuzzyContains`, `CompareAnything` and `Collate`; the pickers that list plain labels (Insert Snippet) use `MatchesFuzzy` and `CompareAnything`. The history (`CommandsHistory`, a least-recently-used list) is kept in memory per window until state persistence (§10.10), limited by `workbench.commandPalette.history` (default 50, 0 turns it off), and `workbench.commandPalette.preserveInput` keeps the text for the next time. *Go to line*: `ParseGoToLine` takes `line`, `line:column`, `:column`, a negative line (from the end) and a negative column (from the end of the line); the hint line says what Enter does ("Press 'Enter' to go to line 3 at column 2.", "Type a line number to go to (from 1 to N)." for a line out of range, "Type a character position to go to (from 1 to N)." for `::`); while typing, the editor scrolls the line into the middle and shades the line (`editor.rangeHighlightBackground`), Escape puts the selection and the scroll position back, Enter goes there and gives the editor the focus. A column counts visible columns (a tab is `editor.tabSize`) and the editor's cursor is at the byte that has it. `::` goes to a byte offset, **in bytes** where VS Code counts UTF-16 units (the button that switches to zero-based offsets is not built). *Help* lists the providers by prefix with their keys. *Views* lists the views and containers of the side bar and the panel, and the channels of Output, grouped by location.
- **Widget (`Host`).** Wraps the body of the workbench above the notifications and below the dialogs, under the menu bar. It is `min(0.62 x window width, 600)` px wide and the list is at most 440 px tall; radius 6, a 1 px `widget.border`, the shadow `widget.shadow`. It draws its own list: scrolling by wheel and by the keys (Up, Down, PageUp, PageDown, Ctrl+Home, Ctrl+End), a scroll bar thumb, Enter to accept, Escape to close, hover and active rows, label with the match highlights, description, detail (12 px), the keys as key caps (`keybindingLabel.*`) and buttons on the active and hovered row. It remembers the widget that had the focus and gives it back on close. It closes when the focus moves away or the pointer presses outside, unless `workbench.quickOpen.closeOnFocusLost` is false. Colors: `quickInput.background`, `quickInput.foreground`, `quickInputTitle.background`, `quickInputList.focus*`, `pickerGroup.foreground` and `pickerGroup.border`, `keybindingLabel.*`, `list.activeSelectionIconForeground`.
- **Commands and keys.** `workbench.action.showCommands` (Ctrl+Shift+P, F1), `workbench.action.quickOpen` (Ctrl+P, Ctrl+E; the argument is the text to start with, a string or `{"value": "..."}`, and a given text is kept as it is), `workbench.action.gotoLine` (Ctrl+G), `workbench.action.gotoOffset`, `workbench.action.openView` and `workbench.action.quickOpenView` (both show `view `), `workbench.action.clearCommandHistory`, and `workbench.action.closeQuickOpen` (Escape and Shift+Escape under `inQuickOpen`; no title). Menu rows: Command Palette... and Open View... in the View menu, Go to Line/Column... in the Go menu, Command Palette... in the Manage menu. Go to File... (Ctrl+P) is in the Go menu (step 6.18). There is no Help menu row and no row in the context menu of the editor.
- **Migrated pickers.** Insert Snippet (`editor.action.insertSnippet`, step 5.9) opens a quick pick ("Select a snippet", the prefix or name as the label, the description or body as the detail, one separator per source), through `ViewModel.SetQuickPick`; without a quick input it does nothing. The choices of a snippet and the list at the caret for tab completion stay on `kit.ListOverlay`, which the dropdowns and the breadcrumbs picker still use too; the picker of the breadcrumbs (5.7) is not moved.
- **Not built.** The modes `@`, `#` and `%`, buttons on separators, the tf-idf ordering of VS Code's recent commands, the title bar, progress bar, message line, check boxes and the multi-select mode of VS Code's quick pick, the zero-based offset button of `::`, and the Korean and icon-aware variants of the filters.

**Hooks of a pick (step 8.4, `Controller.Pick`).** `PickOptions.OnActive(item, ok)` is called when the active item changes while the pick is shown (VS Code's `onDidChangeActive`): by the arrow keys, Page Up and Down, Home and End, and by a new filter; `ok` is false when no item is active. The item that `ActiveID` makes active when the pick opens is not reported, and the pointer does not change the active item. `PickOptions.OnHide(accepted)` is called once when the pick ends, whatever the way: an item was chosen (`accepted` is true, before the item's `Accept` runs, which waits for the focus to be back), the pick was closed (Escape, a click outside, the loss of the focus of the window, `Controller.Hide`; `OnCancel` follows) or another quick input took its place (`Show`, `Pick` or `Prompt` while the pick is shown: `OnCancel` is not called then, as before). The two theme pickers (§8.4) preview with `OnActive` and go back with `OnHide(false)`.

**The input box (step 7.1, `Controller.Prompt`).** `PromptOptions` (placeholder, starting value with the caret at its end, `Validate`, `OnAccept`, `OnCancel`) shows a line of text in place of the picker (VS Code's `showInputBox`); `quickInputType` is `inputBox` while it is shown. `Validate` runs on every change and when Enter is pressed: a message that is not empty shows under the input as an error (`inputValidation.error*`, through `Picker.SetMessage` and the `kit.InputBox` message) and the text is not accepted. Enter accepts a valid text (the hook runs after the box hides and the focus is back) and Escape cancels. It is the fallback of Save As (§6.3).

**File mode as built (step 6.18, `core/workspace`, `gui/quickinput`, `gui/app`).** The default mode (the provider with the empty prefix, context key `inFilesPicker`, placeholder "Search files by name (append : to go to line)": VS Code's text goes on with "or @ to go to symbol), or paste a link", which this mode does not have). Ported from `anythingQuickAccess.ts` (the file part), `search/common/search.ts` (`extractRangeFromFilter`) and, for the structure of the file search, `services/search`.
- **The list of files (`workspace.FileIndex`).** One per window with a folder, made when the folder opens (and again by Open/Close Folder) and walked by the shell in the background: breadth-first, each folder sorted by name, one `string` (the path relative to the folder, with `/`) for each file and nothing else. A folder that `files.exclude`, `search.exclude` (the two merged, `search.exclude` wins for the same pattern; defaults from VS Code: `**/node_modules`, `**/bower_components`, `**/*.code-search`) or the ignore files exclude is not entered; `.git` is never entered or listed; a folder that cannot be read is counted (`IndexSnapshot.Unreadable`) and skipped. With `search.useIgnoreFiles` (default true) the `.gitignore` and `.ignore` of every folder that is walked count, relative to their folder (`core/glob` `Ignore`: nested files, negation, anchored and folder-only patterns, `**`, character classes, a path below an excluded folder stays excluded; `.ignore` overrides `.gitignore` in the same folder), and so does `.git/info/exclude`; `search.useParentIgnoreFiles` (default false) adds the ignore files of the parent folders of the opened folder, and `search.useGlobalIgnoreFiles` (default false) the global ignore file of git (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/git/ignore`, else `~/.config/git/ignore`), both with lower priority than the files in the folder. Links to files and folders are listed and entered with `search.followSymlinks` (default true) and skipped without it; a link that points to the folder itself, to a parent of it or to a folder above the opened one is a loop and is skipped, a broken link is skipped, and a link to a folder elsewhere is listed under the name of the link (as `rg --follow` does; the same folder reached by two links is listed twice). `Snapshot()` gives the files so far and whether the walk finished (the slice is shared and never written below its length, so readers need no lock); `OnChange` tells the listeners `IndexGrew` (at most ten times a second), `IndexDone` and `IndexChanged`. `Apply` takes the events of the watcher (they already reach the window's `onFileEvents`): a created file is added after a stat (a created folder is walked in the background), a deleted one or a folder is removed, a rename is a delete and a create, a path that the excludes or the ignore files leave out is not added (also when a folder above it is), and a change, a creation or a deletion of an ignore file, a rescan of the whole folder, or the deletion of a folder that had an ignore file makes the list again; the list may miss a file that was created and deleted within a moment. `SetConfig` (called when `files.exclude` or a `search.*` setting changes) and `Rebuild` stop the walk and start another; `Close` stops it and waits, and the walk also stops with the context of the shell, so that a window that goes leaves nothing running. The walk lists 800,000 files a second on the development machine (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`).
- **Search (`workspace.SearchPaths`, `FileIndex.Search`, `QuickOpen`).** The query is VS Code's (`fuzzy.PrepareQuery`: several words that must all match, a path separator, quotes for an exact match; an empty query lists no file). Every path is scored with `ScoreItemFuzzy` (label = the file name, description = its folder relative to the opened folder, path = the relative path, non-contiguous matches allowed) on up to eight goroutines, each with its own `Scorer` and without a cache, over chunks of the list taken from the snapshot at the start, and the context is looked at every 256 paths (`FileSearchCancelled`). A score decides the order whenever two scores differ, so only the paths whose score is not under the score of the last place can be in the top 512 (`DefaultMaxResults`, VS Code's `MAX_RESULTS`); those are sorted with `CompareItemsByFuzzyScore` (a cache for them only; ties by their place in the list) and their matches are the highlights. The result is the same for any number of goroutines. `QuickOpen` adds the recently opened files as VS Code does: for an empty query, all of them in the order of recency, under the heading "recently opened"; for a query, the ones that match (with `search.quickOpen.includeHistory`, default true), scored on the name only unless the query has a path separator and without non-contiguous matches, sorted by score (`search.quickOpen.history.filterSortOrder` `"recency"` keeps the order of recency); the file results, under "file results", leave out the files of the first group. Without a folder only the recently opened files are searched (VS Code adds the open editors, which are in the history too). A file outside the folder shows its folder with `~` for the home of the user. Not built: the search for an absolute path or a path relative to the folder that an exclude hides (VS Code stats it and lists it), the first-word-only search with the full query after a hit limit, and `search.quickOpen.includeSymbols`.
- **Recently opened (`workspace.RecentFiles`).** The files opened in the window, most recent first, at most 200 (VS Code's `MAX_HISTORY_ITEMS`), part of the UI state of the window (step 7.5, §10.10: it is saved with it and comes back with it). A file is added when an editor shows it (opened by any command, or made active) and follows the renames and deletions of the Explorer; the button "Remove from Recently Opened" of a row takes it out.
- **The mode (`quickinput.NewFilesProvider`).** The provider does not filter in the picker; every change of the text makes a search on a goroutine of its own, cancelled by the next keystroke (so the newest one wins) and by closing the picker, and the rows are replaced on the UI thread when it is done: the rows of the last search stay until then. While the list is being walked, and when the watcher reports a change, the search runs again (at most once for each batch of changes the UI thread has not taken yet). A text after the name that says a position is not part of the search: `main.go:12:5`, `main.go:12`, `main.go#12`, `main.go(12,5)`, `main.go:20:3-40:5` and `main.go:` (the start) are read as VS Code's `extractRangeFromFilter` does (a text with `@` before the position is not read as one); a change of the position only keeps the rows, and the position is revealed (the cursor goes there and the editor scrolls) in the file that opens. Rows: the Seti icon of the file (`workbench.iconTheme`, the language of the file as in the Explorer; `kit.NewFileIcon`, shared with the Explorer), the name with its matches, the folder dimmed with its matches, the heading "recently opened" or "file results" at the right of the first row of its group, a button "Open to the Side" (split icon, "Open to the Bottom" with `workbench.editor.openSideBySideDirection` `down`) and, for the recently opened files, "Remove from Recently Opened". With no match the row "No matching results" (with "Still looking for files…" while the walk is not done); with an empty text and nothing opened, no row. `?` lists the mode as "Go to File" with its key.
- **Opening.** Enter opens the file in the active group as a kept editor, or as a preview editor when `workbench.editor.enablePreviewFromQuickOpen` is true (**default false, as in VS Code**, so the default is a kept editor; the setting is ignored while `workbench.editor.enablePreview` is off). Ctrl+Enter keeps the editor, and also opens it to the side while previews from the quick open are off (VS Code's `openEditorPinned && ctrlCmd` rule: Ctrl opens to the side by default and keeps the preview with the setting on); Alt+Enter and the button open to the side, in the group on the side of the active group (made when there is none; `openSideBySideDirection`). A file that is open in the group is made the active editor; the editor has the focus after the picker closed (focus goes back first, then the file is read in the background and shown). `workbench.action.quickOpen` takes the text to start with as its argument (`"main"` or `{"value": "main"}`) and keeps it. The right arrow of VS Code, which opens the file in the background (`canAcceptInBackground`), is not built.
- **Settings, commands, keys.** New settings: `search.exclude`, `search.useIgnoreFiles`, `search.useGlobalIgnoreFiles`, `search.useParentIgnoreFiles`, `search.followSymlinks`, `search.quickOpen.includeHistory`, `search.quickOpen.history.filterSortOrder` (`default` or `recency`) and `workbench.editor.enablePreviewFromQuickOpen`. Menu: Go to File... (`workbench.action.quickOpen`, group `3_global_nav`) in the Go menu. The command and Ctrl+P (and Ctrl+E) were registered in step 6.17; the file mode sets the context key `inFilesPicker` and has no key of its own (VS Code reads the modifier keys at accept time).
- **Not built.** The modes `@`, `#` and `%` and symbols in the file results, `workbench.editor.historyBasedLanguageDetection`, opening links (`http://` and other URIs typed in the picker), the editors that are open but not in the history (VS Code lists the open editors in the history), absolute paths typed in the picker, and the open-in-background accept.

**The editor modes and the switcher as built (step 6.19, `gui/quickinput/editors.go`, `gui/app/switcher.go`).** Ported from `editorQuickAccess.ts`, `editorquickaccess.css`, the quick navigate of `quickInput.ts` (`registerQuickNavigation`, `quickNavigate`, `itemActivation`) and the keys of `editor.contribution.ts`.
- **Modes** (`NewEditorsProvider`; context key `inEditorsPicker`, placeholder "Type the name of an editor to open it."): `edt ` lists the editors of all groups in the order of the groups and their tabs (Show All Editors By Appearance, `workbench.action.showAllEditors`, Ctrl+K Ctrl+P), `edt mru ` all of them most recently used first (`showAllEditorsByMostRecentlyUsed`), `edt active ` those of the active group most recently used first (`showEditorsInActiveGroup`). An item is the name of the editor with its folder as the description, its Seti icon, and a close button at the right: a cross on the active and the hovered row, and for an editor with unsaved changes a dot that is always shown and turns into the cross under the pointer. A preview editor is in italics. With more than one group the items are under the separators "Group 1", "Group 2" (the order of the grid). The filter scores the name, the folder and the path as the file mode does (`fuzzy.ScoreItemFuzzy`); with a filter the groups stay in the order of the grid and the editors of a group are sorted by score; no match shows "No matching editors". Choosing an item shows the editor in its group; the cross closes the editor (the question about unsaved changes comes with step 7.2) and takes the item out, and the picker stays open; an editor that stays (the user cancelled) stays in the list.
- **The switcher.** `workbench.action.quickOpenPreviousRecentlyUsedEditorInGroup` (Ctrl+Tab, when `!activeEditorGroupEmpty`) opens `edt active ` as a switcher (`ShowOptions.QuickNavigate`): the input is hidden (it keeps the keyboard), the second item is active (the editor used before the active one; the first when there is one editor), and keys that are held drive it. `quickOpenLeastRecentlyUsedEditorInGroup` (Ctrl+Shift+Tab) starts at the last item. `quickOpenPreviousRecentlyUsedEditor` and `quickOpenLeastRecentlyUsedEditor` open `edt mru ` (VS Code gives both the second item), and `openPreviousEditorFromHistory` the file mode (the first item when the group is empty, else the second).
