As the settings (§11.1 "Settings in an open editor"): when a document of the file is open, the edit goes into it and it is saved when it was not dirty; a dirty document stays dirty and the disk is left alone (VS Code refuses to edit a dirty `keybindings.json`; Emetos edits the document); with no document the file is written (keeping its encoding, line ends and BOM) and made when it does not exist. The keys of a dirty document count when it is saved.
- **The JSON commands.** `openGlobalKeybindingsFile` makes `keybindings.json` when it is not there and opens it as a text editor. `openDefaultKeybindingsFile` opens the read-only document `emetos-virtual:/defaultKeybindings.json` (language `jsonc`, tab "Default Keybindings") with `keybinding.DefaultKeybindingsJSON`: the header comment and an array with every default keybinding in VS Code's layout (`{ "key": "ctrl+,",   "command": "workbench.action.openSettings" }`, the `when` and `args` on the next lines), without VS Code's comment with the title of each entry.
- **Not built**: the sort by precedence, the actions "Show Default/User/System Keybindings", the history of the search, resizable columns, auto-completion in the when box, editing of `args`, the JSON editor widget that VS Code's `editor.action.defineKeybinding` shows in a text editor, the commands that accept or reject the when box (Enter and Escape in the box do that), the actions in the title of the editor and the filters of extensions.

## 12. Privacy

- Emetos never opens a network connection of its own: no telemetry, crash reports, update checks, extension galleries or remote fonts. `internal/archtest` fails the build when a package imports `net/http` (§5.3). The one socket Emetos opens is the Unix domain socket of the single instance (§10.11): `core/ipc` is the only package that imports `net`, it listens and dials `unix` addresses only (never TCP or UDP), the socket is private to the user (mode 0600 in a private folder, peer user checked), and archtest fails the build when any other package imports `net`.
- Links in notifications or help open in the user's browser through the desktop portal; that is the only path to the network, and only on a click.
- Logs stay on disk (§13).

## 13. Errors and logging

- Every error uses `github.com/rm4n0s/errors` (CLAUDE.md). Errors shown to the user become notifications with a short message; the full error goes to the log as a `slog` attribute (`"err", err`).
- Logs: `log/slog` to `$XDG_STATE_HOME/emetos/logs/<date>/main.log` (kept 7 days), and to the "Emetos" channel of the Output panel (§10.13: `logs.Store` has `Subscribe`, `Watch` and `Clear`). `emetos --log debug` raises the level.
- A panic in a background goroutine is recovered, logged, and reported once as an error notification; the window stays open. A panic in the UI goroutine is handled by Glutter's error reporting, logged, and Emetos tries to write backups (§10.10) before exiting.

## 14. Performance targets

Measured on release builds (`-tags glutter_release,emetos_dev`, so dev commands can drive the app) on the user's machine, in their desktop session with its GPU; headless numbers (software rendering) are recorded too but don't decide. The numbers and the method go in `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`.

| Metric | Target | First measured |
|---|---|---|
| Typing a character in a 10,000-line Go file: UI-thread time of the frame that shows it | < 4 ms | step 3.11 (no highlighting), again with highlighting in step 4.9 |
| Scrolling a 100,000-line file: frames | display refresh rate, no UI-thread frame over 8 ms | 3.11 |
| Opening a 10 MB file: first frame with text | < 300 ms | 3.11 |
| Opening a 200 MB file in large file mode: first frame with text | < 3 s | 5.12 |
| Cold start with no folder, to the first frame | < 400 ms | 3.11, again with the workbench in 10.1 |
| Idle, window focused, cursor blinking | < 1% of a core; no frames at all when the window has no focus | 3.11 |
| Resident memory, one 10,000-line file open | no more than an empty Glutter window (its `counter` example) plus 50 MB; measured 271 MB against the baseline's 287 MB headless, where Mesa's software GL dominates (revised in step 3.11 from "< 200 MB", which the graphics stack alone exceeds; the number to hold is the one with the GPU, `scripts/perf.sh --session`) | 3.11 |
| Fuzzy file search over 100,000 paths, per keystroke | < 30 ms, off the UI thread | 6.16 (the scorer alone: 9 to 19 ms), 6.18 (the search end to end: 2 to 8 ms on all cores, 9 to 27 ms on one; the listing of the folder: 800,000 files a second) |

## 15. Testing

### 15.1 Tests

- Core packages: table-driven unit tests, fuzz tests for the buffer (random edits checked against a plain string), the JSONC parser, the snippet parser, the when-clause parser, and the tokenizer (no panics, bounded time).
- **Ported VS Code tests** use 1-based lines and UTF-16 columns; `core/vstest` converts them to Emetos positions. A case with non-ASCII text has its expected positions worked out again for byte columns, with a comment saying so. A case that needs VS Code's TypeScript harness (a full editor, services) is skipped, and the count of skipped cases is recorded.
- Tokenization: VS Code's own oracle, `extensions/vscode-colorize-tests/test/colorize-fixtures` and `colorize-results` (scopes per token), for every bundled language that has fixtures there; small fixtures of Emetos's own for the others.
- UI: `gluttertest` widget tests with the fake platform (keys through `SendKeyEvent`, IME through `SendTextInputEvent`, clipboard, file choosers, window close), and golden images with `MatchesGolden`.
- Golden images need deterministic fonts: UI tests load Go Mono (regular, bold, italic, bold italic), Codicons and Seti through `gluttertest.Opts.Fonts`, and set `editor.fontFamily` to Go Mono (D14). Glyphs Go Mono lacks (CJK, emoji) show as boxes, so their layout is checked through the paragraph queries, not by goldens.
- **Isolation**: no test, proof or check reads or writes the user's real configuration, state, cache, trash or socket. Go tests call a helper that points every `XDG_*` variable at `t.TempDir()` (`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, where the socket and its lock live, at a short folder of its own, since a socket path is limited to 107 bytes); the two-process tests of `core/ipc` start copies of the test binary (`EMETOS_IPC_HELPER` makes `TestMain` run only the ipc code, with no window) in their own process groups under a deadline, and kill the groups when the test ends; scripts run the app through `scripts/isolated.sh`, which does the same with a temporary folder (and can copy a fixture configuration in).
- Display tests (`emetos_display` tag) start the real app under a headless Wayland compositor.
- Performance benchmarks (§14) are Go benchmarks plus `scripts/perf.sh`, which runs the app with `GLUTTER_FRAME_STATS=1`.

### 15.2 Dev commands

`EMETOS_DEV_COMMANDS` holds a JSON array of steps run after the first frame, each `["<command id>", <args>]` or `["sleep", "<duration>"]`, for example `[["workbench.action.quickOpen", ">line"], ["sleep", "200ms"]]`. Any registered command can be used; commands whose id starts with `dev.` exist only for this (opening a file at a position in a group, posting a notification, typing text) and are registered only in debug and `emetos_dev` builds (D15). `dev.openFile {"path", "line", "column", "group", "preview"}` shows a file in an editor group of the window (a relative path is relative to the folder Emetos runs in; line and column count from 1 and are optional; `group` is the place of an existing group, counted from 1 in the order the groups appear in the grid, and the file opens in the active group when it is not given; the group becomes the active group; a `group` that is not a whole number of 1 or more fails with the tag `DevOpenFileInvalid`, and one that does not exist with `DevOpenFileGroupInvalid`; `"preview": true` opens it as the preview editor of the group (an italic tab that the next preview replaces; only with `workbench.editor.enablePreview` and for a document without changes), as a navigation does, where the default is a kept editor, and a `preview` that is not true or false fails with `DevOpenFilePreviewInvalid`); `dev.openDiff {"original", "modified"}` opens a diff editor over two documents in the active group (step 9.5; each side is an absolute path, a path relative to the folder of the window, a `file://` URI, an `emetos-virtual:` URI whose provider generates the text, or the `untitled:` URI of an open untitled document; no folder and a relative path fails with `DevOpenDiffInvalid`, as do missing or empty arguments, an untitled document that is not open fails with `DevOpenDiffSideMissing`, and a side that cannot be opened fails with that side's tag, leaving the other side unused); `dev.switchProfile {"name"}` gives the window another profile in place (step 8.6, `Window.SwitchProfile`; the argument may also be the name as a string; "Default" or any capitalization of the name of a profile that exists, which it does not make; no name fails with the tag `DevSwitchProfileInvalid`, a profile that does not exist with `ProfileSwitchNotFound`); `dev.openFolderInNewWindow {"path"}` opens a folder in a new window (step 7.8; the argument may also be the path as a string; a relative path is relative to the working directory of the process; a folder that is open comes to the front; the new window is made whatever `window.openFoldersInNewWindow` says; an argument that is not a path fails with the tag `DevOpenFolderInvalid`, a folder that is not there with the tags of `workspace.Open`, and a process whose windows cannot be made with `WindowsUnavailable`); `dev.openMenu {"id", "x", "y"}` opens a menu of the window (step 6.7): the id of a menu of the menu bar (`MenubarEditMenu`: opened as a click on its title does), `GlobalActivity` (the Manage menu) or `EditorContext` (the context menu of the editor, at `x` and `y` in window coordinates, 400 and 250 by default; the argument may also be the id as a string); an id of any other menu, and a menu of the bar that has no item, fail; `dev.notify {"severity", "message", "source", "actions", "progress"}` sends a notification through `core/notify` (step 6.11; the argument may also be the message as a string): `severity` is `"info"` (the default), `"warning"` or `"error"`, `message` is a text that is not empty, `source` a text, `actions` a list of button labels (a button closes the toast and writes `dev.notify action` with the label to the log) and `progress` a number from 0 to 100 (the bar of a running progress) or `"infinite"`; a bad argument fails with its own tag, `DevNotifyArgumentsInvalid`, `DevNotifySeverityInvalid`, `DevNotifyMessageInvalid`, `DevNotifySourceInvalid`, `DevNotifyActionsInvalid` or `DevNotifyProgressInvalid`; `dev.setMarkers {"owner", "resource", "markers": [{"severity", "message", "startLine", "startColumn", "endLine", "endColumn", "source", "code"}]}` sets the markers an owner (`"dev"` by default) has for a file, so that the Problems panel has something to show before area 3 (step 6.12; the window implements it; `resource` is a path, relative to the folder Emetos runs in or absolute; a missing or empty `markers` removes the markers of the owner for that file; `severity` is `"error"` (the default), `"warning"`, `"info"` or `"hint"`, `message` a text that is not empty, the four positions whole numbers from 1 (1, 1, the start line and the start column when missing), `source` and `code` texts); a bad argument fails with its own tag, `DevSetMarkersArgumentsInvalid`, `DevSetMarkersOwnerInvalid`, `DevSetMarkersResourceInvalid`, `DevSetMarkersCwdFailed`, `DevSetMarkersMarkersInvalid`, `DevSetMarkersMarkerInvalid`, `DevSetMarkersSeverityInvalid`, `DevSetMarkersMessageInvalid`, `DevSetMarkersPositionInvalid` or `DevSetMarkersTextInvalid`, and without a window it fails with `DevNoWindow`; `dev.log {"channel", "message"}` appends a line to a log channel of the Output panel (`"Emetos"` by default; the argument may also be the message as a string), and fails with `DevLogArgumentsInvalid`, `DevLogChannelInvalid` or `DevLogMessageInvalid`; `dev.type` (a text, one character in each frame) and `dev.scroll` act on the editor that has the focus. The runner waits two frames after each command, so that what the command made (an editor, the focus) is there for the next. The keyboard focus of a new active group follows a few frames later (§10.3), so a script that types after a command that changes the group puts a `sleep` between them.

## 16. Build and runtime requirements

- Build: Go 1.27 only. `CGO_ENABLED=0 go build ./cmd/emetos` makes the binary; users build with `-tags glutter_release` (D16).
- Run: what Glutter needs (Wayland or X11, Vulkan or OpenGL ES, fontconfig), a monospace font, and optionally xdg-desktop-portal for file dialogs and opening links.

## 17. Licensing and third-party assets

Emetos is MIT-licensed (`LICENSE`). The reference sources are pinned in `scripts/reference-versions.txt` (committed). Bundled assets are listed in `NOTICE`, each with its license in `third_party/<name>/`:

| Asset | Source | License |
|---|---|---|
| TextMate grammars and language configurations | VS Code `extensions/*`; each grammar's upstream is listed in VS Code's `cgmanifest.json` files | MIT, and the grammar's own license (checked per grammar when it is added; a grammar without a permissive license is left out and recorded) |
| Color themes | VS Code `extensions/theme-defaults` | MIT |
| Ported code, default keybindings and setting defaults | VS Code `src/vs` | MIT |
| TextMate tokenizer algorithms | `microsoft/vscode-textmate` | MIT |
| Go module grammar | `golang/vscode-go` | MIT |
| Emmet parser algorithms and snippets | `emmetio/emmet` | MIT |
| Seti file icons (font and mapping) | VS Code `extensions/theme-seti` | MIT |
| Codicons (font and `codicon.csv`) | npm package `@vscode/codicons` | CC BY 4.0 (icons), MIT (code) |
| Go Mono (tests only, not bundled) | `golang.org/x/image/font/gofont` | BSD-3-Clause |
| `scripts/headless-wayland.sh`, `scripts/screenshot-headless.sh` | Glutter | BSD-3-Clause |

## 18. Milestones

| Milestone | Content |
|---|---|
| M0 | Skeleton: module, packages, lint, boundary tests, references, paths and logs, an empty window, proof scripts |
| M1 | Text core: positions, buffer, files, documents, undo, decorations, editing commands |
| M2 | Configuration: JSONC, settings, context keys, commands, keybindings, globs |
| M3 | Editor widget: rendering, cursors, keys, IME, mouse, scrolling, clipboard, options, first performance check |
| M4 | Languages: regex, TextMate, languages, themes, tokenization, brackets, indentation, guides, auto-closing, comments, folding |
| M5 | Editor features: find, word wrap, whitespace, minimap, sticky scroll, breadcrumbs, snippets, Emmet, large files |
| M6 | Workbench: widget kit, layout, menus, editor groups and tabs, notifications, panels, explorer, quick input, navigation, status bar |
| M7 | Files and sessions: saving, closing, auto save, external changes, UI state, hot exit, command line, windows |
| M8 | UIs for settings and keybindings, themes, zoom and zen mode, profiles |
| M9 | Diff and merge; the audit of the error routes (9.9) |
| M9b | One window: workspace tabs, Emetos's own title bar, launches that open tabs, the system tray (§10.14) |
| M10 | Performance pass and release of areas 1–2 |

The steps are in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md`.

## 19. Open decisions

| # | Question | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| Q5 | **Emetos's own extension model** (area 11) | Decide later; the command, context-key, view, menu and settings registries are built so extensions can add to them |

## 20. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Text rendering through Glutter is too slow for an editor | Per-line paragraph cache, repaint boundary for the text, the performance check in step 3.11 before anything else is built on the editor; fixes go into Glutter (D8). Glutter's own numbers (41–58 µs per cold code line, 5–10 µs for an unchanged one) fit the budget of §14 |
| TextMate grammars written for Oniguruma behave differently with regexp2 | Translation layer in `core/regex`, a test that compiles every pattern of every bundled grammar, VS Code's colorize oracles, timeouts |
| Glutter is young; Emetos is its first large app | Workarounds are marked and listed (D8); Glutter fixes are made in the Glutter repository |
| Glutter's close requests for the first window are app-wide | Step 7.8 found that the request of the first (implicit) window reaches `PlatformDispatcher.OnExitRequested` with its view ID, and the manager of the windows takes that hook over (`// Glutter:`, `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`); the first window cannot be destroyed, only hidden |
| GNOME shows no tray icons without the AppIndicator extension | The close button quits when no tray is shown (`TrayUnavailable`), so a window is never lost; the README says how to get the extension |
| The scope of "what VS Code has" grows | Areas 3–11 are outside this spec until planned; every feature in §3 is a row with a defined behavior |
